## 1. OmniAct: One Policy, One Million Embodiments

- **作者**：Xiang Fan, Yuheng Wang, Jiayuan Yan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG

A single policy that controls diverse robots must not only transfer task knowledge across embodiments, but also adapt its behavior to each robot's capabilities. Existing approaches facilitate transfer through shared action representations, but these representations do not necessarily capture how differences in robot morphology should shape manipulation strategies. We introduce OmniAct, a cross-embodiment manipulation policy that conditions directly on each robot's kinematics in a specific scene to produce embodiment-specific behavior. This kinematic graph represents the robot's joint positions, motion axes, and connectivity to its visual observation, allowing the policy to account for how its embodiment related to objects and surfaces in the scene. A flow-matching policy predicts relative joint motions for each node, enabling control across robots with different degrees of freedom and kinematic configurations. To learn how physical structure shapes behavior at scale, we procedurally design and filter one million physically feasible robot embodiments; next, we develop algorithms to generate robot trajectories in simulation for all embodiments; finally, we train across diverse simulated manipulation tasks. On 2,000 embodiment-scene-held-out evaluation episodes, OmniAct achieves 63.0% success, compared to 17.8% for pi 0.5 and 57.9% for the strongest baseline; on the task families GPT-6 Astra was evaluated on, it achieves 64.7% against GPT-6 Astra's 41.1%. On a previously unseen real-world robot, it achieves nearly twice the success rate of the next best method, MolmoAct2. Beyond task success, we observe that OmniAct learns distinct manipulation strategies that exploit the capabilities and accommodate the limitations of different morphologies. Project: https://omniact.github.io/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33158) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33158)

## 2. PointX: Multi-View Consistent Intrinsics by Querying Surface Points

- **作者**：Miguel Farinha, Sam Sartor, Iliyan Georgiev et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://pointx-paper.github.io/

The appearance of a scene is determined by the albedo, roughness, metallicity and normal of every point on its surface, and by the light that reaches it, its irradiance. Recovering these intrinsics from images is ambiguous, and methods that predict them per pixel give the same surface point a different decomposition in every view. We introduce PointX, a simple feed-forward model that predicts intrinsics per 3D surface point instead. A multi-view transformer encodes a set of unposed images, and for any queried surface point, PointX pools the point's tokens across the views in which it appears and decodes the point's intrinsics once. Each point of a 3D reconstruction of the scene, such as a point cloud or a mesh, thus receives a single prediction, which is exactly consistent across views by construction and enables view-consistent relighting and editing. The same model also predicts per-view maps, from one image or many, by querying the point seen at every pixel. These maps agree across views up to the precision of the estimated geometry and correspondences. PointX is significantly more consistent than existing methods, while remaining competitive in accuracy from a single image and improving as views are added. Project page: https://pointx-paper.github.io/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33157) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33157)

## 3. DiaCap: Jointly Learning Who Speaks When for Audiovisual Dialogue Captioning

- **作者**：Binfei Ji, Zhiwen Cao, Anqi Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.MM, cs.SD
- **备注**：18 pages, 13 figures, 12 tables

We present DiaCap, an audiovisual LLM for comprehensive dialogue captioning, which produces the transcript, timestamps, and a visual description of the speaker for every utterance in a video. Considering that utterance timing and audiovisual diarization are tightly coupled, DiaCap unifies the tasks in a single model, trained in two complementary stages. First, we train DiaCap on 225K curated clips, using synthetic captions that densely label every utterance in detail. Second, we further refine the model via reinforcement learning on 2.9K human-corrected clips, using fine-grained feedback that directly evaluates utterance timing, speaker grouping, and speaker binding. On DiaDemBench, which we extend with utterance-level timestamps and subtitle redaction, both DiaCap with off-the-shelf transcripts and its end-to-end variant DiaCap-E2E achieve strong results. Specifically, DiaCap performs on par with Gemini 4 Argon in audiovisual diarization while clearly outperforming it in timestamp accuracy (76.59 vs. 42.76 TIME). It also maintains strong diarization on multilingual and minute-long videos, and transfers to audiovisual question answering.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33153) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33153)

## 4. BioLoom: Scaling Biomedical Vision-Language Models by Learning from the Structure of Scientific Articles

- **作者**：Yuwei Yang, Xiaoxiao Sun, Alejandro Lozano et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Project page: https://bioloom-clip.github.io/

The development of biomedical vision-language models (VLMs) with contrastive image-text pretraining has been driven by scaling to increasingly large and diverse image-text datasets. Beyond scaling, further progress can come from learning richer representations from existing data. We hypothesize that the structure of scientific literature itself provides an additional source of supervision: figures, panels, captions, and accompanying article text encode rich relationships across modalities. Yet, treating these as independent image-text pairs overlooks their relationships. To address this limitation, we incorporate these structural relationships into contrastive image-text supervision. We preserve exact image-text matches while introducing weaker positive supervision for related pairs, such as a panel and the caption of a sibling panel. We upweight cross-article negatives of the same content type to encourage finer discrimination. We also enrich panel descriptions using visual content, original figure captions, and mentioning paragraphs. Pairing panels with these descriptions and whole figures with their original captions yields Loom52M, a dataset of 52.4 million image-text pairs spanning scientific charts and biomedical images. Using Loom52M, BioLoom (DFN-2B) achieves 56.82% domain-balanced mean accuracy across 67 zero-shot classification tasks from 43 datasets in 10 domains, compared with 48.77% for MedPMC-CLIP. On BioLoomBench with concise captions and BioMedFlickr, it achieves bidirectional mean Recall@1 of 63.20% and 10.59%, versus 46.37% and 7.55% for the respective strongest evaluated baselines, BiomedCLIP and MedPMC-CLIP. Code, datasets, benchmarks, and models are available at https://bioloom-clip.github.io/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33151) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33151)

## 5. One Take, Many Cameras: Audio-Grounded Shot Synthesis for Dialogue Re-Cinematography

- **作者**：Anthony Chen, Naomi Ken Korem, Matan Ben-Yosef et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://recinematography.github.io/

Creating a dialogue scene typically requires cutting between multiple views (e.g., close-ups, medium shots, and over-the-shoulder shots) of the actors. Yet capturing such coverage is costly and in many settings unavailable. We study dialogue re-cinematography: synthesizing additional shots from a single wide-angle dialogue video. Existing video generators can synthesize new shots, but rely on visual cues alone, so when the source video barely shows the lips, the generated lip motion fails to match the speech. Our key observation is that the speech audio is view-invariant: it constrains lip motion in any target view. Building on this insight, we adapt a pretrained audio-visual diffusion model to generate the new shot from a target frame that specifies the view, a source video that contains the performance, and the speech audio that drives lip motion. In multi-person scenes, however, pretrained audio-visual models drive every visible face with the same speech, causing speech leakage, where silent listeners mouth the speaker's words. We therefore introduce speaker-aware audio gating, a training-free module that lets the audio drive only the faces that speak in the target shot. On a cinematic paired-view benchmark spanning diverse viewpoints and framings, our method lowers perceptual error by 59%, landmark error by 56%, and speech leakage by 97% relative to the strongest baseline, Wan 3.0. Our method also remains robust on in-the-wild scenes, highlighting its potential for film post-production.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33147) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33147)

## 6. Lost on Campus: Evaluating Embodied Spatial Reasoning of Vision-Language Models in the Wild

- **作者**：Zehan Zheng, Yanyuan Chen, Deming Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://dyfcalid.github.io/Lost-on-Campus/

Embodied Spatial Reasoning (ESR) is central to deploying Vision-Language Models (VLMs) in real-world embodied tasks, yet remains inadequately evaluated. Existing benchmarks primarily assess spatial reasoning in static images or synthetic indoor environments, where semantic shortcuts often suffice, making it difficult to faithfully evaluate spatial reasoning capabilities in the wild. In light of this, we present Lost on Campus, a benchmark for evaluating ESR in large-scale real-world outdoor 3D environments reconstructed by 3D Gaussian Splatting. Our benchmark introduces a unified reasoning-action evaluation framework that seamlessly integrates diagnostic QA for isolated reasoning with closed-loop interactive navigation for active reasoning under multimodal instructions. To enable fine-grained diagnosis, we evaluate six capability aspects of ESR: action grounding, spatial foresight, metric awareness, goal-directed planning, ego-allocentric localization, and spatio-temporal consistency. Extensive experiments reveal that: (i) Compared to indoor settings, real-world outdoor environments impose substantially higher demands on spatial reasoning, where existing models exhibit poor performance; (ii) VLMs still struggle with fine-grained visual alignment, with failures in spatial foresight and ego-allocentric localization emerging as primary bottlenecks; (iii) Improving multimodal interaction capabilities and long-term spatial reasoning is crucial for advancing embodied intelligence. These findings highlight that faithful evaluation of ESR demands benchmarks tightly coupling perception, reasoning and action within realistic and continuous environments.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33145) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33145)

## 7. ROAM-Bench: Evaluating Embodied Active Spatial Intelligence through Evidence Verification

- **作者**：Xingjian Ran, Xiaoye Mo, Sihao Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://rxjfighting.github.io/ROAM-Bench/

Embodied spatial intelligence requires agents to gather information through exploration and interaction, then use it to reason and act. Existing benchmarks insufficiently distinguish correct outcomes from completion of these task requirements. We introduce ROAM-Bench to evaluate these abilities under robot motion and collision constraints. Independent checks use simulation state and episode records to assess outcomes and task requirements, with stronger checks of geometry, coverage, or placement. It comprises 250 manually validated tasks across 11 types in five groups and 27 indoor environments, covering object and room search, counting, geometric judgments, direction reasoning, and relocation. Human evaluators achieve 84% strict task success on 100 sampled tasks, with every correct outcome satisfying the required checks. Across seven frontier vision-language models, we identify two complementary failure modes: correct outcomes with unmet task requirements, and incorrect outcomes despite passing stronger checks. Oracle-input diagnostics show that more complete target observations and calibrated feedback improve final spatial judgments.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33143) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33143)

## 8. iWAM: Efficient Action Prediction from World Models without Explicit Future Prediction

- **作者**：Vansh Kapoor, Max Sobol Mark, Sreyas Venkataraman et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG

World-action models (WAMs) build policies on pretrained video-generation models, on the premise that predicting how a scene evolves helps determine how to act. However, generating future scenes is expensive for real-time control. This motivates efficient proxies for future imagination, but how to train such proxies without sacrificing action-prediction quality remains unclear. An effective proxy need not generate a high-resolution frame; it only needs to provide enough information to distinguish between possible futures. This leads to the design principle behind iWAM: rather than denoising an entire future frame, can we condition the action policy on intermediate features from a single video-model evaluation? We show that the answer is yes. Exploiting the reversible dynamics of flow-matching models, we invert each training future into the corresponding latent noise vector. However, a single velocity evaluation at this latent does not necessarily yield informative features for action prediction. We therefore learn a bidirectional flow map that amortizes future-generation computation, enabling a single model evaluation to provide effective conditioning features. At inference, we sample a latent noise vector from the prior and condition the policy using just one video-backbone forward pass. Across simulated benchmarks and real robots, iWAM recovers the generalization benefits of WAMs while substantially reducing its inference cost.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33141) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33141)

## 9. Physicl Scenes: An Artist-created 3D Dataset for Learning Perception and Action

- **作者**：Yueh-Cheng Liu, Snehal Jauhri, Matthias Nießner et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project: https://liu115.github.io/physicl video: https://youtu.be/UgyQFQSU-oc

We present Physicl Scenes, a high-quality artist-created and curated dataset of 6,499 3D scenes spanning household rooms, public interiors, and outdoor spaces. The dataset combines detailed geometry, physically based materials, and carefully composed environments with object- and part-level semantic annotations, providing rich, joint supervision for visual and embodied learning. We demonstrate its value as training data across inverse rendering, 3D reconstruction, scene understanding, and mobile manipulation. Across these tasks, training state-of-the-art methods with our scenes improves performance over alternative synthetic datasets. In addition, we introduce a mobile manipulation benchmark, evaluating vision-language-action models performing physical pick and place in diverse 3D scenes with realistic layouts, and using our part annotations to also score where each object is grasped. Our dataset will be released publicly to facilitate research in visual and embodied AI.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33137) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33137)

## 10. BriConstrain: Geometrically Constrained Decoding for Autoregressive Brick Assembly Generation

- **作者**：Nadav Magar, Amir Hertz, Ariel Shamir et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：Project Page: https://nadmag.github.io/BriConstrain/

Language models can generate LEGO$^\circledR$ assemblies from text, but their builds often contain parts that collide or attach to nothing. The usual approach of rejecting and resampling invalid placements discards decoded tokens without guaranteeing a valid build. We present BriConstrain, a constrained decoding method that restricts a pretrained language model's next-token probabilities before sampling, steering it towards valid placements. Because the validity of a brick placement depends on the non-predetermined build state, it cannot be compiled into a fixed grammar and must be checked dynamically. However, checking the validity of a brick before sampling is challenging as the space of valid placements is combinatorially large and cannot be enumerated exhaustively. Our key idea is to narrow the search space by constraining how parts attach rather than which parts attach to each other. Concretely, given the model's predictions of the currently placed part, and the previously placed parent it wants to connect to, we constrain the model's choice of how they attach. This allows us to span the space of part attachments, using a precomputed geometric oracle, and to effectively filter out colliding options. With valid attachment candidates in hand, we bias the model's next-token distribution towards the remaining valid candidates. We evaluate our method on two LEGO$^\circledR$ formats and models: BrickNet, which annotates connectivity explicitly, and our proposed Implicit Connectivity LDraw Format (ICLF), which uses numeric values for placements and leaves connectivity implicit. Our method raises collision-free builds from 31% to 91% on BrickNet and from 15% to 65% on ICLF, and outperforms rejection sampling on both formats at about 60% of its decoding cost.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33135) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33135)

## 11. Read My Lips: Context-aware Lip Reading Using Language and Audio-Visual Models

- **作者**：Amir Razlighi, Zhuo Ning, Naomi Ken Korem et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.GR, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://amirhossein-razlighi.github.io/read_my_lips/

Real-world speech is often partially observable: audio may be missing or corrupted by noise and competing speakers, while the speaker's lips remain visible. Recovering speech requires combining what can be heard, seen, and inferred from context. We introduce RML (Read My Lips), a collaborative method combining a lip reader, a language model, and an audio-visual generative model. The lip reader provides articulation hypotheses, the language model uses context to resolve ambiguous or missing words, and the audio-visual generator reconstructs synchronized speech while preserving speaker identity. Since source reliability varies, RML generates multiple reconstructions and uses the available evidence to favor the most reliable ones. This allows RML to handle missing audio, strong interference, and poor visual evidence without manually defined thresholds. Quantitative and qualitative experiments across these conditions show the benefit of combining visual, acoustic, and linguistic signals.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33117) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33117)

## 12. EventDiffusion: Learning Native Event Stream Distribution from Videos

- **作者**：Karthik Sundar Senthilkumar, Christopher Thirgood, Simon Hadfield
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：*Equal Contribution, Project Page - https://eventdiffusion.karthiksundar.in/

Event cameras capture asynchronous brightness changes at high temporal resolution, but scarce recordings constrain data-driven research. Sparse RGB observations cannot uniquely determine intervening events since identical frames may correspond to streams with different activity, polarities, and timing. Existing simulators largely rely on prescribed sensor dynamics and estimated brightness trajectories, whose assumptions limit the diversity and fidelity of plausible event streams. We introduce EventDiffusion, a hierarchical generative framework that directly learns the conditional distribution of native asynchronous event streams from real recordings. EventDiffusion decomposes generation into three probabilistic processes: Event Activity Diffusion (EAD) learns stochastic discrete event counts; Event Spatial Allocation (ESA) distributes these counts to individual pixels while preserving cardinality; and Event Timing Diffusion (ETD) learns continuous asynchronous timestamp distributions. We also introduce CEFS, an event-stream realism metric combining event activity and polarity with multiscale space-time structure and temporal fidelity. Across all datasets, EventDiffusion achieves double the accuracy of SOTA methods and demonstrates robustness across different cameras at any resolution.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33096) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33096)

## 13. The Radar-based Activities of Daily Living (R-ADL) Dataset

- **作者**：Dominique Regli, Brian C. Williams
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.HC, cs.LG

Monitoring Activities of Daily Living (ADLs) supports safety and well-being for people living at home, especially older adults, and radar sensing is gaining increasing attention in the field of activity recognition for its ability to ensure privacy in the home while also surpassing technical limitations of other sensing modalities. However, radar-based activity recognition remains constrained by the limited availability of large-scale datasets. We introduce the Radar-based Activities of Daily Living (R-ADL) dataset to addresses the gaps identified in existing work: 1) it expands the scale of available data by providing over eight hours of annotated recordings in which a person performs eight distinct activities; 2) it overcomes room configuration limitations by recording from five different locations in a two-bedroom, two-bathroom apartment; and 3) it extends beyond primitive actions to focus on high-level ADLs. In addition to the dataset, this paper introduces practical guidance for future ADL dataset construction. We conducted a detailed analysis of how ADLs are prioritized across academic research, health survey tools, and clinical trials to identify clinically significant ADLs. Furthermore, we provide our experimental design, data collection and annotation strategies, post-processing pipeline for forming the R-ADL dataset from raw data, and baseline results on standard machine learning architectures. The R-ADL dataset serves as a valuable resource for advancing ADL recognition systems as we continue building larger and more diverse datasets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33088) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33088)

## 14. SUPRA: Synthesizing Amyloid PET from MRI and Plasma Biomarkers with an Explicit Regional SUVR Prior

- **作者**：Hanwen Li, Fadi Deek, Zhi Wei et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, 4 figures, 9 tables

Amyloid positron emission tomography (PET) quantifies brain amyloid-$β$ as a standardized uptake value ratio (SUVR), but is costly and not widely available. Structural magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) is routine, and plasma biomarkers such as phosphorylated tau 217 (p-tau217) are becoming practical. Synthesizing amyloid PET from MRI alone is ill-posed, because brain anatomy reveals little about amyloid burden. We present SUPRA (SUVR prior-guided amyloid synthesis), which divides this task between MRI and plasma biomarkers. A regression maps the plasma panel to regional SUVR levels, painted onto the subject's MRI segmentation as an explicit prior. A 3D network then adds a residual correction guided by MRI. The output is in absolute SUVR, and the model also runs on p-tau217 alone. On the Alzheimer's Disease Neuroimaging Initiative (ADNI) cohort (111 test pairs), SUPRA reaches a peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) of 23.4 dB, against at most 20.7 dB for four published models, and rank correlations of 0.56 to 0.67 with true SUVR in the composite cortex, an early-accumulation region and the striatum, against at most 0.24. In a second, cognitively unimpaired cohort with other plasma platforms (143 test pairs), it reaches 22.8 dB and 0.32 to 0.61, against at most 21.4 dB and 0.20. Its area under the curve for amyloid positivity is 0.91 and 0.82, against at most 0.63. Ablations show that plasma supplies the amyloid information and the network the image quality. Combining MRI with a blood test yields synthetic amyloid PET that is realistic and tracks burden across regions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33061) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33061)

## 15. Spectrify: Framework for Hyperspectral Image Generation Using Computer Graphics

- **作者**：Yasin Mamedov, Daniil Vladimirov, Sofiia Dorogova et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：15 pages, 7 figures, 5 tables; includes supplementary material

Hyperspectral images (HSIs) record the full spectrum of light at every pixel, so projecting an HSI through a camera's spectral sensitivities gives that camera's RAW image, and one HSI can supply training data for hyperspectral reconstruction and camera-pipeline methods. Real HSIs, however, are slow and expensive to capture, and the largest datasets hold only about 1,000 images. High-level vision commonly replaces scarce real data with images rendered from 3D scenes. Low-level tasks, however, need physically correct spectra, which requires spectral rendering with a measured spectrum for every material in a scene, and existing spectral renderings cover only a handful of scenes, mostly with a simplified image formation model. We present Spectrify, a framework that converts 3D scene datasets into HSIs, together with a library of reflectance spectra labeled by material and compiled from close-range hyperspectral datasets and spectrophotometer measurements. In Spectrify, spectra follow the material: each object receives a physically based optical model and measured reflectance spectra or optical constants from its material category, each lamp an emission spectrum of its type and the sky a modeled daylight spectrum, and Mitsuba 3 path-traces the full light transport of each scene per wavelength. We show that a hyperspectral reconstruction network trained only on our synthetic HSIs generalizes to unseen real datasets better than networks trained on the largest real HSI datasets on most test sets, and that assigning spectra by material category instead of by color alone improves this transfer on every test set. RAW images projected from our HSIs are also useful training data for color constancy, performing on par with existing synthetic RAW generators. We release the 4,500 rendered HSIs, the code of Spectrify and its material-labeled reflectance library.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33042) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33042)

## 16. Linear Probes Can Guide: Training-Free Control of Representation Generators

- **作者**：Pengwei Wang, José Morano, Botond Fazekas et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://penway.cn/lism/

Representation-space generative models, such as Representation Autoencoders (RAEs), leverage pretrained vision foundation models (VFMs) primarily to accelerate training and improve sample fidelity. Yet VFMs are more than reconstruction tokenizers, as they feature rich representation spaces that are highly organized and linearly readable. We observe that this removes a key obstacle of post-hoc guidance: by linearity of expectation, a clean probe applied to an ideal denoiser's prediction yields the exact posterior mean of the probe scores at any noise level. This regime, which we call Semantic Mean-Space, turns semantic guidance into a Bayesian linear inverse problem, bypassing the need for training-time conditioning. With this foundation, we propose LiSM, a unified framework for Linear Guidance in Semantic Mean-Space. To validate the framework, we developed and adapted different solvers, and characterized them based on how they estimate probe-space uncertainty and propagate the correction through the representation. In addition, we propose Fit-Path, a solver designed to achieve strong global and local control. Our results in four control setups (class, attributes, foreground, and keypoints) show that LiSM methods, and Fit-Path in particular, provide effective, lightweight control across global and local tasks, outperforming noise-aware and pixel-space guidance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33037) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33037)

## 17. SHARPnP: Self-Supervised and Plug-and-Play Hyperspectral Super-Resolution from Real Noisy Unaligned Camera Measurements

- **作者**：Mohamad Jouni, Brayan Monroy, Jorge Bacca et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.IV, eess.SP

Affordable snapshot hyperspectral cameras capture spectral images in a single exposure but suffer from limited spatial resolution and sensor noise. Low-cost RGB cameras provide fine visible details to guide NIR reconstruction, but introduce spatially varying cross-camera misalignment. Supervised fusion methods require relatively large datasets of clean, high-resolution hyperspectral images, are sensitive to sensor noise, and generalize poorly across new sensors. We introduce SHARPnP, an interpretable, flexible framework coupling registration and reconstruction for RGB-guided hyperspectral super-resolution from noisy, unaligned measurements without clean references. We adapt a pretrained registration model via a cross-view loss backpropagated through a fixed plug-and-play fusion module. Temporal and recorrupted pairs enable reference-free self-supervision on noisy inputs. At test time, we calibrate cross-camera radiometry and refine alignment. Plug-and-play fusion employs sequential three-band denoising. On the REAL benchmark, SHARPnP outperforms supervised fusion baselines at $4\times$, $8\times$, and $16\times$ on clean inputs and at $4\times$ under Gaussian noise ($σ=5/255, 10/255$). On native RGB-HSI snapshot measurements, it reconstructs all 51 VIS-NIR bands, beyond the conventional 31-band VIS setting, without high-resolution references.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33032) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33032)

## 18. GRAPE-Bench: When Vision Degrades, Does Video Reasoning Hold?

- **作者**：Ce Zhang, Junyi He, Tianshi Ming et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://zhangce01.github.io/grape-bench/

Despite strong performance on standard benchmarks, video reasoning models remain vulnerable to visual degradation, raising concerns about their reliability in safety-critical scenarios. Existing benchmarks address this concern in part by simulating visual degradation or probing model reasoning with challenging questions, leaving open how well models handle naturally degraded video and whether they can make the simpler judgments that matter for safe driving. To this end, we introduce GRAPE-Bench, which jointly evaluates video reasoning across visual degradation conditions and question difficulty. It combines a synthetic set spanning twelve corruption types across daily-life and driving videos with real-world footage of driving at night or in rain, snow, or fog. Each video is paired with three progressively challenging questions, from fundamental judgments with potential safety implications to demanding reasoning stress tests. Across open-weight and frontier models, we observe ranking reversals between synthetic and real-world conditions and level-specific weaknesses obscured by overall accuracy. Synthetic-data fine-tuning improves Qwen3.5-9B's real-world accuracy from 76.7% to 81.1%, while clean-privileged on-policy distillation reaches 80.4% in a separate reasoning-enabled setting. Through preference steering, we further demonstrate continuous control over a frozen model's trade-off between safety-relevant judgments and challenging reasoning, with little change in overall accuracy. Together, GRAPE-Bench provides a unified testbed for evaluating, improving, and controlling video reasoning under degraded visual conditions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33020) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33020)

## 19. E-MAPS: Event-Based Promptable Video Segmentation with Memory-as-Prompt

- **作者**：Adonay Demewez Gebremedhin, Mohamad Alansari, Wessam Shehieb et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

RGB-based video object segmentation (VOS) remains brittle under fast motion and adverse illumination. Event cameras preserve signal under blur and challenging exposure, but provide little evidence when motion slows. Interaction can restore object state for subsequent propagation, yet promptable event methods merely address single windows. We formalize event-based Promptable Video Segmentation (ePVS), where prompts at arbitrary windows initialize, correct, or introduce objects and predictions. We present Event Memory-As-Prompt Segmentation (E-MAPS), which transfers a pretrained promptable segmenter to event streams and learns propagation and correction. Event-to-Token (E2T) aligns recurrent event features with pretrained representations while preserving prompt-conditioning priors. Memory-Aware Prompting (MAP) converts object-conditioned history into sparse prompts, allowing memory and user inputs to share one decoder pathway efficiently. We construct Event-View Masklets (EViM) from 206K aligned RGB-event pairs and 620K masks for two-stage representation transfer and native event-video learning, and establish three training-disjoint benchmarks. E-MAPS leads prompt-once and interactive ePVS across all three benchmarks while being more efficient. It further demonstrates strong performance on zero-shot object tracking benchmarks. Project page: https://github.com/adonaydem/emaps-wacv

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33017) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33017)

## 20. RePix-VFI: Re-rendering Observed Pixels for Video Frame Interpolation with Generative Priors from One-Step Diffusion

- **作者**：Dayeon Kim, Jeonghyeok Do, Munchurl Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Please visit our project page at https://kaist-viclab.github.io/RePix-VFI_site

Multi-frame video frame interpolation (VFI) aims to reconstruct dense video from sparse keyframes, requiring accurate intermediate motion and faithful appearance. As temporal gaps grow, flow-based methods can retain observed textures but misplace them under ambiguous motion, whereas iterative latent-diffusion models can synthesize plausible content but incur substantial sampling costs and may alter details during reconstruction with a variational autoencoder (VAE). We present RePix-VFI, named for Re-rendering observed Pixels, a multi-frame VFI framework that couples draft-conditioned one-step video diffusion with reconstruction from observed keyframes. A pretrained video diffusion model refines a flow-rendered draft of the whole window in a single forward pass. For each target frame, our generative readback matches its two bounding keyframes to the generated prediction using an optical-flow estimator. The resulting correspondences guide re-rendering of original keyframe RGB pixels outside the VAE. A lightweight appearance fusion head combines these renderings with generated appearance. Experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate significant improvements in reconstruction and perceptual quality. On SNU-FILM at $\times$8 and $\times$16, RePix-VFI achieves the highest PSNR and the lowest LPIPS, FloLPIPS, and tOF among the compared methods, exceeding the best evaluated diffusion-based baseline by 1.71 and 1.48dB in PSNR, respectively. It also runs 32--42$\times$ faster than the state-of-the-art multi-frame VFI diffusion model.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33016) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33016)

## 21. Training Lightweight Gaze Models without Target Gaze Annotation: What Makes a Good Teacher?

- **作者**：Scott J. Lewis, Di Fu
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：17 pages, including supplementary material

Model-generated labels offer a scalable route to training lightweight vision models, but what makes a model a good teacher, and can we tell before training a student? We study this in gaze target estimation, where interactive robots need models that are both accurate and efficient. In a controlled comparison of eleven supervision conditions, we vary only the source of gaze-point labels, keeping the student, training protocol and 361k training samples fixed, across three video datasets, two student sizes and three seeds; every student is evaluated against human annotation. Three findings emerge. First, imperfect model-generated labels can replace human annotation: Gaze-LLE, never trained on the target datasets' gaze annotation, trains students that match annotation-trained ones on ViT-Tiny and are significantly more accurate on ViT-Small (average distance error 0.187 vs 0.196), and the ViT-Tiny student runs 32$\times$ faster than its teacher on a laptop CPU. Second, apparent competence can conceal unreliable supervision: given another person's face centre in the prompt, InternVL3 and Qwen3-VL return it on 93.4% and 81.1% of test samples, and a copy-the-coordinate baseline matches InternVL3's labels and students. Without that coordinate, both models fall far behind the specialist. Third, good teachers can be identified before training: validation label error ranks student test error (Spearman's $ρ= 0.91$), and ten annotated clips single out the two specialist teachers in 95% of draws. These findings give practical principles for assessing supervision, exposing shortcuts and selecting teachers for lightweight models.

## 22. RFW-W: Scaling Physical Asset Universes for Generative Embodied Simulation

- **作者**：Jiawei Weng, Rui Zhang, Saining Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：38 pages. Project page: https://github.com/RoboFlywheel-com/RoboFlywheel-World

Scaling embodied simulation requires diverse objects with executable physical representations. We present RFW-W, a physical asset universe connecting rigid, articulated, and deformable asset sources to task-conditioned scene composition. Its reported inventories comprise 1,010,386 asset records: 524,611 rigid assets in 1,212 categories (RFW-R), 402,410 articulated assets in 531 categories (RFW-A), and 83,365 jointly verified deformables in 485 categories (RFW-S). These taxonomies contribute 2,228 dataset--category entries. RFW-R couples category-guided generation with collision geometry, composed physical properties, and interaction annotations; RFW-A uses reusable agent-authored procedural generators with explicit part and joint structure; RFW-S uses structure-aware simulation representations, calibration, and independent testing. A common interface preserves physical requirements specific to each asset type for retrieval and scene assembly. Quantitative evaluations demonstrate that RFW-R provides broader asset coverage and stronger scene-fulfillment capabilities than existing datasets. Across five RoboTwin tasks, using 100 demonstrations per task for each asset source, policies trained on RFW-R achieve macro success rates of 71.2% in clean settings and 41.8% under randomization, exceeding the strongest baseline by 15.8 and 10.6 percentage points, respectively. RFW-W provides a scalable and unified asset foundation for generating physically grounded, task-conditioned embodied worlds.

## 23. MESSI: Multi-Expert Soccer Skill Integration for Physics-Based Character Control

- **作者**：Xiaogang Peng, Jiaqi Zou, Lei Zhong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：Website: https://neu-3dvi.github.io/MESSI/

Physics-based soccer characters must perform diverse motor skills and transition smoothly between them, such as dribbling into a shot. However, skills that perform well in isolation may fail when executed sequentially, as preceding behaviors can leave the character and ball in unfamiliar states. We present MESSI (Multi-Expert Soccer Skill Integration), a framework that enables multiple soccer skill transitions through shared adaptation while preserving the original skill policy outside transitions. MESSI first trains eight task-competent experts using motion priors and task rewards, then distills them into a unified, skill-conditioned Transformer. Rather than retraining individual experts for different transitions, we train a shared, context-conditioned low-rank adapter on the frozen student through online reinforcement learning. Activated upon skill switching, the adapter helps the target skill handle unfamiliar entry states, while a learned exit mechanism determines when to return control to the original student. By disabling the adapter during native skill execution, MESSI preserves the original student policy by construction. Experiments across individual skills, skill transitions, and continuous skill chains demonstrate substantial improvements over direct switching, including an increase in dribbling-to-shooting success from 19.8% to 61.8%.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33011) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33011)

## 24. Embodied AI Fails the Mirror Test: How a Simple Mirror Breaks Robot Policies

- **作者**：Xuanchen Liu, Hejun Zhang, Boxuan Dong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO
- **备注**：Code: https://github.com/iPPLab/robodojo-mirror

Can a simple mirror expose the limitations of today's robotic manipulation policies? Inspired by the mirror self-recognition test in animal cognition, we investigate whether robot policies can reliably manipulate physical objects in scenes containing their reflections. We introduce RoboDojo-Mirror, a benchmark built on RoboDojo that preserves task objectives, success criteria, and policy checkpoints while comparing a mirror-free baseline with single-mirror, dual-mirror, and mirrored-tabletop conditions. Across five policies spanning Vision-Language-Action models, World Action Models, and agentic policies, every mirror condition reduces aggregate success rates: averaged over the three conditions, the drop ranges from 10.8 to 56.7 percentage points across policies, and it reaches 62.5 points on the mirrored tabletop. Because a mirror is also a physical obstacle, we compare it against geometrically matched non-reflective panels; 50--88\% of mirror-specific failures occur without recorded robot--panel contact, so direct collisions alone do not explain the degradation. Trajectory and contact analyses instead reveal recurring behaviors consistent with phantom targeting of reflected objects and spatial mislocalization of real ones. Targeted diagnostics further characterize grasping near reflections, policy-dependent pushing progress, and physical-target interaction under main-camera occlusion. Real-robot experiments with $π_{0.5}$ and FastWAM also show lower overall success rates under the upright-mirror and mirrored-tabletop conditions. These findings reveal a shared vulnerability to reflective environments across diverse robot policies and motivate reflection-aware evaluation of embodied intelligence. Code is available at https://github.com/iPPLab/robodojo-mirror.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33010) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33010)

## 25. Train What You Run: Probabilistic Convolutions for Learning Token Compaction in State Space Models

- **作者**：Piotr Kubaty, Mikhail Saladkou, Jacek Tabor et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Visual token compaction reduces inference cost by passing only selected image patches or video tokens to later layers. In sequence models with local convolutions, each removal changes which tokens are neighbors. Learning which tokens to keep therefore requires a training signal that accounts for the new neighborhoods. We introduce ProbConv, which computes the exact expected convolution before activation at a kept position under independent keep decisions. The operator is differentiable in the keep probabilities. With binary decisions, ProbConv and step gating reproduce the compacted Mamba block. Across image, vision--language and video backbones, ProbConv is strongest under strong compaction: its advantage over matched training controls grows as more tokens are removed. In Cobra, it also outperforms adaptations of published vision--language pruning methods at matched inference budgets. ProbConv also improves over matched zero-mask training in video frame selection and speech frame dropping. Our code is available at: https://github.com/gmum/probconv

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33009) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33009)

## 26. One, No One and One Hundred Thousand CTs: Testing Anatomical Control in Text-to-CT Generation

- **作者**：Francesca Pia Panaccione, Eugenio Lomurno, Matteo Matteucci
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Recent advances in deep learning and the public availability of computed tomography (CT) datasets with paired reports have enabled many Text-to-CT generators. However, their evaluations almost always differ in preprocessing and metrics, which hinders comparison. Moreover, their protocols almost never test how editing the report changes the CT, not even at the same seed, so the report is often assumed to control the anatomy without evidence of how much and how consistently it does. We therefore propose CtrlCT, a toolkit that tests two properties of a generator, Credibility and Control. Credibility tests compare generated CTs, as a population, with real CTs and reports; Control tests are metamorphic and need no reference CT: at a fixed seed, editing one finding should change it as requested, keep negated findings absent and shift distant anatomy no more than a new seed, and the change should hold across seeds. A Report Card summarises all measures and insights for each generator. We benchmark five public generators on 940 chest CTs and 687 edits of pleural effusion and cardiomegaly, holding out a sixth to test the toolkit. Among them, the most credible generators barely respond to an added effusion, while the most responsive produce less realistic and less diverse CTs. The analyses proposed in the literature do not reveal this. We release CtrlCT, its edits, seeds and 30,525 synthetic CTs after acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33003) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33003)

## 27. First-Hit Photoconsistency for Neural Surface Reconstruction

- **作者**：Gabriel Lenain, Edmond Boyer, Jean Ponce
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent neural implicit and Gaussian splatting approaches have achieved impressive results in multi-view surface reconstruction. However, jointly optimizing geometry and appearance introduces ambiguities. A parametric appearance model can compensate for geometric errors, reproducing the input images accurately without an accurate surface. We demonstrate accurate neural implicit surface reconstruction using only multi-view patch photoconsistency with geometry-based visibility, without volumetric rendering or parametric appearance models. For each reference ray, sphere tracing computes the first surface intersection. Its position and normal define a local patch whose photoconsistency is evaluated across visible neighboring views using colors sampled directly from the input images. For efficient sphere tracing, we use a 1-Lipschitz multi-layer perceptron. We use a single photoconsistency loss, without foreground masks or auxiliary geometric regularization. Experiments show that our method achieves the lowest mean Chamfer distance on BlendedMVS (BMVS) among compared methods and matches the geometric accuracy of leading reconstruction methods on DTU.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32998) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32998)

## 28. PAVE: Preference-Aligned Visual Egocentric Learning for the Blind and Low-Vision Users

- **作者**：Nan Li, Shaofeng Liang, Shiyun Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Egocentric video question answering enables blind and low-vision (BLV) users to query their immediate surroundings through natural language. However, existing benchmarks and models primarily optimize for semantic agreement with reference answers, reducing visual assistance to a purely factual matching task. In real-world scenarios, a factually correct response remains practically unhelpful if it lacks the spatial orientation, actionable detail, or conciseness required by the immediate task of the user. Drawing upon real BLV egocentric videos and authentic user queries from EgoBlind, we construct the PA-EgoQA, a preference-aligned dataset that reconstructs reference answers according to empirical BLV communication criteria. Based on this dataset, we introduce Preference-Aligned Visual Egocentric Learning (PAVE), a unified learning and inference framework that couples structured answer-policy fine-tuning with deterministic question-conditioned routing to adapt response formulation directly to the surface form of the query at inference time. We further align the model via Filter-GRPO, which pairs a hierarchical correctness-quality reward with contrast-based candidate-group filtering to extract learning signals from both factual accuracy contrasts and fine-grained quality variations among correct answers. Extensive experiments across three vision-language backbones demonstrate consistent improvements in factual accuracy alongside substantial gains in assistive response quality. These results establish a concrete paradigm for translating human-centered response requirements into egocentric supervision, generation policies, and post-training objectives. Code will be provided at https://github.com/shawnliang420/PAVE.

## 29. Decision-Native Multimodal Clinical Modeling via Anatomical Grounding and Confidence Calibration

- **作者**：Juntao Jiang, Wenqi Zhong, Guangyu Guo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages, 6 figures

Structured clinical decisions from volumetric computed tomography (CT) require anatomically grounded predictions and calibrated confidence estimates. However, existing 3D medical multimodal models often suffer from uncalibrated autoregressive decoding and shortcut reliance on irrelevant anatomical regions. We introduce Radev, a decision-native multimodal model that integrates anatomy-guided visual compression, direct candidate prediction, and counterfactual confidence supervision. To bridge global context with fine-grained slice details, a frozen 3D encoder extracts reusable organ-level features, which are dynamically selected by the query to attend to 2D slice features within predicted anatomical bounds. The resulting compact visual tokens enable the language backbone to estimate candidate probabilities in a single forward pass without autoregressive decoding. We further propose Counterfactual-Calibrated Training (CCT) to compare predictions under intact and intervened organ representations. CCT encourages confidence to decrease when removing target-organ evidence degrades decision quality, while penalizing excessive confidence drops under unrelated-organ removal. To facilitate rigorous evaluation, we construct CT-Verdict, a comprehensive benchmark spanning binary finding presence, multiple-choice questions, and ordinal severity assessment across both in-distribution and external cohorts. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Radev significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both decision accuracy and confidence calibration, while generalizing robustly under distribution shifts. Project page at https://juntaojianggavin.github.io/projects/Radev/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32993) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32993)

## 30. AVQ-Attention-2: Adaptive Vector-Quantized Attention with Exact Local Blocks and Linear-Cost Training

- **作者**：Winfried van den Dool, Patrick Forré, Amir Habibian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV

Attention costs $\mathcal{O}(N^2)$ in the number of tokens. Vector-quantized attention reduces this to $\mathcal{O}(NM)$ by clustering the $N$ keys, attending to the $M$ codewords instead, and its adaptive form, AVQ-attention, concentrates codebook resolution where a query's attention mass actually falls. We present AVQ-Attention-2, a better design that merges it with exact local attention and attends to the mean of each cluster's keys rather than to a codeword. We show three improvements: consistently better quality at comparable cost; training that now also costs $\mathcal{O}(NM)$ rather than $\mathcal{O}(N^2)$; and an approximation accurate enough that the layer can be dropped into a pretrained transformer without fine-tuning. Across a variety of sequence lengths the result is competitive not merely with other approximate attention but with exact dense attention, running faster than FlashAttention at little to no loss in quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32989) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32989)

## 31. When Repair Accuracy Misleads: Training a VLM on Its Own Failures

- **作者**：Ang Qi, Yue Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：21 pages, 2 figures, 16 tables

Fine-tuning a vision-language model on its own failures is a direct way to repair it, but accuracy on held-out failures, the obvious measure of success, misleads. Many mined failures are not model errors: a human audit of 100 failures of Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct on questions generated from COCO and GQA found label errors in 37. And mined failures share wording regularities: question-only classifiers get both answers right on 73.2% of held-out failure pairs. We study both traps with verified failure-driven repair (VFR): typed minimal-pair failure mining, verification by three LLM judges that answer without seeing the stored labels, answer-first corrective targets, and LoRA fine-tuning with replay. VFR repairs 82.8% of held-out failures (base model: 10.0%), and on independently labeled, unconditioned pairs it raises overall accuracy from 87.4% to 92.4% through spatial relations, whose accuracy rises from 66.7% to 96.8% even where the wording points to the wrong answer. Yet on negation, of which it repairs 88.9% of held-out failures, its accuracy on unconditioned pairs falls from 88.7% to 79.7%: it breaks negations about absent objects, rare in its training set. Without verification, accuracy on unconditioned pairs falls to 80.8%, below the base model's 87.4%, through a negation shortcut our development benchmarks miss; without failure selection, it stays at 92.0% (one seed) while repair falls to 73.4%. Training also lowers two-image relation accuracy (74.3% to 49.6%; replay data alone: 48.4%); multi-image replay partly and unevenly reverses this. Paraphrasing the spatial questions keeps most of the gain (87.3% vs. the base model's 62.0%), though rewording lowers spatial repair on held-out failures from 92.9% to 76.8%. On What'sUp's independent photos of objects under others, caption choice stays perfect while accuracy on our yes/no pairs falls from 84.5% to 26.4%.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32982) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32982)

## 32. NEST: Uncovering and Nesting Unknown Objects in Open-World Object Detection

- **作者**：Yuchen Zhang, Yuan Gao, Sebastian Schmidt et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, 9 figures, 14 tables

Open-world object detectors aim to localize objects of classes not covered by their training annotations, but most label them only as "unknown". This label tells a system that an object is present but not what it is, while its broader category would allow a more informed decision. We therefore address both uncovering unknown objects and assigning them to the broader categories of a given class taxonomy. For uncovering, we find that most unknown objects are already localized and labeled "unknown" in our base detector, but are discarded during final top-k selection because background receives higher scores. To mitigate this, we propose a class-agnostic ranker that estimates how well each predicted box fits an object and uses these scores to select the final predictions. Across three benchmarks, our detector improves unknown recall by 3.8% to 195.1% relative to the strongest baseline in each task, while maintaining competitive known-class detection. For categorizing, we draw on Euler diagrams, in which the region of a category contains the regions of its members. Our categorizer builds each category's region from the known classes and learns how much room to leave for members it has never seen. On the same detections, it categorizes unknown objects more accurately than existing hierarchical methods. Code is available at https://github.com/TUM-AVS/NEST.

## 33. Teaching Time Without Losing General Retrieval: Adapting Multimodal Embeddings for Time-Sensitive Video Retrieval

- **作者**：Toya Oyama, Rainer Lienhart, Shin'ichi Satoh
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：24 pages, 11 figures, 14 tables

In text-to-video retrieval, MLLM-based embedding models do well when the text describes the scene (general retrieval), but poorly when it describes actions and their direction (time-sensitive retrieval). TARA, a pioneering work, fine-tunes such a model on text triplets with temporal hard negatives; it improves time-sensitive retrieval but lowers general retrieval (mean R@1 over six benchmarks in our rerun on Qwen3-VL-Embedding-8B: 58.4->44.8), and its released model keeps general retrieval only when ensembled with Qwen3-VL-Embedding. Our goal is strong general and time-sensitive retrieval with one model and one vector per clip. Three findings lead to three simple components. Keep loss: general retrieval follows how far the output embeddings drift from the base model, not how far the weights move; we therefore keep them near the base model, which keeps most general retrieval even without general-domain data. Pair loss: the text embeddings of an action description and its reversed-direction version are well separated, but the video embeddings of a clip and its reversed copy are close; we therefore widen the gap between the video embeddings of each clip and its reversed copy, in the direction of the difference between their two text embeddings. Middle-layer LoRA: frames are combined in the middle layers, and temporal information peaks in or just after them; we therefore train LoRA only on these layers, which nearly matches training all weights on most time-sensitive benchmarks with 0.15% of the parameters and keeps general retrieval better. General R@1 stays at 58.4, while R@1 with each clip's reversed copy in the gallery rises from 55.5 to 63.2; the gains carry over to unseen benchmarks and InternVL2-8B-RA. The keep loss is also a remedy for the TARA recipe (general R@1: 44.8->57.8), and ensembling TARA with Ours gives the highest scores on RTime and unseen CiA among the compared models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32971) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32971)

## 34. FocusAnchor: Zero-Shot Metric Depth by Anchoring a Depth Foundation Prior to Focus Cues

- **作者**：Ashkan Ganj, Hang Su, Tian Guo
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Existing depth-from-focus methods can struggle with weak focus cues, new cameras, or fewer focal slices. We present FocusAnchor, a recurrent model that uses focus-volume lookups to refine metric depth and repeatedly aligns a relative depth prior from a frozen foundation model to the current depth estimate. The aligned prior guides subsequent refinement in a feedback loop, carrying metric scale recovered from textured regions into regions where focus cues are weak while preserving scene structure. To support transfer across cameras, we synthesize training stacks with randomized camera parameters and focus distances. However, stack synthesis blurs existing image noise differently across slices, creating artificial depth cues even on textureless surfaces. We add independent per-slice noise to suppress these cues. We also introduce variable-stack training to improve robustness with fewer focal slices and support more practical mobile capture. Compared with FOSSA ViT-B, FocusAnchor reduces AbsRel by 36% on NYU Depth V2 and by 9% on DDFF-12 after fine-tuning. In zero-shot evaluation, FocusAnchor and its variable-stack variant, FocusAnchor-flex, achieve the two lowest AbsRel values on all five benchmarks. On ZEDD test, FocusAnchor achieves 9% lower AbsRel than the best competing method, FOSSA ViT-B, while FocusAnchor-flex achieves lower AbsRel with 40% fewer focal slices on ZEDD validation.

## 35. VidForce: Predicting Force-Aware Articulation by Observing Humans

- **作者**：Rafay Mohiuddin, Jiaqi Chen, Marc Pollefeys et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：Project page: https://rafaymhddn.github.io/vidforce

People can anticipate how an object moves and the effort an interaction may require by watching others. How can embodied agents acquire this understanding from video? Force measurements provide direct sensory supervision, but require specialized equipment and remain limited in quantity. We introduce VidForce, which estimates an interaction trajectory together with a force profile from a third-person RGB video; 2D contact clicks and textual object-action prompts are optional. Our approach makes limited instrumented measurements useful for interpreting human interactions through learned physical prototypes as a prior. The prototypes share learned force levels and temporal profiles across interactions of comparable effort, with visual and semantic cues determining their contribution to each observation. Geometric and vision-language features adapt this prior to the observed interaction, while a joint conditional flow refines an initial tracked trajectory together with the force profile. Paired human and instrumented-gripper recordings provide measured force references for supervising human video, and a separately trained torque model demonstrates that the formulation extends to other sensory quantities. We evaluate trajectory recovery and force estimation through baseline comparisons and controlled ablations on held-out interactions from the Hoi! dataset. On this evaluation subset, VidForce achieves 6.6 cm ATE, peak-force MAE and RMSE of 4.81 N and 5.92 N, respectively, and force-profile RMSE of 5.52 N. On real-world videos, VidForce infers motion and force reliably and consistently despite the variability in human execution. We further deploy VidForce on a Spot robot, where trajectories and force profiles estimated from human demonstrations drive real-world articulated-object manipulation, and report success rates across repeated trials. Project page: https://rafaymhddn.github.io/vidforce

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32967) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32967)

## 36. Benchmarking Evidence Sufficiency in Vision-Language Models for Autonomous Driving

- **作者**：Nhat Ha Nguyen, Sudipta Chowdhury, Lokesh Das
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Reliable driving VLMs must do more than answer correctly or localize relevant scene actors: they must determine whether the available visual evidence is sufficient to support an answer. Existing evaluations largely treat grounding and answerability as separate measurements, making it difficult to distinguish well-supported predictions from those localized to relevant but insufficient evidence. We introduce DriveAnswer, a benchmark of 20,151 questions across seven reasoning families, that operationalizes this distinction using matched strong- and weak-evidence configurations. Each pair preserves the underlying scene fact and semantic answer while changing whether the observations support answering, making abstention the appropriate response under weak evidence. On the six-family evaluation, the strongest zero-shot baseline reaches 67.31% strong-evidence accuracy, yet only 21.55% of matched questions satisfy both answer-abstain behavior and complete grounding. With controlled adaptation, Gemma-4-12B reaches 63.29% on this joint capability, nearly tripling its zero-shot performance. Qwen3-VL-8B exhibits the same broad improvement in evidence-sensitive answering and grounding. These results show that semantic correctness, evidence sufficiency, and grounding are distinct capabilities that should be evaluated jointly.

## 37. FedTumor-IoMT: Federated Learning-Aided Brain Tumor Diagnosis on Resource-Constrained Edge IoMT Devices

- **作者**：Pranshu Jena, Prajnyajit Mohanty, Umesh C. Pati
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Joint brain tumor segmentation and classification models are computationally intensive, limiting their training on resource-constrained edge platforms and restricting privacy-preserving Federated Learning FL in the Internet of Medical Things IoMT. Existing approaches either perform both tasks on GPU-equipped workstations or support only classification on edge devices. To address this gap, this work proposes a lightweight multitask FL framework for joint segmentation and classification through local edge training. A shared pretrained LiteMedSAM encoder with TinyViT extracts features for a coordinate-attention-based segmentation decoder and a segmentation-guided classification head, eliminating a separate feature extractor. Selective encoder freezing retains 95\% trainable parameters while reducing on-device training costs. The proposed framework is experimentally validated on a laboratory testbed of five resource-constrained edge clients using 1,200 T1-weighted MRI samples. It achieves 94.20\% classification accuracy, 88.53\% DSC, 83.68\% IoU, and 99.87\% ROC-AUC. Its classification accuracy remains within 0.6\% of the GPU-trained baseline of 95.21\% across five FL rounds. The attention and feature refinement modules improve DSC and IoU by 3.82\% and 4.60\%, respectively, with 0.74 GFLOPs of additional computation. On edge hardware, the framework achieves 46.54 s inference latency, 52.8\% CPU utilization, and a peak temperature of 65$^\circ$C without thermal throttling. Its inference latency is observed to be 6.4--10.5 times lower than that of existing frameworks. These results demonstrate the feasibility of privacy-preserving joint brain tumor segmentation and classification on resource-constrained edge platforms.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32952) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32952)

## 38. OmniHarness-RSI: Modality-Aware Harness Evolution for Omnimodal Understanding

- **作者**：Shilin Ma, Xingming Shui, Jinglin Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent advances in harness-based recursive self-improvement (RSI) have improved video understanding by evolving executable agent workflows without updating foundation model parameters. However, extending this paradigm to omnimodal understanding remains challenging, as the heterogeneous and interdependent nature of multimodal perception and reasoning creates a complex evolution space, making targeted harness optimization difficult. To address these challenges, we propose OmniHarness-RSI, a modality-aware framework for evolving agent harnesses. Specifically, we factorize the evolution space into three capability dimensions: modality routing and collaboration, modality-aware evidence localization, and modality-specific perception. Based on this factorization, we construct a Factorized-Harness Agent integrating specialized perception experts and audio-video retrieval tools. We further introduce modality-guided harness evolution, which identifies deficiencies through structured modality-capability attribution and refines corresponding harness strategies through interactive code revisions and execution feedback. Validation-guided selection and held-out evaluation assess generalization within and across datasets. Experiments on WorldSense, Daily-Omni, and OmniVideoBench demonstrate test accuracy improvements of 8.86, 8.13, and 6.23 percentage points, respectively, consistently outperforming modality-agnostic RSI. The evolved harnesses also demonstrate cross-dataset transfer without adaptation, while reducing planner calls and viewed frames.

## 39. ProX-MAD: Aggregating Spatial Context into Representative Proxies for Few-shot Medical Anomaly Detection

- **作者**：Seunghyun Oh, Seongsu Lee, Seunghye Chae et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to ACCV 2026

Few-shot medical anomaly detection (MAD) has largely followed a dataset-specific "one-for-one" paradigm, leaving unified "one-for-many" modeling underexplored. Naively extending existing methods to this setting causes substantial performance degradation, especially at the pixel level. We attribute this to their detection mechanisms: a shared decision boundary becomes unreliable across heterogeneous lesion definitions, whereas a normal-only memory bank cannot adequately cover diverse normal distributions with few references. We propose ProX-MAD, a proxy-based framework that aggregates spatial context into compact, learnable, state-specific proxies for unified few-shot MAD. Its Masked Proxy Infusion Attention (MPIA) module cross-attends proxy tokens to reference patch features and applies proxy-wise masking to retain high-confidence correspondences, mitigating proxy collapse and encouraging diverse proxies that summarize both normal and abnormal references. Across six medical datasets, ProX-MAD exhibits only a marginal performance drop from one-for-one to one-for-many training. Despite being trained only once as a unified model, it also outperforms prior state-of-the-art methods on average even when they use their more favorable one-for-one protocol, demonstrating the practicality of unified multi-dataset MAD.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32941) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32941)

## 40. Do Vision-Language Models Know Their Own Visual Weaknesses? Probing Capability-Level Metacognition

- **作者**：Dohoon Kim, Taesup Moon
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：24 pages, 16 figures

Metacognition includes knowing one's own strengths and weaknesses across kinds of tasks, not only judging whether a single answer is correct. We test whether vision-language models (VLMs) know in advance which kinds of visual tasks and image conditions are hard for them, across a wide range of open and proprietary models. Because some tasks are hard for every model, we separate what a model knows about this shared difficulty from what it knows about itself. We find that VLMs know little in advance about their visual weaknesses. They misjudge their overall accuracy and do not even know which kinds of tasks are hard for every model, and most misjudge how degraded images affect them. We further train VLMs to predict their own accuracy, which teaches them their own level of accuracy on kinds unseen in training but not which kinds are hard for them. However, information about these weaknesses appears once a model starts solving. On a few unlabeled questions of a kind, the probability a model assigns to its own answers follows weaknesses particular to the model, across kinds of tasks and image conditions. VLMs thus hold information about their own weaknesses that their stated predictions do not yet reflect. By separating what is particular to a model from what holds for every model, our evaluation offers a test for methods that aim to teach VLMs their own weaknesses, and the answer probability offers them a signal to start from.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32940) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32940)

## 41. Efficient Video Retrieval Augmented Generation via Token Allocation

- **作者**：Ayush Barik, Fan Lai, Francis Y. Yan
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG, eess.IV

Efficient long-video understanding requires identifying relevant evidence while minimizing the tokens processed by vision-language models (VLMs). Existing video retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) methods rely on frame selection and token compression, but overlook how much each additional token is worth. We introduce Lembas, a plug-and-play framework that formulates video RAG as a token allocation problem over frames and subtitles under a shared budget. Our key insight is that the value of a token diminishes within each item and decreases further as similar evidence receives more tokens. Lembas captures both effects in a single objective and solves it with an efficient iterative water-filling algorithm that has a provable convergence guarantee. For multi-turn conversations, Lembas reuses the tokens processed in earlier turns and refines relevance with the VLM's attention at no extra cost. Our evaluations show that Lembas improves accuracy by up to 7.2 percentage points over state-of-the-art baselines at comparable compute. On multi-turn video understanding, Lembas achieves up to 1.5 percentage points higher accuracy with 1.7-17.3x fewer TFLOPs.

## 42. HARI4D: Hand-Articulation Aware 4D Reconstruction of Human-Object Interaction

- **作者**：Semin Kim, Xianghui Xie, Chuqiao Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Reasoning about human-object interaction from RGB cameras is important for gaming, VR environments, and robot learning. Yet 4D reconstruction from a monocular RGB camera is challenging, especially when articulated hands are involved. Prior full-body works neglect hands during reconstruction, causing them to penetrate into, or float from the object. We present HARI4D, a hand-articulation-aware method for reconstructing spatially and temporally consistent 4D human-object interaction from monocular RGB video. We introduce a history-aware object tracker that provides robust and stable tracking. Based on these tracks, we improve the interaction configuration through coarse-to-fine reasoning that follows the order of object manipulation: ReachNet first brings the full body and arms to the object during contact, and TouchNet then finalizes hand articulation according to temporal context and local object geometry. On ARCTIC, unseen in training, our method reduces the object error by 56% and improves hand contact F1 from 0.866 to 0.921 over prior art. Our method recovers accurate full body-object interaction with coherent hand articulations and applies to online videos. Our code and pretrained models will be released.

## 43. IMPACT-Seg: Consistent Anatomical Segmentation and Representation Learning Across CT, CBCT, and MRI

- **作者**：Cédric Hémon, Valentin Boussot, Matthew McCormick et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：preprint (https://github.com/HEMONCedric/IMPACTSeg, https://huggingface.co/HCedric/IMPACTSeg)

Computed tomography (CT), cone-beam CT (CBCT), and magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) provide complementary representations of the same underlying anatomy, yet substantial differences in image appearance challenge consistent segmentation and deformable registration in adaptive radiotherapy. We investigate whether dense anatomical segmentation supervision can promote cross-modality correspondence without explicit feature alignment. We introduce IMPACT-Seg, which segments 126 structures across CT, CBCT, and MRI using shared network parameters. A collection of 8,290 volumes was assembled from public cohorts through label harmonization, model-assisted pseudo-label completion, iterative refinement, and 2,864 physics-based CBCT simulations with voxel-aligned CT labels. Unpaired images were used to jointly train 2.5D and 3D residual-encoder networks. On 139 real CBCTs, IMPACT-Seg 3D achieved a mean Dice of 0.842 versus 0.671 for the strongest comparator. On 497 voxel-aligned CT-simulated-CBCT pairs, the CT-to-CBCT Dice decrease was 0.022 versus 0.153-0.178 for competing methods. On independent pancreatic radiotherapy CBCTs, gastrointestinal Dice was 0.735 versus 0.634 for TotalSegmentator. Prediction agreement reached 0.881 on registered CT-CBCT and 0.790 on CT-MRI pairs. Adding MRI training data reduced CT-MRI encoder feature retrieval error from 36.8 to 3.2 mm. Registration pipelines incorporating IMPACT-Seg features ranked first on two multimodal Learn2Reg benchmarks. The 3D model segmented a whole-body CT in 15 s. These findings show that dense anatomical segmentation supervision can support consistent anatomical representations across modalities, with applications to both segmentation and registration. Code, datasets, and pretrained models are available at https://github.com/HEMONCedric/IMPACTSeg.

## 44. Motion Conditioned World Modeling for Embodied Visual Tracking

- **作者**：Jiahao Zhang, Xuan Yao, Junyu Gao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

World models support embodied decision-making by predicting future environment states. In embodied visual tracking, uncertain target motion and robot-induced viewpoint changes jointly affect visual observations, making future-state prediction challenging. We propose Motion-Conditioned World Modeling, which incorporates predicted target-motion distributions and robot ego-motion into latent state transitions. A Geometry--Progress module jointly models geometric paths and single-step travel distances through shared motion modes and path-conditioned progress distributions. This formulation captures uncertainty in route selection and motion progress while preserving their dependence. Action-induced coordinate transformations express predicted paths in the next robot frame, providing geometrically grounded conditions for future visual-state prediction. Future visual feature reconstruction supervises the motion predictor through the conditioned transition branch, linking explicit motion representations to subsequent observations. The model is jointly optimized with motion likelihood, feature reconstruction, and expert-action supervision, enabling the tracking policy to combine visual context with predictive motion cues.Experiments on three embodied tracking benchmarks demonstrate improved tracking performance and cross-environment generalization with a lightweight $0.5$B backbone.

## 45. Label leakage through file headers: geometry and batch confounds in public mammography datasets, and a pre-training acceptance gate

- **作者**：Achraf Toufah, Rana Saad, Moulay Youssef El Hafidi
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, eess.IV
- **备注**：27 pages, 2 figures, 9 tables. Under review at DMLR. Code and derived tables: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24372683

A dataset whose diagnostic label can be recovered from non-diagnostic information yields high, reproducible and uninformative model performance. We applied pre-training controls that see only image geometry, global texture, a sub-collection identifier or one covariate to six public mammography datasets, against a stop threshold of AUC 0.60. In KAU-BCMD, geometry alone predicted the BI-RADS class at AUC 1.0000 in a local copy and at a mean of 0.979 in a public Kaggle archive; after resizing, texture alone gave 0.847. In CMMD, the sub-collection prefix predicted malignancy at 0.7753; the restricted sub-collection passed geometry (0.5000) and texture (0.5810) but failed an identifier-order control added post hoc (test AUC 0.9926). CBIS-DDSM exceeded the stop on geometry (pooled mean 0.654 over 20 splits), a verdict statistic chosen post hoc; its official mass and calcification splits did not. M-SYNTH, INbreast and a partial VinDr-Mammo copy passed the few controls run. We release the code and derived tables. There is no quantum component in the inference path.
