## 46. DualSpec: Spectral Reconstruction with Optimized Stereo Cameras

- **作者**：Luca Cogo, Marco Buzzelli, Simone Bianco et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project Page: https://lucacogo.github.io/DualSpec/

Spectral imaging captures information that ordinary RGB sensors cannot, but dedicated spectral cameras can be expensive and bulky. Recovering dense spectra from RGB images offers an accessible alternative, but distinct spectra can produce the same camera response, generating an ambiguity that even more advanced recovery algorithms cannot always resolve. Therefore, increasing the spectral information captured at acquisition is essential. We propose DualSpec, a stereo acquisition framework that jointly optimizes two external optical filters and a reconstruction network. Each filter is placed in front of a standard RGB camera, and the two are designed to produce six responses that provide complementary information for the network. Experiments across reconstruction architectures and spectral ranges show that DualSpec reduces mean spectral angle error by 36-72% relative to a single RGB camera and by 11-29% relative to stereo pairs selected from commercial filters. Cross-dataset evaluations, including a new benchmark of spectral images captured from displaced viewpoints, show that the method generalizes and remains robust to viewpoint misalignment, while analyses of thin-film approximations support optical feasibility.

## 47. Biomechanically Grounded Prediction of Selective Motor Control and Clinical Outcomes from Infant Video

- **作者**：Divya Joshi, Ruize Yang, Colleen Peyton et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Submitted as a full contributed paper to IEEE International Conference on Rehabilitation Robotics (ICORR) 2027

Early identification of cerebral palsy (CP) relies on observational assessments of spontaneous infant movement, such as the General Movement Assessment (GMA) and the Baby Observational Selective Control AppRaisal (BabyOSCAR), which require trained raters and substantial scoring time. Existing automated approaches derive hand-engineered features from two-dimensional keypoints, which describe image-plane motion rather than the three-dimensional behavior of individual joints. We present Baby Biomechanical 3D Body (BB3D), an infant-adapted, biomechanical pipeline that estimates joint-angle trajectories from single-camera video. Against a multi-view markerless motion capture reference in 17 infants, BB3D's median Procrustes-aligned error was 17.5~mm for joint centers and 20.4~mm for marker sites. A spatio-temporal transformer, pretrained on BB3D joint-angle trajectories from 1k+ single-camera recordings, and fine-tuned on 550 videos from 350 infants, predicted each individual BabyOSCAR item with a mean within-site AUC of 0.79 and the BabyOSCAR total with a Spearman correlation of 0.86 on held-out recordings. Without being trained on diagnosis, the predicted total separated infants with CP from those without CP with an AUC of 0.86, exceeding a replicated two-dimensional feature classifier (0.67) but below the expert clinician rater's total (0.97). Predicted scores also carried information about functional ability and body distribution, though less than expert clinician scores. These results show that biomechanically grounded representations of monocular video can support scalable, joint-level assessment of selective motor control in infancy

## 48. 3D Tracking Any Point with Events and Frames

- **作者**：Roberto Pellerito, Zijia Dai, Laurent Kneip et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Tracking a fast-moving point in 3D is prohibitive when RGB frames are blurred or too far apart to reveal its motion. Event cameras record brightness changes between frames, providing measurements inside these temporal gaps. We introduce TrackAnyMotion, which combines monocular RGB frames and events to track arbitrary points in 2D and 3D, including at times when no RGB frame is available. The motion expected for a static point, given scene geometry and camera motion, provides a reference for identifying independently moving objects. An independent-motion decoder combines geometric motion cues with event guidance and 2D tracks, feeding its predictions back into iterative 2D and 3D refinement. We also introduce HyperEvent, a synthetic RGB--Event dataset with high-frequency 3D trajectories, geometry, and independent-motion labels. At RGB frame timestamps on this benchmark, TrackAnyMotion reduces 3D and 2D endpoint errors by 70\% and 32\%, respectively.

## 49. Simultaneously Boosting Efficiency and Capability for Linear Attention via Adaptive Channel Compression and Activation Purification

- **作者**：Senqi Guan, Xianchao Zhang, Moyan Tian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：13 pages, 5 figures

Self-attention's quadratic complexity constitutes a core bottleneck for Vision Transformers (ViTs). Existing efficiency-oriented optimizations, including softmax-based and linear attention (LA) variants, generally compromise model representational capacity, leading to inevitable performance degradation. We propose a novel linear attention which alleviates this efficiency-capability dilemma. For the first time, we embed an Adaptive Channel Compression and Activation Purification (ACCAP) mechanism into the QKV projection of linear attention, which enables the model to retain high-dimensional feature information while performing computations with reduced channels. We further fuse a focused kernel function and a self-gating module into ACCAP to construct a lightweight linear attention module (ACCAP-LA). Our theoretical analysis shows that, compared to standard linear attention, ACCAP-LA reduces the attention module's parameter count by 79.17% and FLOPs by 77.5%. Extensive experiments demonstrate ACCAP-LA's effectiveness: when applied to ViT, it achieves 79.4% Top-1 accuracy on ImageNet-1K with only 5.8M parameters, and more notably, it boasts strong cross-architecture compatibility, boosting EfficientViT-b1 and PVT-T by 3.3% and 3.4% respectively.

## 50. MemHand: Memory-Enhanced World-Space Hand Motion Reconstruction from Egocentric Videos

- **作者**：Junyi Ma, Zhangshuo Qi, Erhang Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recovering the continuous world-space motion of both hands from egocentric video is essential for applications such as human-to-robot policy transfer and augmented reality. Achieving this goes beyond reconstructing the hands from each individual frame, as the context that a frame needs is often spread over time, especially around occlusions or when a hand moves out of sight. Recent works have explored how to exploit temporal context, yet they mostly draw it from fixed-length windows. Such windows cannot guarantee that every frame has the context required to reconstruct the hands in world space with accurate poses and metric scale. To address this limitation, we introduce MemHand, a memory-enhanced framework for world-space hand motion reconstruction. MemHand maintains two complementary forms of memory. The Short-Term Event Memory keeps the context of the well-observed hand around each occlusion or out-of-sight event and supplies it to frames with weak or missing visual evidence. In addition, since the metric scale holds over the whole recording, the Long-Term Scale Memory retains the most reliable observations across the video as scale anchors within a bounded state. Across benchmarks featuring severe occlusions and frequent out-of-sight intervals, MemHand achieves state-of-the-art accuracy and generalizes well to unseen domains. Benefiting from the synergy of both memories, it cuts W-MPJPE by 57\% on ARCTIC and 70\% on HOT3D relative to StableHand, delivering continuous and metrically accurate world-space hand poses and trajectories. We will release our code and models at the project page: https://irmvlab.github.io/memhand.github.io

## 51. CityRecast: Suppressing Persistent Artifacts in Generative Satellite 3D Reconstruction

- **作者**：Shuo Cui, Yingyan Hou, Wanxuan Lu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, 8 figures

Satellite-based 3D reconstruction is limited by sparse observations and changes across acquisition dates. Existing methods use generative priors to recover missing visual detail, but ghosting, streaking, and blur persist in their Gaussian scenes. These artifacts become particularly apparent in low-elevation renderings. We introduce CityRecast to suppress these artifacts through Gaussian scene repair and supervision calibration. To correct the Gaussian distributions that produce these artifacts, we first use multi-angle warm-up to constrain their spatial extent. This reduces the initial ghosting carried into progressive refinement. Surface-guided replacement then continues to repair the Gaussian distribution while preserving existing appearance. To provide more reliable supervision for this process, observation calibration mitigates interference from multi-temporal observations. Joint refinement further reduces conflicts among generated views. Experiments on DFC2019 and IARPA demonstrate reduced artifacts in low-elevation renderings while maintaining satellite-view fidelity. Compared with Skyfall-GS, CityRecast reduces FID-CLIP by 25.3% on DFC2019 and 51.8% on IARPA. MUSIQ increases by 14.54 and 24.95 points, respectively.

## 52. DOPER: Deep Object Pose Estimation Revisited

- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

We present DOPER, an RGB pose estimation framework that shares a visual backbone across object detection, segmentation, and keypoint prediction to recover the 6D pose of known objects through Perspective-n-Point. Starting from synthetic training, DOPER adapts to real observations using partial supervision while retaining synthetic keypoint labels. Calibrated multi-view observations provide geometric constraints for keypoint supervision. During inference, optional heatmap refinement fits a rigid pose directly to the predicted keypoint distributions, resolving ambiguous correspondences without depth input or a learned pose refiner. Starting from underspecified CAD models, DOPER progressively refines the object representation and pose estimator. Results on SPEED+ and SHIRT further demonstrate effective adaptation to illumination changes and accurate temporal pose estimation. Across the evaluated single-view and multi-view configurations, DOPER achieves state-of-the-art detection AP results on HANDAL, HOPEv2, HOT3D, and T-LESS while using only RGB and running in real time (35 Hz per object model on one GB300). These results demonstrate the effectiveness of jointly learning detection and pose keypoints, with real observations and geometric constraints.

## 53. Aqua2CAD : Observation-Guided Point Cloud Correction and Completion for CAD Reconstruction from Underwater Stereo Images

- **作者**：Weizhao Huang, Jiayi Zhang, Pangkit Fong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recovering editable computer-aided design (CAD) models from underwater stereo images requires accurate and complete geometry, yet stereo reconstruction produces displaced points and missing regions. The original views retain cross-view disagreement and ray evidence lost during point-cloud fusion. Aqua2CAD exploits this evidence in two stages: view-conditioned correction and quality filtering establish reliable geometric support, while multi-view observations condition a learned shape prior to infer missing surfaces in the original metric frame. The recovered points support surface fitting and solid assembly with manually revised face groups. On an OceanSim-based synthetic benchmark, Aqua2CAD reduces point-cloud Chamfer distance by 60% relative to the stereo reconstruction. Compared with a matched point-only completion backbone, it improves missing-region recovery at 5,mm from 16.27% to 24.16%. With a shared CAD backend, exterior acceptance increases from 6/19 to 14/19. Qualitative results on selected real underwater captures demonstrate recovery of the main structures of six 3D-printed objects.

## 54. SAVERA: Bridging Frozen Vision and Language Models via Sparse Alignment and Visual Evidence Reuse

- **作者**：Jiajun Shen, Chuancheng Shi, Jiayi Lyu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Independently pretrained vision and language models may learn shared structures of the same underlying world, providing a foundation for combining them to build multimodal capabilities. However, a key challenge remains in aligning cross-modal feature representations while preserving access to visual information before and after language adaptation. To this end, we propose sparse alignment with visual evidence-guided residual adaptation (SAVERA), a framework that combines frozen vision and language models through bidirectional alignment in a shared sparse space and prompt-guided residual fusion. Specifically, we first present shared-space projection, activation reconstruction and encoding (SPARE), which represents original visual, adapted visual, and textual features through shared soft-gated sparse encoding, with branch-specific reconstruction to preserve their respective information. Second, we devise bridge--text embedding and activation matching (BEAM), a bidirectional cross-modal alignment module that aligns adapted visual and textual features at both the sample and neuron levels, establishing fine-grained cross-modal correspondence. Finally, we introduce prompt-conditioned residual integration of vision and adapted features (PRIVA), a prompt-guided residual fusion module that models vision--language interactions through gating and attention and integrates complementary information from original and adapted representations through residual fusion. Extensive experiments show that SAVERA achieves competitive performance on image captioning and visual question answering while reducing object hallucination.

## 55. EEG-World: Context-Conditioned Dynamics of the Watching Brain

- **作者**：Junxiang Zhang, Jiayu Zuo, Wenhao Ye et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, q-bio.NC
- **备注**：20 pages, 8 figures, 3 tables; appendix with 3 figures and 6 tables. Ancillary file lists the clip split

Brain encoding models predict neural responses from audiovisual input, but typically overlook how ongoing brain activity shapes an individual's response. We introduce EEG-World, a recurrent world model that forecasts 10 seconds of EEG band-power dynamics from 6 seconds of preceding EEG and the subsequent audiovisual stimulus. Pretrained on continuous EEG, EEG-World learns shared neural dynamics and adapts to unseen individuals through lightweight subject-specific readouts. Using 2,584 hours of EEG from 78 participants, we show that nonlinear recurrent forecasting surpasses strong history-aware linear baselines at scale. EEG-World achieves a forecasting correlation of 0.261 versus 0.233 for the strongest history-and-video linear model, with a 3.3-second forecasting-horizon advantage. Its learned dynamics transfer to unseen participants, outperforming the linear baseline in 23 of 24 individuals after calibration. While ongoing EEG history accounts for most predictive performance, controlled stimulus analyses reveal an additional video-dependent component. This component also enables evaluation of visual representations, with V-JEPA 2 improving clip-locked prediction by 35% over combined CLIP and DINOv2 features. Our results demonstrate the value of transferable neural dynamics for individual EEG forecasting and highlight the importance of separating state-driven from stimulus-driven predictability.

## 56. Reliable and grounded reporting of any computed tomography scan with FORA

- **作者**：Sezgin Er, Utku Turkbey, Alperen Gur et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：58 pages, 5 figures, 7 Extended Data figures, 17 Supplementary figures. Code and models: https://github.com/forithmus/FORA

Artificial intelligence systems for computed tomography (CT) remain largely restricted to specific anatomical regions, examination types or predefined abnormalities, limiting their ability to provide comprehensive and spatially grounded interpretations. Here we present FORA, a general-purpose CT model that recognizes and spatially localizes 1,028 hierarchically organized radiological concepts and generates reports across the 28 CT protocols represented in our cohort. Trained on 304,962 routine CT examinations supervised by their accompanying reports, FORA learns to localize abnormalities without manually annotated lesions. Its architecture combines multiple-instance learning with an interpretable concept bottleneck that connects spatial evidence, radiological findings and report generation. We first developed and benchmarked the architecture on chest radiographs, achieving state-of-the-art performance, before training a separate model on three-dimensional CT examinations. On 36,619 held-out CT examinations, FORA achieved a macro area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) of 0.918 for radiological concept recognition, exceeding the strongest of 18 existing CT models by 0.21. FORA also ranked first on three public benchmarks, outperforming Merlin on its own test set without training on that institution's data (AUROC 0.907 versus 0.826). On independent localization benchmarks for pulmonary emboli, renal masses and rib fractures, FORA's strongest spatial evidence fell within annotated lesions at rates up to 348 times greater than chance. FORA achieved more than threefold higher macro-averaged finding recall than the strongest existing report generator. These results show that a single model can combine broad abnormality recognition, spatial localization and report generation across 12 CT examination types, using routine reports without lesion-level supervision.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32823) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32823)

## 57. PhysMend: Verification-Guided Program Refinement for Physics-Aware Video Generation

- **作者**：Meilong Xu, Ziyi Zhao, Bhagyashree Puranik et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：22 pages, 7 figures, 11 tables, including supplementary material

Video generative models synthesize realistic scenes, yet their interactions often violate physics. A ball dropped onto sand should displace grains and settle into a crater, whereas a generated video may show it passing through the surface or leaving the sand flat. Post-hoc methods typically score generated videos with a vision-language model (VLM), but such scores offer limited guidance on which object or parameter to modify, and each correction costs another synthesis. Simulation-based pipelines instead run fixed stages before synthesis, so simulation errors reach the video. To address these limitations, we present PhysMend, a training-free framework for verification-guided program refinement. PhysMend describes the target event as an editable physical program of grounded objects, events, and required relations, paired with tunable execution parameters. Our key insight is to verify and repair the event on its simulated rollout and on the depth video that conditions the generator (depth control) rather than on generated videos, enabling quantitative violation measurement, targeted editing, and fast re-verification before synthesis. A hybrid verifier evaluates semantic event consistency, physical plausibility, solver stability, and visibility in the depth control. VLM judgments are confined to event semantics and cannot override failed physical measurements, which limits the impact of VLM hallucinations. Each failed check localizes the program fields eligible for repair, and its measured violation scales a bounded update of their parameters. Only accepted depth control reaches the generator, which runs once per input. Experiments on the commonly used benchmarks Physics-IQ, PhyGenBench, and VBench-2.0 demonstrate substantial gains across different generator backbones; for example, PhysMend raises the overall Physics-IQ score of Wan-VACE from 27.33 to 39.40.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32821) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32821)

## 58. Residual Guidance for Real-Time Interactive Video Generation

- **作者**：NaHyeon Park, Kyeong-Hwan Moon, Youjin Jeon et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.MM, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://residual-guidance.github.io

Real-time interactive video generation allows users to change the prompt during generation to steer the evolving video. However, existing streaming models, which generate videos chunk by chunk, often keep following the previous prompt after a switch. We trace this failure to context inertia, where the previous chunk acts as a strong prior that suppresses the new prompt. Furthermore, we find that this inertia persists even when the cache retains only the previous chunk, yet discarding the context breaks visual continuity, which limits approaches that edit the cache. Rather than editing the cache, we propose Residual Guidance (RG), a simple, training-free method that counteracts context inertia with a correction measured outside the cache. Specifically, RG denoises the re-noised previous chunk once under the new prompt without the cache and adds the resulting residual to the early denoising steps of the next chunk, where its content is largely decided and the new prompt is most suppressed. Across settings ranging from a single switch to long multi-switch generation, RG substantially improves prompt switching capability on existing streaming models, including those with dedicated switching mechanisms, while largely retaining visual quality. As it requires no training or per-model tuning and adds only one forward pass per chunk, RG can be easily integrated into existing real-time video generation pipelines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32820) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32820)

## 59. Beyond Image-Text Pairs: Aligning Semantic Changes for Fine-Grained Visual Understanding

- **作者**：Yujie Sun, Zeqing Wang, Shuang Liang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Image-text alignment serves as a foundation for modern vision-language models (VLMs), yet prevailing training paradigms provide limited supervision for learning how semantic changes correspond across modalities. To address this limitation, we propose a post-training method that learns cross-modal semantic change correspondences through controlled visual and textual variations. Specifically, we introduce a Semantic Difference Alignment (SDA) loss which can align the directions of corresponding visual and textual representation changes. We combine our proposed SDA with pairwise sigmoid matching to jointly learn image-text correspondences and cross-modal semantic changes, encouraging more fine-grained and discriminative visual representations. To support this learning, we construct VarFamily-40K, a dataset of image-text pairs with controlled cross-modal semantic variations. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements in compositional understanding and downstream classification performance, alongside further gains on multimodal tasks when the adapted visual encoder is integrated into VLMs.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32817) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32817)

## 60. SpatialArena: Where Do Geometric Foundation Models Stand?

- **作者**：Yu Chen, Haosen Yang, Su Ee Tan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Technique Report, MVP Lab; 61 pages

3D perception is shifting from task-specific pipelines to geometric foundation models: monocular depth networks, feed-forward 3D/4D reconstruction models, and vision--language models that predict geometry. However, their progress cannot be measured reliably. Published numbers are not comparable, because every paper samples frames, sets resolutions, and selects test splits differently. They are not robust because the alignment required for every up-to-scale prediction can outweigh the gap between models: fitting depth in the wrong space nearly triples its error, and standard trajectory alignment understates the error of failing methods by up to $2.6\times$. And they rarely cover long recordings, dynamic scenes, or aerial views. We present SpatialArena, an open arena that scores geometric models with the same metrics and rules, with robust and deterministic alignment. SpatialArena spans five tasks, from monocular depth to SLAM. It covers 23 public datasets with 418,929 images across indoor, outdoor, aerial, object-centric, and dynamic scenes, together with Spatial Arena, our own captures with accurate poses and depth. Evaluating 52 models, we find that single-image depth is close to saturation on public benchmarks but not on large, uncontrolled scenes, and that progress now comes from consistency across frames and views, where feed-forward 3D/4D models match or surpass monocular specialists and overtake video depth models. The benchmark, toolbox, and leaderboard will be publicly released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32816) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32816)

## 61. Back to Raw Motion: Latent-Free Diffusion Transformers for Text-to-Motion and Exact Conditional Generation

- **作者**：Yun Chen, Jeonghyeok Do, Munchurl Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：The first two authors contributed equally to this work (equal contribution). Please visit our project page at https://kaist-viclab.github.io/RawMo_site

As generative models tackle larger and more complex datasets, learned codecs enable efficient generation through compact latent representations, but may lose details and cannot guarantee exact preservation of given conditions. Generating directly in the original data space avoids errors introduced by the codec. Recent advances in image generation demonstrate that pixel diffusion can produce high-quality samples at practical computational costs. Inspired by these advances, we conduct a preliminary study of pixel diffusion, demonstrating its potential for adaptation to motion generation. Building on these findings, we present RawMo, a framework that brings text-to-motion generation to raw motion space. Our Motion Diffusion Transformer (MiT) models local skeletal structures and whole-body dynamics, while refining motion across frames to improve temporal consistency. Working directly in raw motion space further allows our high-noise text-motion alignment loss (HiNCE) to improve text consistency without intermediate motion decoding. RawMo supports multiple motion-conditioning tasks with a single conditional model while preserving given motion conditions exactly. Experiments on HumanML3D and KIT-ML show the best text alignment among compared methods and competitive motion quality, with ablations confirming the benefits of MiT and HiNCE.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32793) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32793)

## 62. Simulator-as-Proxy World Model for Robot Manipulation

- **作者**：Tingting Liao, Mingjia Huo, Hao Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：27 pages. Project page: https://sim-pwm.pages.dev Code: https://github.com/TingtingLiao/SimPWM

Video world models for robots are increasingly conditioned on a simulated or rendered proxy of the scene, on the assumption that the video will show what the proxy shows. Testing this assumption requires a proxy whose state is explicit and can be perturbed. We present SimPWM (Simulator-as-Proxy World Model): given a calibrated camera, it represents, from the first frame alone, the robot by its 3D model and the objects the gripper may touch as 3D points; a rule-based simulator with no trained parameters moves them under the actions, and a video model renders the result. SimPWM returns the realistic video together with the simulated state. We then freeze the simulated objects while the arm moves as recorded and check whether the video still shows the object lifted. On DROID episodes from unseen setups, the answer differs for the arm and for the objects. With each of three base video models, the arm follows the proxy (robot IoU 0.75-0.78, against at most 0.69 for end-to-end models); rendering the robot alone already gives this. What the simulated objects add is the object state: against a robot-only proxy, they cut missed pick-ups from 54% to 38%, and the video follows them only with some base models: on rigid objects, freezing them lowers the lift rate of MiniMax-H3, fine-tuned on episodes whose simulation agrees with the real video, from 42% to 10%, but that of LTX-2.3, trained on the same episodes, only from 57% to 49%, as if it inferred the grasp from the arm motion; more than half of the remaining lifts of MiniMax-H3 are of cloth and rope, which the simulator carries rigidly. With any base model, the simulated state records what the simulation did; under perturbed actions, a video that disagrees with it reveals that the video ignores the simulated state.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32786) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32786)

## 63. Beyond Static Semantics: Instance-Adaptive Vision-Language Alignment for Building Damage Assessment

- **作者**：Hang Yin, Jiepan Li, Hongruixuan Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Instance-level building damage assessment (IBDA) from bi-temporal remote sensing imagery remains challenging, as the same damage grade may exhibit diverse visual manifestations across individual buildings. Natural language offers explicit descriptions of physical damage, providing complementary semantic cues beyond discrete category labels. However, existing vision--language approaches have not fully addressed how damage semantics should adapt to local bi-temporal evidence and subsequently serve as instance-specific references for severity discrimination. To bridge this gap, we propose LAMIA-IBDA, a unified vision--language framework that progressively grounds shared damage semantics in local observations and individual buildings. Specifically, Adaptive Text-Visual Alignment (ATVA) enables evidence-adaptive semantic interaction by jointly contextualizing multiple damage descriptions and bi-temporal visual features under reliability-aware attention supervision. Dynamic Instance-Prototype Grounding (DIPG) further associates these contextualized semantics with individual building regions to construct instance-adaptive prototypes for fine-grained damage classification. Instance-level description preferences additionally supervise fusion-stage attention, linking semantic adaptation with instance discrimination. Extensive experiments on xBD and BRIGHT demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across optical--optical and optical--SAR settings. The source code is publicly available https://github.com/Cutensil/LAMIA-IBDA.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32777) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32777)

## 64. MemAnchorWorld: A Sparse World Model with Horizon-Conditioned Memory Retrieval for Autonomous Driving

- **作者**：Xinyang Jia, Yuqi Zhang, Jiakai Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 4 figures, 4 tables

Sparse world models for 4D semantic occupancy forecasting represent scenes with a small set of evolving queries to efficiently predict future geometry and semantics for autonomous driving. However, existing sparse world models face a bottleneck in using historical temporal evidence for long-horizon forecasting. They rely on scene states to carry historical information implicitly, allowing errors to propagate and accumulate across successive forecasting steps and causing geometric and semantic deviations. To address this limitation, we present MemAnchorWorld, a sparse occupancy world model that explicitly retrieves relevant historical geometric and semantic evidence for 4D semantic occupancy forecasting. Inspired by the von Restorff effect, we introduce a forecast-time-conditioned hierarchical dual-path memory-anchor retrieval mechanism. Guided by the effective age and semantic compatibility of historical information, it combines query-level candidate selection with point-level memory-anchor retrieval to provide historical evidence for sparse points at different forecast times. To better use memory anchors and existing features, a point-level residual adapter jointly gated by the task and forecast time separately controls residual corrections to semantics, occupancy confidence, and spatial positions. On Occ3D-nuScenes, MemAnchorWorld achieves an average mIoU of 13.55% and an average IoU of 22.84% across the 1-3 s forecasting horizons, attaining state-of-the-art performance on both metrics.Project page: https://memanchorworld.github.io/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32745) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32745)

## 65. A Morphological Approach to Inverse Lithography

- **作者**：Gijs Bellaard, Bart M. N. Smets, Tim van der Leek et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Lithography is a critical step in the manufacturing of chips that works by projecting light through a patterned mask and subsequently focusing it onto a photosensitive layer on top of a wafer. The task of finding a mask that prints a desired pattern on the wafer is called inverse lithography technology (ILT). The design of the mask is complicated by the fact that it must satisfy a set of constraints in order to be manufacturable. In this paper we provide a mathematical characterization of these constraints. Next, we develop a scoring function that allows us to measure whether a given mask satisfies the manufacturing constraints and if not, how badly the constraints are violated. Since our scoring function is (sub-)differentiable it can be used as the regularizer in a gradient-based inverse problem solver. We show that the resulting MorphILT algorithm that we developed can generate manufacturable masks that have nearly the same print quality as the (unmanufacturable) masks obtained from unconstrained inverse lithography.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32742) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32742)

## 66. Qwen-Image-HD: Autoregressive Diffusion for High Definition Image Generation

- **作者**：Zihao Liu, Zekai Zhang, Shengming Yin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Fine-tuning a generator on a complete high-resolution canvas requires long image sequences and substantial computation, yet local regions can still exhibit unnatural repetitive stripes when training resources are limited. We introduce Qwen-Image-HD, which adapts Qwen-Image 2.0 to high-resolution synthesis through local training and blockwise autoregressive generation. Each prediction covers a target tile at 1K scale, conditioned on high-resolution upper and left neighbors and a compressed historical thumbnail. These conditions provide adjacent fine-scale structures and an overview of the scene generated so far. To integrate these complementary cues, we map both forms of history and the target to a shared canvas coordinate system to express their spatial relationships and scale differences. On Qwen-Image-Bench at 4K, Qwen-Image-HD achieves stronger overall evaluation performance than direct 4K generation with FLUX-3-Image-Klein. Compared with full-image 4K supervised fine-tuning of the same backbone, it achieves better generation quality with lower training compute and higher inference efficiency, while exhibiting fewer unnatural repetitive stripes in original-resolution comparisons. We further introduce a Wavefront-AutoRegressive inference schedule that generates tiles on the same anti-diagonal concurrently across GPUs to reduce single-image latency.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32736) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32736)

## 67. AnyPrune: Optimization-Free Pruning for Any Feedforward 3D Gaussian Splatting Pipeline

- **作者**：Nicola Leonetti, Federico Stella, Aoxiang Fan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Feedforward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) pipelines reconstruct a scene from images in a single forward pass, representing it as real-time-renderable 3D Gaussians. However, the resulting representations contain far more Gaussians than are needed to render the scene faithfully, especially when there are many input views. This drastically increases memory and storage requirements. Existing pruning methods either require costly re-optimization, which defeats the purpose of a single forward pass, or rely on simple strategies that do not exploit all the available information. We present AnyPrune, a transformer-based, optimization-free pruning module designed to work on top of any feedforward 3DGS pipeline. To remove unnecessary Gaussians, AnyPrune learns and exploits pipeline-specific redundancy patterns through a short single-GPU training on the frozen target feedforward pipeline. AnyPrune achieves better rendering quality than existing optimization-free pruning methods in most settings and generalizes to unseen datasets, as shown through extensive experiments across multiple feedforward pipelines, datasets, and input view counts. It prunes a 1.6M-Gaussian field in about $5\,\mathrm{s}$ on a consumer GPU, faster than every method that merges Gaussians or re-optimizes the field. It can also be combined with the pruning built into feedforward pipelines, pruning further at moderate ratios while preserving or improving rendering quality. Finally, it can even suggest a scene-specific pruning ratio that retains most of the quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32735) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32735)

## 68. Diving Deeper into Dense Keypoint Localization in Object Pose Estimation

- **作者**：Jiahong Chen, Jinghao Wang, Yueneng Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages, 14 figures. Accepted by TCSVT 2026

6D pose estimation of rigid objects is a fundamental and challenging task in computer vision. Recent dense semantic keypoint-based methods employ binary encoding for efficient global keypoint localization, delivering strong performance in object pose estimation. Nevertheless, the discontinuous nature of binary encoding hinders effective training at high resolutions, while its inherent discrete and quantization errors lead to significant localization uncertainty. We propose GHLAPose, a global-to-local framework that synergizes hybrid Gray-binary (HGB) encoding with local-aware refinement (LAR). Specifically, HGB encoding first alleviates discontinuity to achieve accurate initial global keypoint localization. To further address intrinsic encoding errors, the LAR module estimates uncertainty and refines keypoint localization. This synergy enables a precise global-to-local strategy by aggregating global and local features. Extensive experiments on LM, LM-O, YCB-V, and TUD-L datasets demonstrate that GHLAPose surpasses state-of-the-art methods, particularly on fine-grained metrics. On LM-O, it outperforms the second-best method by 1.4% and 1.9% on ADD(-S) 0.02d and 0.05d respectively, and on YCB-V by 1% on ADD(-S) 0.1d. The code will be available at GHLAPose.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32728) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32728)

## 69. Support2Sim: Visually Faithful Real2Sim with Stable Object Supports

- **作者**：Samarth Chopra, Ankit Prabhu, Akshayan Nagarajah et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Reconstructing a scene for physics simulation requires recovering its spatial layout and the support relationships that govern its behavior. We present Support2Sim, a Real2Sim system that reconstructs simulation-ready scenes from a single RGB image. Our design objective is to maximize source fidelity, support realization, and stability within a practical time budget for scene generation. Support2Sim infers a support graph, which a contact-guided pose initializer uses to propose poses for objects with multiple supports. The initializer generates and ranks candidates by geometric fit and source-image agreement. A coarse-to-fine refiner allocates a fixed search budget across supported objects and evaluates pose updates with physics rollouts. After an accepted update, it searches from the new pose at a finer translation and rotation scale. We benchmark our method on 50 scenes spanning four datasets. Compared with the strongest baseline, Support2Sim improves source-mask IoU after 10 seconds by 21.4 pp, realizes 87.1% versus 73.2% of intended supports, and reduces mean object drift from 79.6 to 45.3 mm. See our project page at https://support2sim.github.io for more details.

## 70. Seeing to Drive on a Single Board: Attention-Free Depth for Onboard Robot Navigation

- **作者**：Itai Mizlish, Boaz Ben-Moshe
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：13 pages, 14 figures, 5 tables. Code, models and data: https://github.com/itaim18/DANIEL

A small ground robot can carry one camera and a few watts of compute, but the depth foundation models that could turn that camera into a range sensor are vision transformers sized for GPUs. We bring one to a Raspberry Pi 5 with the Hailo-8L, an accelerator that runs every layer in integer arithmetic, attention included. Quantised for it, Depth Anything V2 loses large parts of the scene, and we measure why: at 449 tokens, 91% of softmax weights fall below one int8 step while carrying 17-33% of the attention mass. Our answer is to distil, not quantise. An attention-free ResNet-34 U-Net student of Depth Anything V2-Large (24.5M parameters) runs at 27.1 FPS on the chip, its int8 output matches its float twin (Pearson 0.99993), and on held-out scenes of its simulated yard it is the most accurate depth network on the chip (AbsRel 0.269 against 0.354-0.530). On 1,000 stereo-verified points of a real outdoor drive it is not resolvably different from the vendor's Depth Anything V2-Small build, while running faster (27.1 vs 21.6 FPS). Ranging obstacles by where they meet the floor needs only the camera height, so mapping, hybrid-A* planning and SLAM run on the Pi's own CPU. In real-time hardware-in-the-loop tests in a Gaussian-splat reconstruction of a real yard, the stack reaches the goal without contact in 7.9$\pm$1.1 of 10 scenes, against 9.2$\pm$0.4 with exact obstacles, and in 7.7$\pm$0.7 on its own pose with simulated wheel odometry (ten seeds each; 8.2$\pm$0.4 with the real Pi and Hailo-8L in the loop, five seeds). The source code, pre-trained models and data are available at https://github.com/itaim18/DANIEL.

## 71. SplatSeg3D: Efficient Zero-Shot 3D Segmentation via Point Cloud Surfelization and Splatting

- **作者**：Sandeep Inuganti, Mahdi Chamseddine, Marcel Rogge et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Open-vocabulary 3D segmentation methods rely on 2D foundation models applied to registered images captured alongside the 3D point clouds. However, such images may not be available in many cases, while direct 3D point cloud segmentation methods require semantic class supervision and are challenging to deploy in a zero-shot manner. To address these issues, we present SplatSeg3D, which segments a bare colored point cloud in an open-vocabulary manner without requiring the accompanying images, camera poses, class supervision, or instance mask proposals. To achieve this, we first surfelize the input point cloud and obtain oriented 2D Gaussians. We then render views of the scene by splatting, with a view selection strategy that maximizes scene coverage with as few views as possible. Next, we apply text-prompted segmentation on the rendered views, giving each 3D point a set of multi-view semantic cues, fused to produce a 3D segmentation of the scene. We evaluate SplatSeg3D on semantic segmentation and novel-class instance segmentation on 4 standard benchmarks: Scannet, Scannet++, Replica, and S3DIS. Our pipeline not only performs better or on-par with the state-of-the-art, but we also achieve these results efficiently across runtime and compute requirements. Code will be published.

## 72. Knocked Off Course: Tracing Uncommanded Motion from Perception to Action in Embodied VLAs

- **作者**：Dan Zlotnikov, Nadav Alali, Ohad Ben-Shahar
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) enable simultaneous processing of egocentric video and textual target descriptions to produce action commands for embodied tracking platforms. However, these platforms rarely move exactly as commanded, in part due to unexpected environmental disturbances such as wind, unleveled terrain, and road bumps. Here we study how such models handle uncommanded egomotion. First, we explore performance across diverse disturbances by benchmarking three tracking VLAs on three datasets. We find that models differ in which environmental disturbances hurt them most, but all are sensitive to sudden target loss. Tracing egomotion from pixels to emitted actions, we identify two gaps. In representation, motion is reliably recovered through explicit relations between frames, but aggressive compression of past frames weakens small-motion readouts. In action, large rotations do reach the decision state, yet the decoded direction carries only a few percent of a mirrored rotation's effect on the turn. Targeting both gaps, we fine-tune one model, Uni-NaVid, on data amounting to just 1.7% of its original training set in a ladder of analysis-motivated variants. These gain 5-22 points after target-loss without degrading clean success; after a smooth loss, the best one improves success by 72% (relative), recovering 76% of its gap to clean. We also show that targeted fine-tuning improves recovery partly via a text-mediated action dependency, without new architecture, large-scale data collection or training from scratch.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32705) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32705)

## 73. Cif-query bridging for audio-native asr transfer to visual speech recognition

- **作者**：Chang Sun, Dongliang Xie, Wanpeng Xie et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.SD, cs.CV

Audio-native automatic speech recognition (ASR) models provide strong speech-language priors, but their compact acoustic interfaces are mismatched with long, frame-level visual features. We propose CIF-Query Bridge, a lightweight connection from a Paraformer visual speech recognition (VSR) encoder to Qwen3-ASR. The existing visual continuous integrate-and-fire (CIF) predictor produces an input-dependent number of token-rate queries; a two-layer cross-attention bridge refines them against the complete frame sequence, and a linear adapter feeds the pretrained ASR projector and decoder. This design preserves access to uncompressed visual context while avoiding direct autoregressive processing of hundreds of video frames. On CMLR, the complete visual-only transfer system achieves 7.97% character error rate, compared with 11.78% for native Paraformer under the same test split and scoring protocol---a 3.81-point absolute and 32.3% relative reduction. Controlled ablations characterize bridge depth, projector initialization, adapter capacity, and CTC supervision. The results show that CIF-derived adaptive queries provide a compact interface for transferring audio-native ASR capability to VSR.

## 74. RestPose: Canonicalization for Open Object Pose Estimation

- **作者**：Sandeep Inuganti, Weihang Li, Benjamin Busam et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In object pose estimation methods, predicted poses are expressed with respect to a canonical frame, defined by a reference model of that object. In learning-based methods, an arbitrarily chosen modeling convention defines this canonical frame, which is then used to generate the ground-truth pose-annotations for training. Object pose estimation methods are therefore bound to such arbitrary conventions, restricting generalization. In this work, we propose that such a canonical frame can instead be derived for any object, and present RestPose, an approach towards open object pose estimation that does not require reference models or canonical frame conventions during training. From a single input image, we generate an object mesh and canonicalize it through a geometric and physical analysis of the object, then use foundation model-based dense matching to estimate the pose. We choose category-level benchmarks for our experiments, which contain instances across various object categories and enable us to measure the effectiveness of our canonicalization. Across HouseCat6D, NOCS, and Omni6DPose datasets, we improve on the supervised state-of-the-art methods by 18.3 points in absolute metrics and 13.8 points in scale-agnostic metrics, without requiring task-specific training. Code will be published.

## 75. Are We Rewarding the Wrong Module? Module-Specific Policy Optimization for Multimodal Reinforcement Learning

- **作者**：HuiSeong Cho, Jun Yeong Park, Yu Rang Park
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) based on Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) has been widely adopted to enhance the multimodal reasoning of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). However, existing methods share a common limitation of Module-Level Credit Entanglement, in which the vision encoder, projector, and LLM modules are jointly optimized using the same advantage signals even when perceptual correctness and final-answer correctness differ. To address this limitation, we propose Module-Specific Policy Optimization (MSPO), a framework that separates perceptual and outcome-based learning signals across model modules. Specifically, MSPO uses responses to perception checklists comprising auxiliary questions about visual information to derive an advantage for training the vision encoder. From these responses, MSPO computes the Confidence-aware Perception Reward (CaPR), using a perception sign obtained by thresholding checklist accuracy to determine reward direction and posterior confidence from a Beta distribution over latent perception accuracy to scale reward magnitude. We then derive the vision advantage through group-relative normalization of CaPR and scale it using main-task success rates. This advantage guides updates to the vision encoder, while the projector and LLM retain outcome-based GRPO updates. Extensive experiments on seven mathematical and general reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that MSPO outperforms existing RL-tuned models on average at both the 3B and 7B scales.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32687) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32687)

## 76. TokenMatte: Unlocking T2V Diffusion Priors for Pixel-Native Video Matting

- **作者**：Zanyi Wang, Dengyang Jiang, Fengfei Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：13 pages, 11 figures, 9 tables

High-resolution video matting requires accurate recovery of continuous alpha values, including fine hair, soft boundaries, and motion blur, yet dense alpha supervision remains scarce. Pretrained text-to-video diffusion transformers (DiTs) offer rich spatio-temporal priors, whereas existing generative video matting approaches typically route alpha predictions through RGB-trained VAE latents, which can attenuate fine details and introduce additional decoding overhead. We present TokenMatte, which adapts a pretrained video DiT into a single-pass, auxiliary-free RGB-to-alpha predictor with a pixel-native output interface. At its core is a single shared linear readout that maps each spatio-temporal token directly to its corresponding ($t \times p \times p$) block of alpha pixels, predicting spatial details and temporal variations within the same local video block. Tiling these blocks produces the full-resolution alpha sequence without a convolutional decoder or learned upsampling. The predicted alpha is supervised directly in pixel space, while the ground-truth alpha never enters the VAE, eliminating target-side alpha encoding and output-side VAE decoding. Leveraging the pretrained video prior, TokenMatte is trained using only existing public matting datasets, without additional data curation or auxiliary inputs at inference. Compared with a matched latent-output baseline, our pixel-native interface improves matting accuracy while achieving a 1.51$\times$ inference speedup. TokenMatte establishes a new auxiliary-free state of the art on VideoMatte, YouTubeMatte, and V-HIM60.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32675) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32675)

## 77. Text Is Just Another Shot in Vision-Language Models: Simple Probes on Frozen Encoders Do It

- **作者**：Reda Bensaid, Ismail Ben Ayed, François Leduc-Primeau et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Few-shot adaptation of vision-language models has produced increasingly elaborate methods built on CLIP and on one shared principle: the zero-shot text classifier is the anchor, and labeled images only correct it. We argue that this principle reflects CLIP's visual features rather than a property of the task. We provide a simple Bayesian interpretation, in which a class name is worth exactly kappa = sigma^2 / tau^2 labeled images, the ratio between the visual spread of a class and the misalignment of its name. The Bayes classifier of this model counts text as kappa more samples. With modern frozen encoders, few-shot adaptation then reduces to a well-regularised convex probe. Specifically, we introduce TASP, a simple convex logistic regression on frozen features that incorporates class-name embeddings as weighted training examples and as a regularization prior. On the standard 11-dataset benchmark, TASP with frozen PE-Core-L features achieves 87.1% accuracy at 1 shot and 91.4% at 16 shots, outperforming state-of-the-art few-shot vision-language adaptation methods by 4.6 to 11.0 percentage points. While stronger representations explain much of this improvement, controlled comparisons on matched features show that existing frozen-encoder adaptation methods do not materially improve on this probe. We further find that the weight assigned to text matters more than how it is incorporated, and that combining self-supervised visual features with vision-language representations is most beneficial for weaker visual encoders. Together, these findings suggest that progress in few-shot vision-language adaptation depends more on representation quality and the appropriate use of text supervision than on increasingly complex adaptation mechanisms.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32672) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32672)

## 78. Attack-Inference Aligned Universal Adversarial Attacks on Video Object Segmentation

- **作者**：Ruiqi Shen, Chang Liu, Henghui Ding
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026, Project Page: https://henghuiding.com/AUV/

Modern video object segmentation (VOS) relies on a two-stage inference paradigm: target initialization and memory-based propagation. However, existing adversarial attacks on VOS often overlook this structure. To bridge this gap, we propose AUV, an adversarial framework that Aligns with the Universal two-stage paradigm of VOS models. Specifically, AUV optimizes a universal adversarial perturbation (UAP) through decoupled training strategies tailored for both initialization and propagation stages. By disrupting the shared memory space, it achieves prompt-agnostic attacks across different modalities, including masks, points, boxes, and language. Extensive experiments show that AUV establishes state-of-the-art VOS attack performance, such as degrading mask-based SAM3 to only 8.4 J&F on DAVIS17-val (compared to 55.4 for the previous SOTA under the same training setup). It also exhibits superior temporal dynamics with the fastest onset, the most enduring persistence, and the support of any-time mid-video attacks. The AUV optimization framework is applicable to different VOS models, revealing vulnerabilities in real-world streaming VOS. Code is available at https://github.com/FudanCVL/AUV.

## 79. LaWanle: Why Can't Leading Driving Models Make a Simple Turn?

- **作者**：Chengzhi Gao, Junzhe Gao, Yongzhi Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：29 pages, 20 figures, including appendix

We were surprised to find that leading end-to-end driving models across major paradigms, despite scoring close to human drivers on NAVSIM, cannot reliably make a simple 90-degree turn in photorealistic closed-loop simulators. To investigate why, we build LaWanle, a benchmark of turns drawn from three such simulators. Driving each turn from several starts, we find that the same model passes it from one start and fails it from another, and that the two drives look alike until they enter the junction. Rotating the car by a few degrees at the junction entry can fail a turn it otherwise passes. Conversely, a slight heading correction to the plans can rescue about half of the failed turns we tested. Such errors arise when a model drives by itself, so we post-train an open-source lane-keeping model with closed-loop DAgger on its lateral path. Training 5% of its parameters is enough to raise its turn pass rate from 19% to 76%. The gain transfers to the two other benchmarks, where the policy also scores among the leading models. Further fine-tuning on recovery data repairs the deviations it is trained on but, against a control trained equally long, improves turns on none of the three benchmarks, and every variant stays far below a human driver.

## 80. ICLWorld: Benchmarking In-Context Learning of Agents from Digital to Physical Worlds

- **作者**：Qingle Liu, Jieming Li, Pei-lin Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO
- **备注**：Project page: https://iclworld.github.io Code: https://github.com/ICLWorld/ICLWorld Dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Aoraku/ICLWorld

In-context learning (ICL) lets agents acquire new rules from context and adapt to user-specific requirements at inference time. The same frontier agents now operate both computers and robots, yet their ICL remains insufficiently assessed: existing benchmarks are largely offline, rely on text or static images, and risk training-data contamination, while robot evaluations are limited to method-level ablations. We present ICLWorld, an online benchmark that evaluates the ICL of agents from digital to physical worlds, covering computer use and robot use, together with UIverse, a unified harness that turns every environment into a user interface. We build 30 environments, record 630 demonstration videos (10+ hours), and design 160 tasks with customized, often counter-intuitive rules that cannot be satisfied without learning from the context. Each task comes with three rule versions, given as text or video, and we test generalization at three levels, from replaying the demonstration to new instances and unseen environments. Across six frontier models, the strongest agents, Claude Opus 5.5 and GPT-6 Astra, succeed on 82% and 76% of computer-use runs but only 27% and 26% of robot-use runs, and a no-rule control reduces success to at most 8%. GPT-6 Astra succeeds under all three rule versions on only 38% of computer-use tasks, half its rate under a single rule, and answers 98% of robot rule questions correctly yet completes only 20% of these tasks. Frontier agents learn rules comparably well from video and from text and transfer them across environments, whereas weaker agents rely on text and lose most of their video-based success once the demonstrated situation is resampled. These findings reveal substantial gaps between the digital and the physical world and between identifying a rule and acting on it.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32649) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32649)

## 81. ReFocus: Progress-Guided Adaptive Practice for Long-Horizon Robotic Manipulation Reinforcement Learning

- **作者**：Haoyuan Li, Wei Huang, Jiehong Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：27 pages, 15 figures

Online reinforcement learning (RL) enables vision-language-action policies to improve through environmental interaction. However, in long-horizon manipulation, sparse success/failure feedback provides little information about where policies encounter bottlenecks. Moreover, repeated full-task rollouts also leave these bottlenecks weakly analyzed and insufficiently practiced. To this end, we introduce ReFocus, a progress-guided multi-round RL framework that alternates between Global and Local RL to identify task-specific bottlenecks and allocate additional practice to them. Specifically, during Global RL, we decompose each task into a Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG) of subtasks. Then a DAG-based Progress Reward is leveraged for both policy training and bottleneck diagnosis. Based on the identified bottlenecks, ReFocus selects Adaptive Practice Windows, defining task segments from an entry stage to a target stage. It allocates additional interaction to these segments during Local RL while updating the entire policy. After that, it evaluates the policy to perform the full task for the next round of RL, forming an iteration between Global and Local RL. Experiments on LIBERO-Long and RoboCasa365 show that ReFocus achieves success rates of 97.8% and 51.3%, respectively, compared with 90.6% and 39.3% for the $π_{0.5}$ baseline. Furthermore, we evaluate a real-world adaptation on three long-horizon tasks. On BreadBaker, ReFocus achieves 95% success, compared with 30% for the $π_{0.5}$. Project page: https://refocus-robotics.github.io.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32639) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32639)

## 82. HazeFlux: Few-Step Generative Priors for Real-World Image Dehazing

- **作者**：Yunong Liu, Danna Xue, Javier Vazquez-Corral
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Real-world single-image dehazing suffers from severe domain shifts, as paired real data is scarce and models trained on synthetic haze generalize poorly to real-world haze. While pre-trained generative editing models offer powerful image priors to bridge this generalization gap, applying them to image dehazing poses two major challenges: standard generative models prioritize distribution alignment over strict pixel-level fidelity, and operating in compressed latent spaces inherently discards fine-grained structural details. In this paper, we propose a novel two-stage framework that adapts the generative prior of a few-step flow-matching backbone to real-world dehazing. In the first stage, we fine-tune the latent backbone using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), supervised not only on a flow-matching objective, but also on pixel-level supervision, thus enabling direct hazy-to-clear translation. In the second stage, we introduce structure-preserving skip connections directly to the VAE decoder, bypassing latent compression artifacts to recover precise spatial edges. Experiments on four realworld benchmarks demonstrate that our approach significantly outperforms existing dehazing methods in real conditions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32632) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32632)

## 83. Completion-Aware Compression for Progressive 3D Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Reagan Koo, Yeong-Gyu Kim, Eun-Seok Ryu
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, 6 main-paper figures and 4 supplementary figures

3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables high-quality novel-view rendering, but its large scene representations are costly to transmit. Pruning and compression reduce file sizes, yet progressive delivery also requires usable reconstructions before the full scene arrives. Existing progressive representations provide partial scenes, but level-based updates are limited by predefined coding boundaries. This paper presents a progressive 3DGS codec that balances attribute fidelity with the image benefit of making Gaussians available earlier. Its single compact stream supports partial 3D reconstruction and adds newly decoded Gaussians as more bytes arrive. The stream supports fine-grained updates at arbitrary received-byte budgets, without separate encodings or later attribute refinement. At matched complete-file sizes and received bytes, completion-aware allocation improves 1/8 PSNR over attribute-only allocation by 0.48-1.43 dB. The proposed codec compresses original 3DGS representations by 62-88 times while retaining near-reference Full PSNR. Under the evaluated 50-Mbps delivery model, partial reconstruction becomes available in approximately 0.2 s.

## 84. On the Capabilities and Limitations of Zero-Shot Open-Vocabulary Cloud Segmentation

- **作者**：Jose Sosa, Thomas Gillet, Rim Sleimi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent advances in vision-language foundation models have enabled open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS) without task-specific training, offering new opportunities for Earth Observation (EO) applications. However, their capabilities for cloud detection remain insufficiently explored. In this work, we evaluate twelve OVSS approaches across six publicly available EO datasets in a zero-shot setting, i.e., without fine-tuning or adaptation to the target data. We investigate both binary cloud detection and multiclass segmentation, distinguishing between thick clouds, thin clouds, cloud shadows, and background. Our results show differences in performance across models and datasets, with SegEarth-OV3 achieving the highest binary cloud detection performance on four of the six datasets. While several approaches demonstrate promising performance in binary cloud detection, multiclass segmentation remains more challenging, particularly when distinguishing between fine-grained cloud categories. Furthermore, our controlled prompt sensitivity analysis reveals model-dependent responses to alternative textual descriptions, with appearance-oriented prompts benefiting certain approaches while degrading the performance of others. Overall, our findings demonstrate both the potential and current limitations of zero-shot OVSS for cloud detection, establishing a comparative benchmark for future research on generalisable cloud segmentation. Code will be available at: https://github.com/josesosajs/zero-shot-cloudovss.

## 85. ShapeQuest: Towards Granularity-Controlled Edit-Conditioned 3D Asset Retrieval

- **作者**：Faris Abu-Ali, Sankalp Sinha, Didier Stricker et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 5 figures

Given a reference asset and an instruction such as "remove one armrest," a retriever must find variants that make the edit and preserve the other parts. This search is well established for images but remains underexplored for individual 3D assets. To study this problem, we introduce ShapeQuest and ShapeQuester, a new benchmark and model for edit-conditioned 3D asset retrieval. ShapeQuest's open-source pipeline edits textured meshes through part additions, removals, and material and colour changes. It controls edit granularity across five levels, defined by the fraction of the object's surface area each edit affects. It constructs multiple valid targets when an instruction leaves the choice of part open. A vision-language model judges each edit from views chosen to expose the edited parts, and human raters audit its judgments. ShapeQuest has 87,824 queries in 50 categories and a 50,919-shape gallery with competing edits. To distinguish these variants, ShapeQuester learns retrieval alongside reconstruction of the edited point cloud, with part masks supervising changed and preserved regions. With a frozen pretrained point encoder, ShapeQuester reaches 50.4 operation-macro mAP@10 on the test split, against 47.2 for the fusion baseline with the same inputs and 48.6 for the strongest baseline. Reconstruction adds 6.5 points on the validation split when the point encoder is trained from scratch, including 3.3 from part masks.

## 86. VenusOCR2.0: Towards Efficient and Effective Document Parsing

- **作者**：Lei Li, Zheng Wei, Yi Yuan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We present VenusOCR2.0, a compact 1B-parameter vision-language recognizer designed for both high-accuracy and high-throughput document parsing across text, tables, and formulas. To continuously improve recognition quality without self-training collapse, we build a bounded recursive self-improvement (RSI) data engine where the model acts strictly as a weakness probe, while external multi-expert medoid consensus, VLM visual verification, and factorized synthesis supply reliable supervision across pretraining, curated SFT, and RLVR. VenusOCR2.0 couples a dynamic-resolution SigLIP2 encoder and 2x2 spatial token merging with a Qwen3-0.6B decoder, adopting compact atomic OTSL tokens to shorten table sequences and reduce structural errors. To further eliminate the autoregressive decoding bottleneck, we introduce PTPv2, a lightweight ($\sim$67M) decoupled parallel draft module that combines frozen multi-layer verifier conditioning, a standalone learned mask, and block-causal all-position next-token prediction. On OmniDocBenchv1.6, VenusOCR2.0 achieves an overall score of 96.45 and a state-of-the-art 98.57 formula CDM, substantially outperforming massive general-purpose VLMs such as Gemini-3-Pro (92.91) and Qwen3-VL-235B (89.78). Deployed in vLLM on a single H20 GPU, PTPv2 delivers a 2.96x single-request speedup and up to 2.36x higher batched throughput while guaranteeing lossless, token-for-token equivalence with standard autoregressive decoding.

## 87. FewRef-5: Fixed-Reference Test-Time Adaptation for Virtual Staining

- **作者**：Wenxuan Wang, Liwen Wang, Mingmin Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Virtual staining synthesizes special-stain images from routine hematoxylin and eosin (H&E), extending the information available from an existing digital slide. However, a fixed inference rule can leave available real target-stain references unused, limiting query-specific correction of stain appearance at deployment. Test-time adaptation (TTA) provides a mechanism for this reference-guided, query-specific refinement without retraining the generator. We therefore propose FewRef-5, a reference-assisted TTA method for virtual staining built on StainPromptInversion. We extract tissue-masked optical-density means and standard deviations from five real Masson's trichrome (MAS) training patches and average them into a fixed stain target. A scheduled statistic-matching loss introduces this target into per-query visual-prompt optimization alongside our edge/Gram consistency penalties and the inherited reconstruction objectives. The target remains unchanged across queries, avoiding recursive dependence on earlier generated outputs while the pretrained generator stays fixed. On recorded H&E-to-MAS outputs for 1,989 queries, FewRef-5 reduces target-set FID from 183.201 to 166.904 and increases source-referenced SSIM from 0.92787 to 0.94996 relative to our Edge+Gram variant. Source PSNR and MS-SSIM decrease, showing an endpoint trade-off rather than uniformly better reconstruction.

## 88. CoReQ: Condition-Aware Cross-Modal Repair and Quality-Guided Robust Collaborative Perception

- **作者**：Ziwei Guo, Lingzhi Li, Jin Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 6 figures, 4 tables

Multi-vehicle collaborative perception can use observations from different perspectives to alleviate the problems of occlusion and limited sensing range. However, missing echoes and false echoes caused by bad weather will reduce the quality of single-vehicle features and affect collaborative detection. LiDAR provides richer geometric information, and 4D Radar has strong weather adaptability. The complementarity of the two provides the possibility to improve perception under degraded observations. However, how to adjust cross-modal repair according to the current observation conditions and how to reasonably integrate multi-vehicle features whose reliability still differs after repair are still problems that need to be solved. To this end, we propose CoReQ, a condition-aware repair and quality-guided collaborative perception framework. For the former, the condition-guided bidirectional cross-modal repair module (CBCR) extracts vehicle-level observation conditions from point cloud occupancy, density and feature statistics, and adjusts the bidirectional correction of LiDAR and Radar features accordingly. For the latter, we combine local reliability assessment with collaborative weighting: first, the task-aware quality estimation module (TaQE) uses the classification and positioning information of the EMA teacher to construct a supervision target and predict the spatial quality of the repaired features; then, the condition and quality guided fusion module (CoQF) matches the quality information with the content, and the observation conditions of both parties are jointly used for multi-vehicle feature weighted fusion. Experiments on V2X-R and V2X-Radar-C show that the framework is competitive under a variety of observation conditions, achieving significant gains in AP@0.7 on V2X-R under snowy conditions, and achieving the highest AP among the compared V2X-Radar-C methods.

## 89. Sol$^{3}$: Accelerating Video Diffusion for Free via Hierarchical Sparse Attention Routing

- **作者**：Guoxin Fan, Weilun Feng, Chuanguang Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Sol-Attn (Sparsifying online attention) combines sparse exact computation with low-cost representative approximation to accelerate training-free inference in diffusion video models. However, intra-block attention can focus on only a few tokens. While preserving these key interactions exactly, the block-level decision incurs the cost of evaluating many low-response members. We propose Sol$^{3}$, a training-free hierarchical sparse attention method that further refines how exact computations are allocated. First, we introduce Gaussian-guided hierarchical routing, which determines the scope of exact computation step by step, from blocks to tokens, using statistical thresholds at each level. Only a subset of members within Mixed blocks that require refinement is selected for exact computation, while representative terms approximate the remaining members. We further design heterogeneous routing statistics that combine block-center correlations with variations in Key and Value within blocks to identify blocks that are difficult to approximate. Among these Mixed blocks, we uniformly compare raw token relevance to select members requiring exact computation. Experiments on video generation, audio-video co-generation, and world simulation tasks demonstrate that Sol$^{3}$ achieves 86% attention sparsity and delivers a $3.07\times$ attention speedup while preserving quality. Our code is available at https://github.com/FofGofx/sol3.

## 90. PartAgent: Agentic 3D Part Segmentation via Expert Integration and Closed-Loop Refinement

- **作者**：Yang Cao, Zhe Zhu, Yiheng Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://yangcaoai.github.io/PartAgent_ProjectPage/

Segmenting a 3D object into semantically meaningful parts requires joint reasoning about geometric structure and semantic context. Pretrained 3D experts provide spatially grounded region proposals, while 2D experts provide semantic cues for identifying object parts. The challenge is to integrate these complementary capabilities into a framework that can both produce meaningful part segmentations and progressively refine them. We present PartAgent, a training-free agentic framework for 3D part segmentation built upon two complementary modules: (i) Structural-Semantic Expert Integration combines regions predicted by the 3D expert with multiview semantic evidence from the 2D expert through image-to-surface correspondences, constructing compositional part partitions at multiple granularities. (ii) Observation-Driven Closed-Loop Partition Refinement observes the current partition and its supporting evidence to select targeted inspection, evidence repair, composition, or reselection actions. It verifies proposed changes and uses the updated state and action outcomes to guide subsequent decisions. On PartObjaverse-Tiny-WT, PartAgent achieves 68.41 mIoU, outperforming the strongest competing method by 8.53 points. Across 3DCoMPaT200, PartNeXt, PartNetE, and PartObjaverse-Tiny, PartAgent achieves the highest mIoU on each dataset, averaging 69.36 across the four datasets, 7.28 points above the highest mean among competing methods.

## 91. UnifiedAD: One-Shot Anomaly Detection across Modalities via Evidence Calibration

- **作者**：Shangze Li, Chuancheng Shi, Simiao Xie et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Both single-modal and multi-modal anomaly detection aim to identify deviations from normal patterns, providing a common foundation for unified detection. However, existing methods rely on modality-specific modeling and scoring, making it difficult to obtain comparable anomaly evidence with limited normal references. To that end, we propose UnifiedAD, which unifies single-modal and multi-modal anomaly detection through anomaly evidence calibration, requiring only one normal reference per target category and no gradient updates. Specifically, we first derive neuron-level discriminative priors from normal and anomalous samples in an independent auxiliary dataset and combine them with neuron response consistency under normality-preserving transformations of the target reference to select reliable neurons, termed anomaly neurons, and identify anomaly directions. Next, we construct a low-rank normal subspace for each layer and modality from the selected neurons' spatial responses to the reference and quantify query feature deviations from normal structure using projection residuals. Finally, we separately calibrate projection residuals and anomaly-direction responses using the response distributions obtained from the normal reference and its transformations, converting them into comparable anomaly evidence for aggregation across layers and modalities. Experiments on multiple anomaly detection benchmarks demonstrate that UnifiedAD achieves strong performance in both image-level anomaly detection and pixel-level anomaly localization.
