## 130. Visual-State Graph Memory for Streaming Video Understanding

- **作者**：Ailar Mahdizadeh, Pritam Sarkar, Xiangteng He et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In streaming video understanding, the evidence needed to answer a question may have already left the recent-frame window. Keeping all frames does not scale with video length, while dropping older frames limits the model's ability to reason about the past. We propose Visual-State Graph (VSG), a training-free memory that stores the state of a stream rather than the raw sequence of frames. VSG represents the stream as a graph of objects and their relations, together with a log of events; similar to a dynamically evolving scene graph. An object observed repeatedly is represented by one persistent node, with a timestamped history of how its state changes. To determine whether a newly mentioned object already exists in the graph, we compare the relations it takes part in with those of stored objects. We build this memory as the video arrives, before the questions are known. When a question arrives, we retrieve relevant objects and events alongside recent frames under a fixed input budget. Our evaluation spans four streaming-video benchmarks and seven open-source vision-language models ranging from 7B to 32B parameters. Under matched input budgets, VSG improves reasoning about the past while preserving real-time understanding.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32369) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32369)

## 131. Sub-Riemannian structure for Oriented Point Clouds

- **作者**：Thomas Besnier, Stefan Sommer
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Many frameworks dealing with 3D (oriented) point clouds define locality in the ambient Euclidean space: $k$-nearest-neighbor graphs and radius balls in $\mathbb{R}^3$ are critical to define which points are "close to one another" and should exchange information. This can unintentionally merge sampled surfaces that are close in space but geometrically distinct such as the two faces of a thin leaf. Then, features are more prone to leak across boundaries which hinder performance. To address this, we propose to define a sub-Riemannian contact metric on position-orientation space $\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{R}^3\times \mathbb{S}^2$: points are lifted to $(x,n)$ with $n$ a surface normal, and "motion" is constrained to a non-holonomic horizontal distribution in which spatial velocity must stay in the plane orthogonal to the normal which can reorient at a cost. From this, we derive an explicit score, which locally approximate the distance, to compare oriented points for downstream tasks. We report improved segmentation and correspondence accuracy near contact when using the score as a drop-in change inside PointNet++ aggregation mechanism as well as reduced leakage when using it to simulate heat diffusion on oriented points.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32361) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32361)

## 132. Characterizing Datasets to Anticipate Unsupervised Domain Adaptation Performance for Radiographic Pneumonia Detection

- **作者**：Iftakhar Hossain, Md. Imran Hasan, Salahuddin Ahmed et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, 8 figures. Submitted to IEEE Journal of Biomedical and Health Informatics

Deep learning models for pneumonia detection from chest radiographs exhibit degraded accuracy when applied across institutions, which motivates Unsupervised Domain Adaptation (UDA). We observe that UDA performance depends on the properties of both the source and target datasets, and that performance gains are asymmetric with respect to dataset pairs. We investigate which structural properties of chest X-ray datasets are the most important predictors of UDA performance, and whether they can be measured before adaptation is attempted. Five chest radiograph datasets were characterized by the class separability of their features, obtained from one forward pass of a frozen DenseNet-121 and summarized by the Silhouette Score, with the Fisher Ratio as a supporting measure. All twenty directed source-target pairs were adapted with a Domain-Adversarial Neural Network (DANN), while direct transfer without adaptation was also evaluated. Seven published transferability estimators were computed on the same representation for comparison. We found that adaptation performance depended on both endpoints in opposite directions. More separable target datasets attained higher AUC scores ($r=0.934$, $p=0.042$), whereas more separable sources produced lower AUC scores ($r=-0.922$, $p=0.017$). The signed difference in separability tracked the signed difference in AUC between the two directions of a pair ($r=-0.932$, $p=0.017$) and identified the better direction in 10 of 10 pairs ($p=0.001$). The proposed descriptor was comparable to the best published estimators on target evaluation. Source and target datasets play opposite structural roles in adaptation: higher class separability makes a dataset easier as a test set but not as a training source. This framework can guide dataset selection for adaptation in low-resource settings without running full experiments.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32360) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32360)

## 133. LookAnything: A Foundation Model for Holistic Gaze Following

- **作者**：Jiayu Tang, Yuchen Zhou, Hongsheng Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Human gaze provides a rich window into attention and intent, making its understanding essential for applications ranging from social signal analysis to human-robot interaction. Existing methods typically employ highly customized architectures relying on auxiliary inputs like head crops or depth maps, causing cascading errors and inference overhead. Trained on single domains and confined to isolated gaze following with heatmap outputs, they lack flexibility and generalization for diverse interactive scenarios. To bridge this gap, we present LookAnything, a foundation model that unifies diverse gaze-centric tasks for holistic gaze following. Benefiting from large-scale multi-task and cross-domain training, LookAnything flexibly adapts to various settings, generating diverse outputs (points, boxes, heatmaps, and language) and supporting broad gaze-centric localization, description, and reasoning. At its core, we introduce learnable gaze-prior queries to promote gaze-aware representation learning, eliminating the need for auxiliary external inputs. To facilitate this paradigm, we construct HoliGaze, a comprehensive corpus and benchmark comprising 311K question-answer pairs across 5 capability dimensions and 9 distinct tasks. Experiments demonstrate that LookAnything consistently outperforms 33 leading vision-language models on holistic gaze following, while remaining highly competitive with dedicated specialists across 5 conventional target detection benchmarks. Evaluation on unseen datasets and deployment on a real robot further validate its strong out-of-domain generalization and flexible adaptation in real-world scenarios.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32351) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32351)

## 134. LRQ-Cache: Compressing Feature Caches in Video Diffusion via Low-Rank Approximation and Quantization

- **作者**：Ruizhe Yao, Zhen Huang, Songpan Gao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video diffusion transformers (DiTs) achieve high-fidelity generation, but inference is expensive because every denoising step evaluates a large transformer over all spatiotemporal tokens of the video. Temporal caching accelerates them by reusing intermediate features across denoising steps. However, retaining dense feature tensors introduces a persistent storage cost that grows with video length, resolution, and cache coverage, reaching 109-346 GiB per video for the predictive caches we evaluate. Such states exceed the memory of a single GPU, so native caches must either be offloaded to the CPU, which slows generation, or be sharded across several GPUs, e.g., with sequence parallelism, so that the cache rather than the compute sets the number of GPUs. We instead shrink the cached state itself, exploiting its redundancy: a few channel patterns shared across tokens capture much of the energy of each cached feature. Based on this observation, we propose LRQ-Cache, a training-free method that combines low-rank approximation and quantization for feature-cache compression. LRQ-Cache represents each cached feature with a small set of factors, quantizes them before storage, and reconstructs the feature at reuse time, leaving cache mechanisms unchanged. Across three caching mechanisms on HunyuanVideo and Wan2.1, LRQ-Cache reduces the persistent state of predictive caches by 42-71x, to 2.3-4.9 GiB per video. VBench stays within 0.25 pp of the native cache for TeaCache, and the larger drops of the Wan2.1 predictive caches, up to 3.5 pp, shrink at higher ranks. As a result, predictive caches that exceed the memory of a single 80 GB H100 GPU fit on it with LRQ-Cache, making generation 2.7-5.9x faster than with CPU-offloaded native caches.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32345) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32345)

## 135. DiCT: Teaching X-ray Vision-Language Models to Reason via Imagining CT

- **作者**：Zhenghao Lu, Xiaotian Zhang, Songtao Jiang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：18 pages, 8 figures. Supplementary material included

Diagnosing with X-rays often requires reasoning about the 3D locations of abnormalities and their spatial relationships to surrounding anatomical structures, yet such reasoning remains challenging due to the loss of spatial information in 2D projections. To address this challenge, we propose Dreaming in CT (DiCT), a three-stage progressive CT-to-X-ray reasoning transfer framework that leverages a CT vision-language model (VLM) to guide an X-ray VLM. Through this CT-guided transfer, the student acquires CT-informed 3D reasoning capabilities, enabling it to reason directly from 2D X-rays without explicit CT reconstruction at inference time. The framework begins with instruct the dreamer, which equips the X-ray student with initial CT-oriented reasoning capabilities and establishes a structured interface that distinguishes X-ray observations from CT imagination. Building on this structure, visualize the dream introduces CT-guided on-policy distillation (OPD), where the CT teacher selectively guides the X-ray student's reasoning trajectories to transfer 3D anatomical knowledge. Finally, manifest the dream adapts the acquired reasoning capabilities from digitally reconstructed radiographs (DRRs) to real clinical X-rays. Experiments across two VLM backbones on 3D-RAD and ReXVQA demonstrate DiCT's effectiveness across radiological reasoning tasks, achieving a 1.66 percentage point improvement over a CT reconstruction-based baseline on 3D-RAD Abnormality Identification with 12.77$\times$ lower inference computation. Our code is available at https://github.com/HongYep/DiCT.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32344) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32344)

## 136. Part-Set Prototypes for Explainable Mountain Gazelles Sex Classification

- **作者**：Tali Boneh Shitrit, Amir Kedem, Ilan Shimshoni et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Self-explainable visual models for fine-grained recognition are often hindered by rigid geometric constraints that cannot capture diverse semantic shapes in unconstrained "in-the-wild" imagery. Real-world camera-trap data introduces challenges, including non-optimal poses, lighting variations, occlusions, animal camouflage, and environmental shortcuts. To address this, we introduce set prototypes, a general architecture where both part cardinality and unconstrained spatial layouts are mined directly from data. Our joint-matching formulation places parts simultaneously on a frozen DINOv3 token grid via truncated Sinkhorn competition, allowing unsupported parts to abstain without spatial collapse. We evaluate our approach on Mountain Gazelle sex classification, a camera-trap benchmark, where our model achieves 0.878 macro-F1--outperforming prior architectures and at par with an unconstrained linear probe. The strongest prototypes localize to distinctive biological areas such as horns, neck and body, allowing ecologists to visually verify decisions before trusting the metric. This formulation generalizes naturally to diverse ecological monitoring challenges beyond a single species.

## 137. Get the Next Moment Right: Short-Term Event Planning for Physically Plausible Video Generation

- **作者**：Hao Liu, Xiang Tang, Ruotong Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video generation models synthesize realistic imagery yet still struggle to produce physically plausible evolution. Existing methods guide generation with a motion trajectory predicted for the entire clip in advance, so prediction errors accumulate and degrade later frames. We propose STEP(Short-Term Event Planning), a training-free closed-loop framework that guides generation one short clip at a time. Each round consists of four steps: grounding measures the scene state from the latest frames, event planning predicts the next physical event and renders it into a control clip, latent-guided generation produces the next short clip under this control, and verification checks whether the event occurs and resamples the clip if it does not. Because each round is grounded in the frames actually generated, control relies only on reliable short-range predictions. STEP improves the physical plausibility of three frozen generators on Physics-IQ Verified and PhyGenBench, and can be applied to new generators without training.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32337) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32337)

## 138. GOLD-SLAM: Gaussian-splatting Omnidirectional LiDAR-Depth SLAM

- **作者**：Weicong Zhang, Yuan Huang, Yu Zheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Panoramic cameras capture nearly the entire spherical field of view in a single frame. However, the mismatch between this panoramic coverage and the field of view of LiDAR poses a substantial challenge for panoramic Gaussian splatting SLAM that relies on LiDAR depth priors. We introduce GOLD-SLAM, a Gaussian-splatting Omnidirectional LiDAR-Depth SLAM system that combines high-quality rendering with accurate geometric reconstruction. It performs tracking and mapping in the spherical domain and uses tracked visual features in regions without LiDAR coverage to improve pose estimation. To address insufficient coverage of newly observed surfaces and floating artifacts during incremental mapping, we introduce Depth-Aware Map Extension and Pruning (DAEP). To enable broader evaluation, we collect a new dataset, WHOLE, using a UAV platform across Weak-geometry, High-dynamic, Omnidirectional, Low-light, and Easy scenarios. Extensive experiments on public benchmarks and WHOLE demonstrate competitive rendering quality and improved geometric accuracy relative to perspective systems. Compared with existing panoramic baselines, GOLD-SLAM improves mean PSNR by 5.56-9.10 dB and reduces mean Depth-L1 by 71.9-83.9%. To assess deployment feasibility on resource-constrained platforms such as UAVs, we evaluate an embedded configuration of GOLD-SLAM on a Jetson Orin NX (16 GB), demonstrating online operation at the cost of reconstruction quality.

## 139. GUI-WEdit and GUI-SkillEvo: Gradient-Free Adaptation of Small GUI Agents

- **作者**：Haixia Bi, Yingtian Chen, Sihan Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Small graphical user interface (GUI) agents can execute desktop workflows, but repeated local errors and fragile procedural decisions limit their reliability. We present GUI-WEdit-4B and GUI-SkillEvo-2B, two gradient-free adaptations of GUI-Owl-1.5 backbones. For the 4B model, we use trajectory feedback to construct guarded, closed-form updates to a late multilayer perceptron (MLP), retaining successful states as protection constraints. The final checkpoint changes 23,739,095 parameter values, or 0.535\% of the 4,437,815,808-parameter backbone, within one weight tensor. For the 2B model, an assistant iteratively proposes and refines procedural skills while the model weights remain fixed. On the 361-task OSWorld subset excluding Google Drive tasks, the frozen systems obtain final aggregate scores of 48.02\% and 45.47\%, respectively.

## 140. Cross-Version Compatibility of TESSERA Embeddings Through Lightweight Translation

- **作者**：Yutong Yang, Zhengpeng Feng
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：5 pages, 2 figures

Downstream models often outlive the embedding release on which they were trained. We test whether lightweight converters let a classifier trained on one Tessera release use embeddings from another. We fit converters between v1, v1.1, and v2 on 6,280 co-located triplets from 25 sites in 2024, with disjoint training, validation, and test sites. On a four-class land-cover test, a classifier trained on true v2 reaches 0.937 accuracy and 0.844 macro-F1. The same classifier scores 0.061 and 0.050 on raw v1.1, but 0.917 and 0.795 on converted v1.1. Direct v1-to-v2 translation has lower error than composition through v1.1. Geographic transfer is less reliable. On a separate cohort of 20 sites, a converter trained on the original sites reaches 0.597 accuracy despite 0.902 cosine similarity to v2, whereas a converter trained within the new cohort reaches 0.784. Tests with 2021 WorldCover labels and at 24 sampled sites without valid native v2 values extend the evaluation. Conversion recovers much of the frozen classifier's performance on the tested sites, but the new-site result shows that embedding similarity cannot stand in for downstream evaluation.

## 141. FlowVecFont: Scalable Vector Font Generation via Latent Flow Matching

- **作者**：Takumu Fujioka, Gouhei Tanaka
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：20 pages, 17 figures, including supplementary material

Fonts are natively represented as vector outlines. Many existing studies on vector font generation have relied on specialized generation or refinement mechanisms, bitmap representations, or externally pretrained models to address the challenges of learning vector representations. These approaches often increase system complexity, incur additional computational and implementation costs, and introduce dependencies on external resources. We show that scaling a simple model enables high-quality pure vector font generation without external pretraining. We propose FlowVecFont, a scalable model for vector font generation based on latent flow matching and trained solely on vector outlines. A Transformer-based variational autoencoder maps glyph sequences into fixed-length continuous latent tokens, enabling standard conditional flow matching for vector glyph generation. The model supports conditioning on class labels or reference glyphs, with the latter enabling generalization to unseen characters and fonts. Experiments on 5M glyphs collected across Google Fonts, including non-Latin characters, show that generation quality improves with model scale across conditioning strategies. In a comparison on Latin glyphs, the 800M-parameter FlowVecFont outperforms DeepVecFont-v2 and DesigNet and achieves quality competitive with the pretrained 27B-parameter VecGlypher. We also provide TorchFont, a machine learning library for vector fonts.

## 142. Longitudinal Characterization of Personalized Hydration Dynamics Using a Facial-Video Digital Hydration Biomarker

- **作者**：Janindu Athukorala, D. M. J. Prathapa, Thanveer Ahamed et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, q-bio.QM
- **备注**：18 pages, 8 figures, 5 tables

Background. Hydration is often assessed at a single time point despite continuous changes in body water. A facial-video biomarker has tracked hydration-state transitions, but its longitudinal behavior is unclear. Objective. To assess within-person hydration score changes, individual differences in fluid response, and implications for personal calibration. Methods. Five adults completed water and electrolyte days with an identical 2.0 L fluid schedule, using facial videos every 30 minutes over 12 hours (250 videos). On a separate morning, each completed a 500 mL electrolyte challenge with baseline and four follow-up videos over 2 hours (25 videos). Analyses assessed trajectories, post-drink changes, variability, time at or above score 50, and exploratory effects of starting score and beverage day. Results. The first scan was the day's lowest on 7 of 10 days. Across 50 intakes, the mean post-drink change was +4.0 points, exceeding a shuffled-time comparison (p less than 0.001). For 21 intakes starting below 50, no subsequent score fell; the mean rise was +11.3 points versus +5.7 expected under permutation (p less than 0.001). Intakes starting at 50 or above showed no rise beyond chance. After 500 mL electrolytes, mean change over 2 hours was positive for all five participants, with peak increases of 7.2 to 22.6 points at 30 to 90 minutes. Despite identical fluid schedules, time at or above 50 ranged from 16% to 100%. The electrolyte day showed a greater mean increase from morning baseline than the water day (+7.7 points), although day order was fixed. Conclusion. Facial-video hydration scores showed longitudinal changes associated with drinking and marked individual differences. These exploratory findings support further research on personal calibration and hydration protocols in larger controlled studies.

## 143. A recursive self-improvement framework for electron microscopy analysis

- **作者**：Yaotian Yang, Sijia Tao, Yuping Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cond-mat.mtrl-sci, cs.AI

Resolving local atomic structures such as defects and interfaces from electron microscopy contrast relies heavily on researcher judgement, leading to low efficiency and subjective standards. Here we present PhyEvo-Mic-RSI, a physics-guided and expert-informed recursive self-improvement framework that turns one-off human corrections into reusable, verifiable capabilities by updating executable operators, parameters and routing rules with versioned inheritance. Given researcher-defined tasks, the system selects analysis paths from image features without expert labels, while testing and inheriting code updates under fixed budgets. Combining physical priors with expert feedback improves atomic point-defect detection over larger models and raises cross-material interface coverage from 5.13% to 72.11% within a 10% area budget. Adapting the framework to micrographs from unseen studies further separates touching nanoparticles by aligning optimization with particle-level measurements. This work moves computational microscopy beyond standalone models and static scripts towards self-evolving platforms that accumulate human scientific expertise

## 144. ASTERISM: Do Multimodal LLMs Actually Look at All the Images?

- **作者**：Chanseul Cho, Dongwoo Kim, Noori Bae et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.LG
- **备注**：Code and datasets: https://github.com/chanseul01/ASTERISM

Multi-image benchmarks aim to test whether a multimodal large language model (MLLM) combines evidence across images, but many of their items can be answered from a single image. Across about 33k items from 19 public benchmarks, our audit and a two-stage single-image filter find that one image or the question alone settles 24.2% of them. On these items, MLLMs recover from one image a median 78% of the accuracy that all images add over the text alone, so these items reward answering from one image, which we call the single-image shortcut. MLLMs take this shortcut even on items where one image is not enough. On such items, most of their wrong answers repeat an answer they give from one image, at a rate well above chance. We build ASTERISM, to our knowledge the largest audited multi-image benchmark, from these 19 benchmarks by keeping their items that need more than one image. Its 18.7k items, 2.0k of them corrected or rewritten, share one format, one scorer and seven task categories. We also release ASTERISM-Train with 49k training items and ASTERISM-Lite, a hard subset whose items we manually verify one by one. On ASTERISM-Lite, the strongest proprietary MLLM scores 68%. Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), now common in MLLM post-training, rewards the shortcut as well, because a correct answer read off one image earns as much reward as one that combines images. In line with this, existing RLVR objectives add little to the ASTERISM score. To stop rewarding the shortcut in training as ASTERISM does in evaluation, we propose cross-image credit assignment (CICA), an extension of RLVR that moves the credit toward the tokens that need more than one image. On three open-source MLLMs, CICA raises the ASTERISM score by 2.6 to 6.0 points, more than twice the gain of each general and vision-specific RL objective trained on the same data.

## 145. SynAVID: A Synthetic Audio-Visual Immersive Dataset of Dynamic Scenes

- **作者**：Jun Xu, Sujing Li, Chunhao Bi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.SD

Immersive scene modeling requires synchronized visual and auditory observations under changing viewpoints and scene conditions. Obtaining such observations and references for controlled scene changes remains challenging. We introduce SynAVID, a synthetic audio-visual immersive dataset comprising eight indoor and outdoor scenes. Each minute-scale episode combines fixed camera arrays and moving trajectories with synchronized, viewpoint-dependent binaural audio. Shared scene dynamics, sound-source events, and observer poses drive visual rendering and geometry-aware acoustic synthesis. We evaluate existing methods for visual reconstruction, binaural audio reconstruction, and audiovisual editing. Mixed-observation configurations generally improve visual reconstruction on fixed and moving test views, while transiently occupied and sparsely observed regions remain challenging. Methods rank differently on spectral, interaural, and perceptual audio metrics. Geometric and instance annotations expose uneven observation support and local Gaussian representations beyond aggregate scores. Paired references assess target edits and surrounding content, while controlled geometry changes produce listener-dependent acoustic responses. The project page is available at https://avery-xu.github.io/synavid-project/.

## 146. CREDIT: Compositional Reliability Learning for Noisy Composed Image Retrieval

- **作者**：Tiancai Zhou, Yongxiang Li, Suimei Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Submitted to CVPR 2027

Composed Image Retrieval (CIR) aims to retrieve a target image that satisfies user intent based on a multimodal query consisting of a reference image and a modification text. However, real-world training data are often corrupted by Noisy Triplet Correspondence (NTC), where the semantic correspondence among the reference image, modification text, and target image becomes unreliable. Existing methods implicitly assume that the correctness of triplet correspondence can be determined by fixed decision rules. However, under NTC, the authenticity of semantic correspondence is difficult to determine accurately, and different triplets exhibit varying degrees of semantic reliability rather than binary correctness. Consequently, deterministic confidence is insufficient to faithfully characterize correspondence reliability, often leading to overconfident supervision for unreliable triplets and progressively reinforcing noisy semantics during compositional representation learning. To address this challenge, we propose a Compositional Reliability learning framework with Evidential uncertainty and DiffusIon-guided refinemenT (CREDIT), which explicitly quantifies the uncertainty of triplet correspondence to estimate its reliability and suppress the propagation of noisy supervision. CREDIT consists of two complementary components: The Evidential Compositional Uncertainty Learning (ECUL) module explicitly models correspondence uncertainty via evidential learning, enabling uncertaintyaware reliability estimation to suppress noisy supervision. Building upon the reliable supervision provided by ECUL, the Diffusion-enhanced Reference Refinement (DRR) module further refines reference representations through textconditioned diffusion, producing more robust compositional query features. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CREDIT consistently outperforms existing methods under various settings.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32310) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32310)

## 147. Query-Guided Visual Minimization for Selective Disclosure to Cloud VLMs

- **作者**：Pedram Zaree, Kleomenis Katevas, Vandana Rajan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CR

Cloud-hosted vision-language models (VLMs) require sending images beyond the user's local trust boundary, potentially exposing sensitive information the query does not need. We study query-guided visual minimization: a trusted local transformation preserves query-relevant visual content and reduces unnecessary disclosure before transmission. Unlike query-agnostic anonymization, it preserves the same sensitive content when the query requires it and suppresses it otherwise. We introduce QGVM, a framework that combines query grounding with an independent sensitive-item inventory, determines disclosure necessity at the type, instance, and text-content levels, and renders a minimized image for a black-box downstream VLM. We evaluate QGVM on complementary benchmarks measuring task utility, directly recoverable sensitive information, and inferential geolocation leakage. With OpenAI GPT-6.1 Sol, QGVM achieves 85.3% DoxBench answer correctness versus 77.5% for PGP, while a separately prompted Sol geolocation attacker succeeds within 1 km on 5.8% of QGVM images versus 25.7% of unprotected images. On Scene-Privacy, QGVM reduces face re-identification from 27.7% (PGP) to 4.5%. On held-out BIV-Priv-Seg, unnecessary private-text recovery falls from 33.9% (PGP) to 15.3%. To verify that disclosure is query-dependent, we evaluate paired queries that either require or do not require the same sensitive information. QGVM achieves 69.6% correct disclosure switching, compared with 29.1% for grounding-only cropping. These findings highlight the value of distinguishing visual relevance from disclosure necessity, while revealing the privacy-utility trade-offs of selective visual disclosure.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32302) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32302)

## 148. Towards a Multimodal Aesthetic Agent for Photographic Reference Grading

- **作者**：Kunjie Lin, Haitao Zhang, Bosheng Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Reference-guided photographic color grading helps users realize their aesthetic intent by selectively drawing on the color and tonal characteristics of a reference photograph. Doing so requires understanding which characteristics users wish to adopt and translating these preferences into controllable edits. However, image-only reference methods align overall appearance without instruction-grounded selection, while pixel-space generative editors typically produce flattened images without editable parameters, limiting subsequent refinement. To address these challenges, we introduce JarvisAura, a multimodal aesthetic agent that formulates photographic color grading as structured decision-making in an editable parameter space. JarvisAura offers three key advantages: (1) an aesthetic agent paradigm that integrates visual attribute perception, strategy-level reasoning, and parameterized execution for interpretable and controllable photographic color grading; (2) a unified reasoning-to-execution pipeline that connects aesthetic planning with backend rendering and editable parameter refinement; and (3) a three-stage training strategy comprising Parameter-Grounded Continual Pre-Training for parameter-appearance grounding, Reference-Grounded Supervised Fine-Tuning for reference-guided reasoning and parameter decisions, and Reference-Aligned Group Relative Policy Optimization for rendered-outcome refinement with multidimensional rewards and dynamic data derivation. On Ref-Bench, JarvisAura achieves the best scores among methods with results across reported automatic metrics and a 74% human-rated task-success rate, exceeding GPT-Image-2.5 by 9 percentage points.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32299) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32299)

## 149. MaskedVGGT: Masked Multi-View Transformers Are Scalable 3D Learners

- **作者**：Zichen Zou, Xiaosong Jia, Wen-Ming Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 9 figures, 7 tables. Code is available at https://github.com/zzctmd/MaskedVGGT

Multi-view feed-forward models show strong scalability, but their reliance on costly geometric annotations limits data scaling. Existing 3D self-supervised approaches reduce this reliance through cross-view completion (e.g., CroCo, MuM, Muskie) or novel-view synthesis (e.g., RayZer, E-RayZer), typically learning image representations and cross-view interactions jointly. We argue that learning single image representations from scratch is unnecessary for 3D pretraining methods, as doing so fails to benefit from the continuing advances in 2D foundation models (e.g., DINO, CLIP, frontier VLMs). We introduce MaskedVGGT, which freezes a strong pretrained 2D encoder and pretrains only an added, randomly initialized multi-view transformer, focusing on learning interactions across views. MaskedVGGT pretrains the multi-view transformer through masked self-distillation without annotations: a student predicts targets at masked locations based on complete anchor views and heavily masked remaining views, while an exponential-moving-average teacher provides prediction targets based on unmasked inputs. In experiments across model sizes up to 1B parameters, MaskedVGGT pretraining followed by fine-tuning outperforms single-stage supervised VGGT-$Ω$ training with the same training data, model architecture, and total number of optimization updates. MaskedVGGT also outperforms E-RayZer, CroCo, MuM, and Muskie, providing evidence for the effectiveness of building multi-view pretraining on strong 2D representations. Finally, extending pretraining with additional unlabeled videos further improves generalization, and the learned representations transfer to diverse downstream tasks. These results demonstrate an effective division of learning: reuse strong image representations from 2D pretraining and learn cross-view interactions from unlabeled multi-view data without relying on geometric annotations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32297) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32297)

## 150. $ε$-Seg++: Ensembling Across Hierarchy and Training Time for Label-Efficient EM Segmentation

- **作者**：Edoardo Giacomello, Sheida Rahnamai Kordasiabi, Mehdi Seifi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：23 pages, 9 figures, 10 tables. Edoardo Giacomello and Sheida Rahnamai Kordasiabi contributed equally and are listed in alphabetical order

Semantic segmentation of electron microscopy (EM) volumes is a central step in quantitative cell biology, but dense voxel-wise annotation is prohibitively expensive. $ε$-Seg showed that a hierarchical variational autoencoder trained on sparsely annotated voxels can yield competitive segmentations, but it classifies from its topmost latent level only and operates exclusively on 2D patches. We introduce $ε$-Seg++, which places a semantic expert at every level of the hierarchy and consolidates their predictions with a parameter-free product of experts. We further enable 3D inputs through a leakage-free sub-volume splitting protocol, which allows 2D and 3D models to be compared under identical sparse supervision. Because validation labels are just as scarce as training labels, we treat checkpoint selection as part of the method and average the top-ranked checkpoints, a second ensemble, this time across training time. On the BetaSeg FIB-SEM dataset, the multi-level experts improve 2D mean Dice by about 0.1 at 2,500 training labels. With up to 600 training labels, the 2D model matches or exceeds 3D $ε$-Seg at about one eleventh of its prediction cost. Above roughly a thousand labels, 3D inputs dominate, and at matched annotation cost 3D $ε$-Seg++ reaches a mean Dice of 0.905 versus 0.880 for D3PM, while predicting between 5.4 and 27 times faster (with our checkpoint ensemble or a single model, respectively). Checkpoint ensembling matches single-best selection on average and markedly improves the worst case when the validation set is small.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32290) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32290)

## 151. SofaSpace: A Benchmark for Executable Visual Spatial Reasoning in Continuous Motion and Environment Construction

- **作者**：Ze Rong, Kun Shi, Zhedong Zheng
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, including supplementary material

A rigid body can fit at its start and goal yet collide with a wall as it moves between them. Inspired by the moving sofa problem, we introduce SofaSpace, a benchmark for visual spatial reasoning through continuous planar rigid-body transport and environment construction. SofaSpace contains 3,000 scenes organized around three challenges: geometric compatibility, nonlocal maneuver planning, and interactive and temporal coordination. Four task views ask models to plan a motion, reconstruct a layout, design an environment that meets transport requirements, or jointly generate a layout and a motion. A separate set of 534 SofaSpace-Hard tasks tests planning and judgment. A geometric verifier checks the entire body throughout each submitted motion. On static planning tasks, endpoint agreement and continuous validity differ substantially within the same submitted plans: Claude drops from 97.2% to 52.8%, DeepSeek from 100.0% to 30.6%, and Kimi from 97.2% to 29.4%, while the strongest configuration retains 98.3% full validity. Environment construction reveals a further gap. Of 295 feasible layouts generated by the strongest configuration, 181 satisfy all evaluated functional requirements; for Kimi, one of 172 does. These results show how executable checks distinguish endpoint agreement from valid motion and geometric feasibility from functional compliance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32285) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32285)

## 152. StreamCoach: Proactive Sports Coaching via Continuous Visual Reasoning

- **作者**：Xiaoye Liang, Yiming Huang, Jialu Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：19 pages, 6 figures

In fitness and sports training, timely action quality assessment (AQA) is essential for learners to identify and correct technical errors during movement execution. However, most AQA methods assess completed performances, limiting their ability to provide timely feedback. Streaming methods alleviate this latency, but limited reasoning hinders their use of subtle movement cues and sport-specific knowledge. Moreover, their fragmented feedback focuses on one body part at a time, overlooking movement details of other body parts and transitions between action phases. To address these limitations, we propose StreamCoach, a streaming AQA framework under the OnCueCoaching paradigm that couples continuous chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning with proactive, evidence-gated coaching. Technique-Aware Reasoning tracks body-part movements and action-phase transitions against sport-specific criteria, while Holistic Coaching integrates accumulated evidence to deliver timely, comprehensive feedback on strengths and technical errors. To support training and benchmarking, we construct EE4D-Stream from 1,213 sports video sequences in Ego-Exo4D through LLM-assisted reannotation and manual review of selected claims, pairing dense CoT annotations with evidence-gated speech content. Building on this dataset, we train StreamCoach using Temporal-Semantic Alignment Reinforcement Learning to jointly optimize feedback timing and content. We benchmark 11 offline and streaming baselines on EE4D-Stream, including three proprietary models. StreamCoach achieves state-of-the-art results across intervention timing, action assessment, and coaching quality, surpassing both GPT-6.1 Sol and Claude Opus 5.5.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32281) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32281)

## 153. CaveBench: Benchmarking Abductive Physical Reasoning from Internal Observations

- **作者**：Guanxin Lin, Chen Yang, Shenxiang Zeng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：50 pages, 47 figures, 13 tables, including the appendix. Guanxin Lin and Chen Yang contributed equally. Chen Wang is the corresponding author. Project page: https://pier-lab.github.io/CaveBench/

Vision-language models (VLMs) are advancing in physical reasoning, but whether they can understand external physical drivers from internal observations remains an open question. Existing benchmarks have not specifically targeted this setting. To this end, we introduce CaveBench, a benchmark for abductive physical reasoning from internal observations. Models receive apparatus descriptions and internal images while the external controls remain hidden, and must infer the operations responsible for the observed responses. CaveBench spans 20 apparatus scenes in five physical domains and contains 10,200 simulated episodes, split into 9,000 training, 1,000 validation, and 200 test episodes. Questions span one to six events and require the complete control set at every event, testing whether an explanation accounts for the entire process. Synchronized observations and control records provide the inputs and reference answers. Evaluating 27 VLM configurations yields six-event exact-match accuracies of 68.0% for the strongest closed-source model and 12.5% for the strongest open-weight model. Seven adaptation baselines show that single-event gains need not improve complete sequences. Error analysis identifies failures in visual identification, physical inference, evidence integration, and action composition, specifying where current models struggle to connect internal phenomena with their external causes.

## 154. Can You Find the Edit When Nothing Looks Wrong? A Paired Benchmark for Text-Driven Image Manipulation Localization Beyond Semantic Abnormality

- **作者**：Peiyu Zhuang, Jianquan Yang, Zhuoying Cai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Instruction-driven image editing can alter a photograph while keeping the result coherent with the scene. Existing benchmarks lack matched plausible and anomalous edits of the same target, making it difficult to measure how localization performance changes with semantic plausibility. We construct a paired benchmark by generating semantically plausible and anomalous replacements of the same target in each source photograph. Both edits use the same editor, language, and generation settings. We estimate a separate mask for each output and retain pairs with sufficient mask overlap, enabling direct comparison of localization performance under the two semantic conditions. The release contains 47,747 bilingual triplets from 6,500 photographs, spanning four editors, three source domains, and six intended violation types. Across nine public localizers, editor-averaged IoU is lower on plausible edits in 26 of 27 model-dataset comparisons. This gap persists under pixel-change controls and region-size analyses. These findings reveal a semantic-plausibility shortcut: localization often weakens when an edited result looks ordinary. The benchmark provides a controlled setting for measuring this gap and developing localizers that remain effective on plausible edits.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32242) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32242)

## 155. Motion Chain of Thought: Disentangling Motion Semantics from Visual Appearance for Robotic Manipulation

- **作者**：Xiangkai Ma, Xinyi Wang, Junjie Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

World Action Models (WAMs) aim to construct a unified architecture capable of understanding world state evolution and guiding action generation. However, existing visual branches focus on predicting static visual observation, rather than reflecting potential transition information that captures the evolution of world states under motion interactions. This leads to representational entanglement between high-level physical condition evolution and low-level action trajectory generation within the Action Model, creating a structural bottleneck while weakening the predictive capability of world evolution modeling for action generation. We propose PILOT (Physical Inference for Latent Optimized Trajectories), whose core Representational Deduction (RD) bridges this gap by integrating motion chain-of-thought (CoT) guidance as a native model capability. Specifically, RD aims to encourage the action branch to explicitly model potential state transition tokens, which are retained as CoT in the reasoning space to guide fine-grained motion trajectory. Experiments demonstrate that RD not only improves the success rate and generalization ability of WAMs in complex robotic manipulation tasks but also enhances the model's physical interpretability by decoupling high-level motion semantics from low-level trajectory details. Furthermore, the abundant state transition supervision signals introduced by RD effectively alleviate the sparse supervision in action generation, supporting data-efficient real-robot adaptation with reduced demonstration sets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32241) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32241)

## 156. MYSTIQUE: Few-Shot Facial Identity Transfer with Performance Preservation

- **作者**：Shihan Cheng, David Hyde, Junting Deng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR, cs.LG

In visual effects, identity transfer replaces the identity in a driving image with that of a target person, while preserving the expression, lighting, pose and scene of that image. Paired ground truth is rarely available, so many methods are trained by self-reconstruction, where the model can learn a shortcut by copying identity cues from the driving image. This shortcut causes identity leakage at inference, where the driving image shows a different person. Our key observation is that leakage can enter through any input that reads the driving image. We therefore present MYSTIQUE, a diffusion-based identity transfer method that takes identity from a set of the target's photos and reads expression, lighting and scene through separate streams. It learns to disentangle identity from the other streams through multi-stage training, with disentanglement strategies at their inputs: a random block mask whose boundaries do not follow the face outline, self-augmentation, which randomly changes the face shape in the expression input and keeps its expression, and face randomization, which replaces the person in the expression and lighting inputs while the ground truth stays the driving image. At inference, we also warp the lighting input onto the face shape of a first pass, which reduces face-shape leakage through the lighting stream. Since face-recognition scores and human judgments each have limits, we evaluate identity with both, together with face-shape metrics on rendered faces of known shape. In a user study, raters judge MYSTIQUE's results more like the target than those of each compared method. Among these methods, MYSTIQUE also recovers the target's face shape most accurately, and its recognition score gains the most as more photos of the target are given.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32232) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32232)

## 157. ST-SfM: Spatio-Temporal Structure-from-Motion for Initialising 4D Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Harshit, Sami Brandt
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：9 pages, 5 figures

Dynamic Gaussian splatting is usually initialised either with random points or with a structure-from-motion (SfM) cloud of the first frame. Neither says anything about what moves, where and when. We introduce ST-SfM, a sparse spatio-temporal SfM for synchronised, calibrated multi-camera video that splits the scene into two branches. Static structure is detected once per camera on a temporal-median image, gated for temporal stability and matched along epi-polar lines. Motion is detected as space-time interest points (STIPs) in the video volume, described with HOG3D and matched only between candidates that agree both epipolarly and in time. Union-find tracks that refuse inconsistent merges are triangulated with RANSAC over all view pairs, giving static 3D points and dynamic 4D points (x, y, z, t) with sub-frame times. We use ST-SfM derived sparse 4D cloud to initialise 4D Gaussian splatting. In comparison with random initialisation on N3DV scenes we get improvements in PSNR, SSIM and LPIPS.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32231) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32231)

## 158. Relational Bottleneck Models

- **作者**：Shin'ya Yamaguchi, Kosuke Nishida
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：14 pages, 8 figures

Achieving both interpretable and high-performance inference is a key challenge in deep learning. While Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) provide inherent interpretability, they are constrained by task-specific training and rely on isolated visual concepts that fail to capture higher-order visual semantic relationships. Conversely, Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs) demonstrate zero-shot capabilities through Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning. However, standard CoT lacks a strict causal dependency between the generated rationale and the final output, compromising faithfulness. To bridge this gap, we propose Relational Bottleneck Models (RBMs), a novel training-free framework that transforms any off-the-shelf LVLMs into inherently interpretable models in a plug-and-play manner. RBMs consist of two stages: (i) Structured Rationale Generation (SRG), which explicitly models semantic interactions between visual concepts as subject-predicate-object triplets, and (ii) Rationale-Grounded Decoding (RGD), which enforces a strict causal dependency between the rationales and the final output. RGD aggregates rationale-conditional next-token distributions via an importance-weighted product-of-experts strategy, strictly excluding the image inputs from the decoding context. Extensive zero-shot evaluations across diverse benchmarks and LVLM backbones demonstrate that RBMs achieve superior interpretability while improving task performance over CBM baselines, entirely without task-specific fine-tuning.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32227) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32227)

## 159. Relation Before Action: Explicit Relation Modeling for Latent Action Pretraining

- **作者**：Boning Zhang, Junyu Gao, Changsheng Xu
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Learning latent actions from unlabeled video sequences offers a promising way to acquire reusable action representations without relying on embodiment-specific control annotations. However, existing latent-action pretraining approaches commonly couple the modeling of visual transitions with action inference, directly mapping paired observations or derived transition cues into latent action codes. This formulation leaves two distinct tasks entangled: characterizing the relational structure of an observed transition and abstracting it into a compact action representation. We propose RELACT, a relation-first framework that explicitly separates transition relation modeling from predictive action abstraction. RELACT first constructs a structured relation representation $R$ that captures how visual elements and their interactions evolve across observations. It then infers a compact, state-conditioned stochastic latent action $U$, whose predictive effects are learned through action-conditioned latent dynamics. The resulting action representation can be grounded in native control spaces for model-based planning or used to supervise downstream embodied policies. Extensive experiments across in-domain and out-of-domain image-goal navigation, goal-conditioned manipulation, and downstream policy adaptation demonstrate improved predictive action representations and consistent gains in planning and control, supporting the effectiveness and generality of relation-first latent action pretraining.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32226) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32226)

## 160. FaceNet versus VGG Features in Wasserstein Distortion for Face Image Compression

- **作者**：Xinyu Wang, Jona Ballé
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：6 pages, 2 figures

Wasserstein distortion (WD) compares local statistics of deep features and has been used to optimize learned image codecs for perceptual quality, but existing WD objectives rely on features from networks trained for generic image classification, such as VGG. We ask whether features from a face recognition network better reflect the perceived quality of compressed faces. In the Cool-Chic codec, we replace the VGG features in a WD-based objective with intermediate feature maps of FaceNet, and compare the two objectives on 20 LFW face images at a matched average bitrate of about 0.15 bits per pixel. In a preliminary pairwise human study, FaceNet-based reconstructions were preferred in 72.5% of judgments and by a majority of participants on 16 of the 20 images. These results suggest that the choice of feature network inside WD affects the perceived quality of face images, and that face recognition features are a promising direction for face-aware perceptual compression.

## 161. NativeDrive: Native Multimodal Foundations for Autonomous Driving

- **作者**：Zehua Fan, Qi Jiang, Wei Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 8 figures, 7 tables, including supplementary material

Autonomous driving requires semantic understanding and fine-grained scene perception for precise action prediction. Vision-language-action models (VLAs) inherit semantic priors, but low-dimensional action labels provide limited structural supervision. Some studies extend VLAs with visual generation tasks or adopt video-based world-action models (WAMs), but both approaches introduce appearance-reconstruction overhead, which is less relevant to planning. Native multimodal models offer an alternative to unify understanding and generation within one backbone. On this basis, we propose NativeDrive, a native encoder-free multimodal driving framework adapting the unified backbone for policy learning and introduce a dense supervision task tailored to driving. The Native Action Interface reuses the understanding expert's token-prediction interface for the discrete policy and adapts the generation expert for continuous policy. Driving Semantic Editing reuses the generation expert's editing capability for dense scene-structure supervision during training, while inference runs only the selected planning policy. NativeDrive achieves competitive planning performance among unified understanding-and-generation models on NAVSIM v1 and v2, with ablations validating its components' effectiveness. Code will be released.

## 162. EvoSearch: Self-Evolving Active Perception for Wide-Area Geospatial Search

- **作者**：Zhenghao Xie, Jing Xiao, Zihao Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Geospatial search is fundamental to resource surveys and environmental monitoring, requiring the discovery of relevant evidence across large geographic areas and multiple spatial scales. Existing active perception methods adaptively select observation locations and scales to improve search efficiency, yet their search trajectories become increasingly long and costly as the search area expands. A key limitation is that final-answer correctness alone provides little guidance on which observations to retain and when to stop, hindering efficient wide-area search. To address this challenge, we propose EvoSearch, a self-evolving active perception framework that co-evolves the perception policy and exploration program to compress search trajectories while preserving answer quality. Specifically, quality-cost comparisons between completed branches from shared evidence train the policy to acquire useful observations, avoid redundant ones, and stop when sufficient evidence is available. Execution feedback from the updated policy then guides exploration-program revision, adapting experience generation to the policy's changing observation needs. We further introduce GeoSearchBench, a benchmark for evaluating search quality and visual processing efficiency through relational search and region search. Experiments demonstrate that EvoSearch shortens search trajectories and substantially reduces visual processing while maintaining overall search quality, supporting scalable active perception for wide-area geospatial search.

## 163. ArEssays: Towards Robust Arabic Handwritten Text Understanding in Educational Settings

- **作者**：Hamdy Mubarak, Anas Madkoor, Ehsaneddin Asgari et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CL, cs.CV

Automated understanding of handwritten Arabic student essays remains challenging due to script complexity and high variability in children's writing. We introduce ArEssays, a large-scale dataset of authentic essays from primary to secondary students across Egypt, with both verbatim transcriptions and linguistically corrected versions. We benchmark 34 vision-language and OCR models, including fine-tuned systems, and find large performance gaps on real student handwriting. We release the dataset, training pipelines, and results to support research in Arabic OCR, document understanding, and educational NLP.

## 164. FlashSegmentator: High-Throughput TotalSegmentator Inference without Loss of Accuracy

- **作者**：Yosuke Yamagishi, Tomohiro Kikuchi, Shouhei Hanaoka et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：19 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables; supplementary material included. Code: https://github.com/yamagishi0824/FlashSegmentator

Purpose: To develop, evaluate, and publicly release FlashSegmentator, an open-source inference pipeline that increases the throughput of TotalSegmentator without retraining or loss of segmentation accuracy. Materials and Methods: This retrospective study used public datasets: 73 CT examinations from the TotalSegmentator test split (median age, 65 years; 37 male, 35 female, one unknown), 30 abdominal CT examinations from the Beyond the Cranial Vault (BTCV) dataset, 512 training-split CT examinations for concurrent-process analysis, and 55 MRI examinations. FlashSegmentator adds static-shape compilation, pipelining, batched sliding-window inference, and GPU postprocessing. Throughput was compared with the reference implementation (including its lower-resolution modes) and FastSegmentator on GH200, A100, and T4 GPUs. Dice agreement was tested with two one-sided tests (margin, +/-0.01). Results: FlashSegmentator processed 3782, 1810, and 270 examinations per hour on the GH200, A100, and T4 (57.7, 33.9, and 6.4 times the reference implementation and 3.20, 2.50, and 1.22 times compiled FastSegmentator). Dice scores were equivalent for all 117 structures on every GPU (mean difference <= 1.2 x 10^-5; P < .001) and at the examination level on BTCV. In contrast, the lower-resolution modes of the reference implementation processed 181 and 213 examinations per hour on the A100 but reduced mean Dice by 0.116 and 0.241. On MRI, throughput increased 94-fold with all 50 structures equivalent. Estimated A100 cloud cost per 10,000 examinations decreased from 997 to 29 US dollars. Conclusion: FlashSegmentator increased the throughput of TotalSegmentator on data center and entry-level GPUs without loss of segmentation accuracy compared with the reference implementation.

## 165. Seeing Is Not Reaching: Instruction Shortcuts Override Visual Grounding in Vision-Language-Action Models

- **作者**：Qing Zhang, Xuesong Li, Zhaoyuan Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：31 pages, including supplementary material. Code: https://github.com/QZhang2111/psg

Vision-language-action models (VLAs) are expected to inherit visual grounding from vision-language models, yet often reach for an object's training location after it has been moved. Across six VLAs, we identify two shortcuts: a scene shortcut, where the arm follows the scene's original task despite a new instruction, and an instruction shortcut, where the instruction retrieves the requested object's training location. In $π_{0.5}$, the instruction shortcut persists even though object-name attention follows the moved target. We call this Seeing Is Not Reaching. To determine whether this grounding is unused or overridden, we separately mask an image-mediated grounding pathway and a direct instruction-to-action pathway. Masking the grounding pathway eliminates reaches to moved targets, whereas masking the direct pathway increases them. Thus, grounding already guides action but is often overridden by the instruction shortcut, which acts through the direct pathway. We introduce Pathway-Separated Guidance (PSG), which amplifies grounding and suppresses the shortcut without training or external target locations. On LIBERO-PRO Object under Position perturbations, PSG raises the target-reaching rate from 22.0% to 52.6% and task success from 16.6% to 40.8%, while largely preserving performance on the original tasks.

## 166. SplatTok: Data-Driven Compression of 3D Gaussian Splats with Scene-Level Tokens

- **作者**：Sai Karthikey Pentapati, Shashank Nelamangala Sridhara, Birendra Kathariya et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR

3D Gaussian splats enable real-time rendering but require substantial storage. Existing compression methods either encode the primitives of a given splat or optimize a compact representation independently for each scene. Yet a Gaussian splat is only one realization of a scene: different arrangements of primitives can produce nearly identical rendered views. We exploit this freedom on the decoding side with SplatTok, a learned scene-resynthesis codec. Trained across thousands of real-world scenes, a transformer encodes an existing splat into compact, spatially anchored latent tokens, and a shared decoder synthesizes a new set of standard Gaussian primitives without preserving correspondence to the input. The representation is learned through rendering supervision rather than primitive reconstruction. At compression time, a quantization-aware latent search refines only the scene code, keeping the networks frozen, and closes most of the encoder's remaining error. On standard benchmarks, using only renders of the input splat for the latent search, without the original training images, SplatTok matches the PSNR of the feed-forward codec FCGS with 4 to 6.5 times smaller files, compressing input splats about 75 to 110 times. With the training images, it exceeds the PSNR of the per-scene method HAC++ with 13 to 41% smaller files. A single model trained on DL3DV generalizes to unseen scenes with millions of Gaussians and supports multiple target rates without retraining.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32202) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32202)

## 167. MoGe-4D: Efficient, Flexible, and Unified Dense 4D Reconstruction and Tracking

- **作者**：Ruicheng Wang, Linlin Xin, Sicheng Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project Page: https://microsoft.github.io/MoGe-4D

Reconstructing dynamic scenes from RGB video requires reasoning about both 3D geometry and motion across viewpoints, yet existing methods either split reconstruction and tracking into fragmented pipelines or face prohibitive dense spatiotemporal decoding costs. We introduce MoGe-4D, a feed-forward framework that unifies geometry and motion prediction via ordered queries of paired allocentric and egocentric dynamic point maps. Each query maps source pixels to a target time simultaneously in both camera frames, resolving relative camera pose in closed form while flexibly serving depth, dynamic registration, and dense 3D tracking. For efficient dense querying, MoGe-4D decouples scene modeling from query decoding: a high-capacity context encoder caches coarse global spatiotemporal features once, while a lightweight query encoder rapidly decodes dense point maps at flexible resolutions. Furthermore, as supervising dense 4D queries demands continuous 3D correspondences whose raw trajectory storage is prohibitive, we introduce RigIDTrack, a synthetic data pipeline and dataset that compresses surface dynamics into rigidity-regularized affine bodies, recovering exact arbitrary-frame 3D trajectories with $\sim15\%$ storage overhead over RGB-D video. Across diverse benchmarks, MoGe-4D achieves state-of-the-art dense 3D tracking, matches dedicated 3D foundation models in reconstruction, and runs substantially faster than prior 4D baselines.
