## 168. What to Change, What to Keep: Experience-Evolving Agents for Video Recasting

- **作者**：Shiying Zhang, Yuxia Zhu, Huaze Xue et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 6 figures, 2 tables. The first three authors contributed equally to this work. Supplementary material is not included in this version

Video recasting aims to transform the entities and environments of existing videos into new visual worlds while preserving their essential actions and interactions. However, achieving coherent transformations remains challenging, as coordinated edits can disrupt the original event structure. In this paper, we introduce RecastAgent, an experience-guided agentic framework for source-conditioned video recasting that operates under frozen model weights. Our framework employs an Experience-Guided Planner to choose edit prompts and input video states under qualitative visual critique, allowing the agent to build upon partial progress or backtrack to preserve source structure. Across tasks, an Experience Accumulation module distills completed trajectories into conditional, evidence-linked principles that record both an editing strategy and its scope of validity. We additionally construct a benchmark of 67 source videos with 134 paired-specification tasks across six creative categories for evaluation. Experiments demonstrate that RecastAgent outperforms single-pass and agentic rewriting baselines across Bernini and Ditto. Furthermore, accumulated experience improves performance on held-out test videos and transfers zero-shot to external benchmarks like FiVE-Bench and VE-Bench.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32196) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32196)

## 169. SolarFilNet-V4: Multi-Scale Transformer Vision with Physics-Informed Adaptive Post-Processing for Solar Filament Segmentation

- **作者**：Indra Agustian, Ruvita Faurina
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：4 pages, 1 figure, IEEE format, Technical Report for Kaggle Solar Filament Segmentation Challenge 2026. Source code and model: https://github.com/indraagustian/SolarFilNet-V4-Kaggle2026

Solar filaments are cool, dense chromospheric plasma structures held by magnetic field lines above polarity inversion lines. Their destabilization triggers coronal mass ejections and severe space weather hazards. Segmenting individual filaments from ground-based GONG H-alpha observations (2048 x 2048 pixels) is challenging due to low contrast, turbulent fibrils, delicate barb structures, and annotator boundary variance. In this technical report, we describe SolarFilNet-V4, a targeted segmentation framework developed for the MAGFiLO 1.0 benchmark (Solar Filament Segmentation Challenge 2026). Our method couples a lightweight Pyramid Vision Transformer (PVT-v2-B1) backbone with anisotropic curvilinear strip convolutions (k=15) and dual-frequency feed-forward blocks (DFFN) to follow extended flux ropes while capturing thin central spines. A shallow full-resolution convolutional branch operating at 1024 x 1024 preserves high-frequency edge details and mitigates transformer downsampling. We train the network using multi-task supervision over masks, centerline spines, barb boundaries, and pixel-contrastive representations. At test time, we apply 8-fold dihedral test-time augmentation (D4), bilinearly upscale predictions to the native 2048 x 2048 grid, and apply an activity-adaptive post-processing routine that calibrates morphological joining parameters for quiet versus active solar disks. On the official benchmark, SolarFilNet-V4 achieves an out-of-fold validation Panoptic Quality of 0.4340 and a Public Leaderboard score of 0.380. The model contains 15.46M parameters and requires 224.34 GFLOPs per forward pass, reducing parameter count by 36.8% compared to standard ResNet-34 U-Net baselines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32193) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32193)

## 170. OVOK: A Recursive Self-Improving Agentic Framework for Video-Based Articulated Robot State Estimation

- **作者**：Cheng Dai, Renkai Luo, Guanfeng Tang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：24 pages, including appendix. Submitted to CVPR 2027

Recovering articulated robot state from video supports metric spatial sensing for manipulation, scene reconstruction, and action planning. Yet the problem is ill-posed: different intrinsics, camera-to-robot poses, and joint angles can explain similar observations. Prior task-specific regressors typically assume known intrinsics, fit one robot through laborious data collection and robot-specific training, and still transfer poorly across scenes. Foundation models apply to any robot zero-shot, but their estimates have large errors. We present OVOK (One Video to Kinetics), a recursive self-improving agentic framework for this task. Under a human-authored protocol, an agentic initializer constructs robot-specific OVOK-4k; a proposer--executor--verifier loop then jointly evolves tools and executable workflows from feedback. Updated tools and workflows are accepted only when the verifier judges a positive improvement. The retained workflow is frozen for accurate inference. Experiments demonstrate that the evolved workflow achieves state-of-the-art results on DREAM-V and RoboKeyGen-V, delivering robust and accurate estimates.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32185) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32185)

## 171. ThinkMorph-SSL: Scaling Interleaved Multimodal Reasoning with Pretext Tasks

- **作者**：Jiawei Gu, Linjie Li, Yiming Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://thinkmorph.github.io/ssl/

People reason with pictures as well as words. Interleaved multimodal reasoning gives models a similar way to solve problems by creating intermediate images and using them to guide further reasoning. Training models with this ability usually relies on task-specific demonstrations, which are difficult to collect. We introduce ThinkMorph-SSL, a two-stage training recipe that uses images themselves as a source of supervision. The first stage, pretext reasoning, converts visual pretext tasks, such as image colorization and denoising, into instruction-free interleaved traces. Models describe a corrupted or transformed image, generate a restored or canonical image, and interpret the result. The second stage uses automatically constructed demonstrations for question-driven instruction tuning. At the same instruction-tuning budget, models trained with pretext reasoning outperform those trained with instruction tuning alone. The full recipe improves over the base model by an average of 11.33 accuracy points across twelve benchmarks. These results show that supervision derived from images can build the visual skills needed for interleaved reasoning and improve learning from task-specific demonstrations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32183) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32183)

## 172. DocPristine: Content-Aware Document Appearance Enhancement under Real-World Mixed Degradations

- **作者**：Zihan Huang, Junle Liu, Zhonghao Ye et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures

Camera-captured documents often exhibit mixed degradations, such as highlights, shadows, and blur, that distort backgrounds and obscure text details. Document appearance enhancement (DAE) aims to mitigate these degradations and improve readability. Existing document appearance enhancement methods rely either on input-derived visual cues that can become unreliable under severe degradation or on LLM-based degradation diagnosis that lacks spatial guidance. Notably, we find that pretrained document visual encoders continue to attend to text regions in degraded documents, providing content-aware guidance. Based on this insight, we propose DocPristine, which adaptively fuses multi-layer features from a frozen document visual encoder and injects them into a pixel restoration network. To integrate these features into a pixel restoration network, DocPristine combines Adaptive Multi-Layer Feature Fusion (AMFF), Hierarchical Gated Conditioning (HGC), and Shifted-Window Cross-Attention (SWCA). Specifically, SWCA draws on structural cues from neighboring locations across window boundaries to recover fine text details.We also introduce DocACE, with a synthetic mixed-degradation training set (DocACE-Synthetic) and a real-world test set (DocACE-Real) containing manually restored ground-truth images and document parsing annotations. Comprehensive experiments across three benchmarks demonstrate that DocPristine achieves state-of-the-art appearance enhancement quality and consistently improves downstream document parsing. Code, models, and data will be publicly available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32176) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32176)

## 173. LoopFit: Retrofitting Pretrained Diffusion Transformers with Recurrent Refinement

- **作者**：Xiaocheng Lu, Haoxi Li, Dazhao Du et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Scaling inference computation offers a way to improve diffusion transformers (DiTs), but taking more sampling steps leaves the depth of each denoising prediction unchanged. Recurrently reusing Transformer blocks increases computation within each step, allowing further refinement of hidden representations. Retrofitting pretrained models enables this capability without repeating pretraining, but requires blocks learned for fixed-depth computation to refine states produced by their own repeated application. The challenge is to use each additional pass to correct local errors without disrupting the structure established by earlier passes. We introduce LoopFit, which uses feedback from earlier passes to guide refinement at each image patch. Residual Attention Modulation (RAM) uses each patch's feature change in one pass to scale and shift its input to attention in the next. During training, Residual Relay Loss (RRL) gives greater weight to patches whose prediction errors persist across passes. A noise-dependent depth schedule speeds up sampling by using four passes at high noise and one at lower noise levels. At comparable cost, backbones trained with four passes solve more problems using recurrent depth than extra sampling steps. Across 12 visual puzzle settings, LoopFit solves 89.8% of boards, compared with 87.8% for the strongest baseline evaluated on all 12. Most of the gain comes from tracing long paths on mazes and frozen lakes.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32175) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32175)

## 174. MediaStormBench-CameraLog: Can Multimodal Models Identify a Camera's Log Curve?

- **作者**：Ming Ma, Wenhao Hu, Qichao Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

MediaStormBench is a multimodal benchmark for real-world film and television production, with each subset drawn from MediaStorm's day-to-day work. We release its first subset, MediaStormBench-CameraLog (CameraLogBench), on a common foundational task in footage ingest and initial color grading: identifying the Log curve of a clip without metadata. Cameras record Log footage by compressing a wide range of scene light into code values with a vendor-defined logarithmic curve, and color can be restored correctly only once that curve is known. The curve, however, is recorded only in file metadata, easily lost in transcoding and team hand-offs. A colorist then has to try candidate curves one by one against a waveform scope, and a wrong choice shifts the tones and colors of the whole clip. Recovering the curve from the image alone is challenging, because vendor curves cluster tightly in code value and are coupled with unknown exposure. CameraLogBench is built on professionally shot footage and tests whether multimodal models can identify the curve, with a probe that removes the correct option to expose guessing from priors. Because every candidate curve is strictly defined by a published vendor formula, we derive four controls from one implementation of these formulas: graded negatives, synthetic frames of identical scenes, in-context specification digests, and a training-free formula baseline, which together decouple semantic priors from photometric measurement. Across 11 models, real-footage accuracy barely exceeds always guessing the most frequent curve; when the correct option is removed, models almost never abstain, revealing heavy reliance on scene and brand priors. Supplying the vendor specification sharply improves recognition on calibrated charts but hardly helps on natural images without a photometric reference, exposing a gap between reading a specification and measuring pixels.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32172) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32172)

## 175. Mind the Band: The Hidden Frequency Variable in Geometric RoPE

- **作者**：Seonghyun Jin, Youngmin Kim, Jong Chul Ye
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：33 pages, 15 figures, 23 tables. Project page: https://bispl-wm.github.io/MindTheBand/

Geometric rotary position encodings (RoPE) incorporate 3D camera geometry into multi-view attention by rotating token features according to viewing rays, depths, and reprojected image coordinates. Each coordinate is rotated across a progression of frequencies--its frequency band--yet existing implementations leave this band to inherited defaults, either retaining standard 2D RoPE's top frequency or letting the frequency span grow exponentially with the attention head dimension. We show that this unstandardized setting can reverse benchmark rankings. In novel view synthesis on RealEstate10K, RayRoPE matches the strongest baseline in the 47M model (144-channel heads), yet trails by 1.2 dB in the 25M model (48-channel heads), and exhibits the same reversal when varying head size alone at fixed model width. Populating a 243x range with four frequencies instead of the 3x default at 48 channels recovers 0.9 dB of that gap. Across view synthesis and two-view depth estimation, overly narrow bands consistently blur correspondence, whereas all three projection-based encodings perform reliably when their top frequency lies within 0.3--3 rotations per 8-pixel patch (10--90 rotations across 256x256 RealEstate10K). At the opposite extreme, overly wide bands degrade RayRoPE through a forward-backward asymmetry: averaging rotations over a predicted depth interval damps high frequencies in the forward pass, yet leaves their gradients with respect to predicted depth undamped. Blocking only those high-frequency depth gradients recovers 90--93% of the lost PSNR, showing that forward smoothing does not guarantee backward stability. Guided by this analysis, we distill practical guidelines--setting bands in image coordinates, populating spans in small heads, and avoiding unconditional same-view damping--and advocate reporting and matching frequency bands in future multi-view benchmarks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32171) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32171)

## 176. Multi-view Random-set Learning

- **作者**：Mengchi Zuo, Tianyang Wang, Cai Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, including supplementary material. Corresponding author: Cai Xu

Trusted multi-view learning aims to integrate heterogeneous views for accurate and reliable predictions. However, multi-view data often exhibit the view degradation phenomenon: individual views contain only partial discriminative information and cannot independently determine the instance-level label. Requiring such views to produce unambiguous class evidence can distort evidence learning and mislead subsequent fusion. Existing methods addressing this problem have at least one of the following drawbacks: (1) assigning evidence to individual classes without explicitly representing unresolved class groups; (2) projecting composite beliefs onto individual classes before fusion, preventing direct refinement of candidate groups through cross-view set intersections. To address these limitations, we propose a new Multi-view Random-Set Learning (MRSL) paradigm, which preserves view-specific support for candidate class sets and refines candidate classes through cross-view fusion. Specifically, we quantify class overlap to select a compact collection of sets for each view, avoiding the exponential cost of modeling all class subsets. We then perform set-level fusion by assigning joint support to set intersections, allowing complementary views to narrow the candidate classes before probability conversion. Experiments on synthetic and eight real-world datasets validate the proposed overlap measure and demonstrate that MRSL achieves the highest accuracy on all eight datasets. Compared with the strongest baselines, MRSL improves average accuracy by 4.71% and reduces average expected calibration error by 6.33%. All datasets and code will be made publicly available upon acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32170) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32170)

## 177. SoccerNarrate: Event-Grounded Streaming Soccer Commentary with Macro-Window Preference Alignment

- **作者**：Zihan Jia, Zhilin Dai, Zhengming Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Existing clip-based soccer commentary models often produce delayed, repeated, or poorly synchronized narration on untrimmed full-match videos. Recent streaming video-language models make low-latency full-match narration feasible, but they often favor general descriptions rather than professional, event-grounded soccer commentary. They may describe nearby actions while failing to mention key events such as goals, cards, substitutions, and offsides correctly and on time. In this paper, we present SoccerNarrate to bridge low-latency streaming narration with event-grounded professional soccer commentary across data, training, and evaluation. First, we construct SoccerNarrate-Data, a large-scale time-anchored dataset with word-level ASR alignment, quality filtering, and entity calibration. Second, we perform macro-window preference alignment (MWPA), which aligns the model with complete event-level commentary semantics by comparing causal multi-step rollouts from the same streaming prefix while keeping second-level inference unchanged. We further use event-mismatched human commentaries as counterfactual negatives to emphasize event semantics over commentary style. Third, we introduce SoccerNarrate-Eval, an event-centric benchmark based on temporally constrained event entailment. Full-match experiments show that SoccerNarrate improves event coverage and event-aligned precision over strong offline and streaming baselines. Code and models are released at https://github.com/MCG-NJU/SoccerNarrate.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32153) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32153)

## 178. RoboProbe: Benchmarking Physical Plausibility of Robot World Models

- **作者**：Zhuoying Yang, Jiachen Ni, Yichen Guo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：42 pages, including supplementary material

Evaluating the physical plausibility of generated videos is critical for reliable robotic planning with world models. These videos inform action proposal and assessment before real-world execution, so undetected physical violations can lead to erroneous assessments of action feasibility and safety. Yet current video generators often produce physically implausible interactions. Detecting such violations directly with vision-language models (VLMs) remains challenging as physical verification requires evidence-grounded spatiotemporal reasoning beyond high-level semantic understanding. We develop five law-specific metrics for evaluating physical plausibility by combining spatiotemporal evidence from segmentation, tracking, 3D geometry, or appearance comparisons with targeted VLM reasoning: Contact fidelity, Object persistence, Solidity, Robot deformation, and Object deformation. Building on this metric suite, we introduce RoboProbe, an agentic evaluation framework that coordinates metric execution and selectively refines evaluations through constrained actions. To evaluate the physical plausibility of robot world models, we further introduce RP-Bench, with 761 human-labeled generated videos and 130 real demonstrations of 239 manipulation tasks from 12 robot datasets. On RP-Bench, all five metrics achieve AUROCs above 0.85 and outperform the evaluated learned judges, prior evaluators, and a frames-only Gemini baseline. Applying these metrics to five video generators reveals distinct patterns of physical violations across models, in both violation types and frequencies.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32152) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32152)

## 179. Let The Pixels Through: Rethinking Long Skip Connections for Scalable Pixel Diffusion

- **作者**：Jiachen Lei, Jiahong Wu, XiangXiang Chu
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Training pixel diffusion models remains less straightforward, owing to the ad hoc training recipes and architecture designs, along with the sensitivity to hyper-parameter choices. We show that strong denoisers can be built by refining existing pixel-native designs from the literature. Specifically, we revisit the long skip connection in U-Net and find that the optimization benefit commonly attributed to it is not necessary for its denoising benefit. We further find that the conventional linear combination of features from the skip and main backbone is inadequate for regimes of various noise levels. Motivated by these observations, we introduce two core modifications. First, we adopt asymmetric long skips, which propagate fine-grained information from input embeddings to decoder layers. Second, we introduce adaGLU, a SwiGLU-like MLP block, in decoder layers to adaptively filter information from the embeddings before being added to main-branch features. The above designs give a simple pixel diffusion transformer architecture. When trained from scratch, it scales smoothly from small-scale baselines up to 1.6B parameters, achieving 1.62 and 1.70 FID on ImageNet-256 and -512, respectively. Besides, it natively supports multimodal inputs, such as text and images, yielding competitive performance in text-to-image generation. We release our codes at https://github.com/AMAP-ML/PiT

## 180. ARS-VLM: Visually Aligned Augmented Random Search for Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Xiangwei Wang, Jianyuan Lin, Peng Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.LG

First-order reinforcement learning, such as Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), is widely used to post-train Vision-Language Models (VLMs) for multimodal reasoning. However, it incurs heavy memory and system overhead from backpropagation, optimizer states, and a reference model. Forward-only zeroth-order (ZO) methods, such as Evolution Strategies (ES) and its top-k variant Augmented Random Search (ARS), avoid these overheads via reward-guided weight perturbations, yet remain unexplored for VLM reasoning. We find that directly applying standard ES or vanilla ARS to VLMs suffers from two fundamental failure modes: (i) patch-grid overfitting, fitting fragile grid-specific visual token artifacts while ignoring spatial invariants, and (ii) catastrophic forgetting, degrading general perception and out-of-distribution (OOD) tasks due to unbounded high-rank parameter and policy drift. We propose ARS-VLM, a visually aligned Augmented Random Search framework for forward-only VLM post-training. First, ARS-VLM extracts a contrastive visual basis from bilinearly paired cross-grid views, concentrating perturbations on shared visual signals while suppressing patch-shift artifacts. Second, ARS-VLM anchors shallow visual-fusion layers with a soft subspace prior while freeing deep reasoning layers, restraining policy drift without a reference model. Third, ARS-VLM scores perturbations on paired views to discard view-fragile directions during top-k selection. We evaluate ARS-VLM on Qwen3-VL-4B and Qwen3-VL-8B on multimodal reasoning tasks. On 8B, ARS-VLM reaches 61.0% validation accuracy in 20 steps versus 150 for GRPO (7.5x fewer optimization steps) and avoids the late OOD collapse of vanilla ARS and ES. Moreover, ARS-VLM achieves 75.41% single-view micro accuracy across seven OOD vision reasoning benchmarks, compared with 75.31% for full-parameter GRPO, using forward-only post-training.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32147) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32147)

## 181. CSC-Mamba: Bridging multimodal convolutional sparse coding and state space models for multi-contrast MRI super-resolution

- **作者**：Iman Marivani, David Van Hamme, Jan Aelterman et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：This manuscript has been submitted to the IEEE International Symposium on Biomedical Imaging (ISBI 2027) and is currently under review

Multi-contrast MRI super-resolution accelerates clinical protocols by reconstructing high-resolution (HR) target modalities from low-resolution (LR) inputs, guided by easily acquired reference contrasts. While purely data-driven deep neural networks achieve strong performance via rich representation capacity, they rely on heuristic fusion mechanisms (e.g., naive concatenation or black-box cross-attention) that lack theoretical grounding. Alternatively, model-based deep unfolding networks enforce principled cross-contrast fusion through explicit priors, yet their unrolled steps remain constrained to shallow convolutions, lacking modern deep blocks to capture global context. To bridge this gap, we propose CSC-MAMBA, unifying deep-unfolded multimodal convolutional sparse coding with the Vision Mamba state-space backbone. By fusing common structural priors from the guidance modality via unfolded sparse coding and globally refining contrast-specific representations with a Mamba back- bone, our approach benefits from interpretable, model-guided fusion and long-range spatial context. Experiments on the IXI dataset demonstrate that CSC-MAMBA outperforms state-of-the-art methods in reconstruction quality, achieving up to a 0.62 dB gain in PSNR while remaining computationally efficient.

## 182. LiteSTAR: Low-Rank Channel Modulation for Temporally Consistent CVD Video Compensation

- **作者**：Peiyang Lin, Sichen Lin, Yipeng Liao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Color Vision Deficiency (CVD) affects a large population worldwide, yet practical recoloring must run continuously on-device. Frame-wise video recoloring produces color flickering, while existing methods rely on costly cross-frame alignment in pixel or feature space. We observe that color flickering correlates with variations in channel-wise responses, with color-sensitive variations concentrated in a low-rank subspace. Based on this observation, we propose Lightweight Spatio-Temporal Adaptive Recoloring (LiteSTAR), built around LiCaD, a plug-and-play Lightweight Channel-Adaptive Decoder. LiCaD performs temporal filtering in a low-rank state space with learned process and observation uncertainties. It then modulates feature channels to achieve temporal consistency without explicit spatial alignment. LiteSTAR further employs adaptive patch routing to improve color discrimination for CVD users while avoiding unnecessary over-enhancement. We also introduce EgoCVDSet, the first egocentric video benchmark for CVD recoloring. Extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate that LiteSTAR achieves superior performance with only 0.05M parameters. LiCaD further demonstrates strong generalization across different tasks and network architectures. The code, models, and dataset will be released after review.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32140) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32140)

## 183. VIDE : Training-Free Video Anomaly Detection via Visual Intrinsic Distribution Estimation

- **作者**：Kevin D. Nguetche, Atif Belal, Lilian Hollard et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

In embedding-based video anomaly detection (VAD), normal clips can have high similarity to anomalous descriptions, an anomaly bias that leads to false positives. Methods such as PRISM mitigate this bias by measuring deviations from a normality reference along anomaly directions. Both the normality reference and the anomaly directions are derived from text and shared across videos, although normal activity and the appearance of anomalies vary across scenes. The resulting scores are also sensitive to prompt selection. To address these limitations, we propose Visual Intrinsic Distribution Estimation (VIDE), a training-free framework that adapts text-derived scoring to each test video using frozen clip embeddings. VIDE estimates normal scene content from clips with low initial anomaly scores and uses this estimate to partially correct the textual reference. It then rescales category responses, penalizes clips with strong responses across categories, and refines the anomaly directions using high-scoring clips. VIDE averages scores across prompt views to reduce sensitivity to prompt selection and fuses scores from two frozen encoders. With both encoders, VIDE improves AUC over the best PRISM result by $1.5$, $1.9$, and $3.5$ points on UCF-Crime, XD-Violence, and MSAD, respectively, and AP by $6.5$ points on XD-Violence and $9.3$ points on MSAD. VIDE yields the highest AUC among the compared training-free methods on all three benchmarks and the highest reported AUC among all compared methods on XD-Violence and MSAD. Our code is included in the supplementary material and will be made publicly available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32138) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32138)

## 184. Interpretable Orthogonal Latent Diffusion with Scale-Selective Uncertainty for Medical Image Super-Resolution

- **作者**：Onkar Jadhav, Tim French
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Diffusion models are increasingly used for medical image reconstruction and super-resolution, but their samples may contain plausible fine-scale hallucinations that are difficult to identify from the reconstructed image alone. We propose Med-PODiff, a patch-wise proper orthogonal decomposition (POD) diffusion framework for brain MRI super-resolution that performs generative reconstruction in compact and interpretable local coefficient spaces. Unlike pixel-space or learned-latent diffusion models, Med-PODiff uses the POD structure to separate low-rank anatomical variation from fine-detail residual variation, enabling predictive uncertainty to be decomposed into total, coarse, and fine components. Building on this decomposition, we define a pixel-wise hallucination-risk score, $H_p$, that emphasizes regions where fine-detail uncertainty dominates coarse structural uncertainty. On brain MRI, Med-PODiff outperforms representative diffusion baselines in reconstruction quality while producing well-calibrated predictive intervals, with total uncertainty detecting high-error regions at AUROC 0.954. More importantly, the decomposed uncertainty is scale-selective: coarse and fine uncertainty preferentially predict errors at the corresponding scales, with $H_p$ localizing fine error at AUROC $0.906$. Controlled injections and retrospective Cartesian $k$-space undersampling further support fine-detail localization and degradation robustness. The discarded POD tail is only $3.8\%$ of the retained fine-error magnitude, and Med-PODiff improves downstream segmentation consistency while requiring substantially less inference time than pixel-space diffusion.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32135) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32135)

## 185. EditText: Structure-Aware Generation and Selective Visual Transport for Fine-Grained Text Editing

- **作者**：Zhaobin Chu, Guandong Li, Xin Lv et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Fine-grained text editing aims to accurately replace text in a specified region while preserving the source text's font, color, and texture, as well as visual consistency in unedited regions. We present EditText, a unified framework for fine-grained text editing that combines target character generation with visual transport from the source image. The model uses dual-condition Fill inputs: a masked image and a source text style reference, both sharing a spatial reference with the target image. The masked image hides the original text and retains unedited content as a source canvas; the style reference supplies appearance cues that guide new character generation together with the target text. We introduce an Attention Transport framework in which Source Weight describes the attention allocated to a condition and the Spatial Aggregation Kernel describes its internal spatial attention distribution. Accurate transfer of source details depends on attention strength, the retention of details in condition features, and spatially accurate aggregation. For selected non-edit queries, we therefore preserve the total attention weight assigned to the masked image while redistributing its attention toward spatially corresponding neighborhoods, enabling selective visual transport. Multi-stage training, combined with a Glyph-aware Structural Loss on local target character structure, teaches the model to generate accurate text, preserve source text appearance, and retain unedited details. We also introduce TBench, a fine-grained text editing benchmark covering Alphabetic, Logographic, and Arabic numerals, to evaluate character updates, appearance preservation, and the preservation of unedited details. On TBench, EditText achieves state-of-the-art non-edit-region SSIM (0.9769) and full-image LPIPS (0.0460).

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32134) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32134)

## 186. The Old Scene Lives in the Cache: Diagnosing and Shedding Stale History in Streaming Video Generation

- **作者**：Hao Zhou, Ziyang Xu, Chenhan Jin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Switching scenes while preserving the original subject in streaming video generation exposes a conflict: cached history helps maintain the subject but also sustains old-scene objects that should disappear. Through controlled interventions on text conditioning, denoising steps, the local window, and the attention sink, we identify how cached visual history keeps this obsolete content. When the original frames remain in the cache, KV flush and sink recomputation still leave old-scene objects in the generated videos. Replacing this history with target-scene history largely removes those objects, but also changes the subject, reducing face similarity to the pre-switch subject. We then propose Molt Forcing, a training-free method that rebuilds scene history from a cold-start target scene while selectively retaining subject tokens from the original cache. Across 16 scene families, Molt Forcing removes old-scene objects at least as reliably as an Echo-Forcing hard cut while keeping the subject much closer to its pre-switch appearance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32116) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32116)

## 187. An Automated Framework Integrating Deep Learning and Anatomical Reasoning for Cardiac Phenotyping From CCTA

- **作者**：Hien Minh Tran, Thi-Thu-Hien Pham, Le Duy Tan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：12 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables

Coronary computed tomography angiography (CCTA) depicts coronary anatomy and cardiac fat, but segmentation alone does not ensure anatomically consistent measurements. We propose an automated framework that combines deep learning with anatomical reasoning for quantitative cardiac phenotyping. Its anatomical measurement module uses graph geometry, branch identity, and surrounding structures to define vessel-specific pericoronary adipose tissue (PCAT) sampling and a surrogate epicardial adipose tissue (EAT) compartment. The latter also generates training targets without requiring expert pericardium contours. A companion module quantifies coronary calcium on non-contrast CT. Component-wise benchmarking was conducted on five public datasets: ImageCAS, ImageCAS-X, COCA, Visual Lab, and MultiD4CAD. On ImageCAS-X Test-160, lumen segmentation with validation-selected post-processing achieved a Dice score of 0.9133 and a clDice score of 0.940, exceeding the corresponding published CAS-Net point estimates. In the four-split ImageCAS evaluation, the four-member ensemble outperformed the single ResEnc-L model. Analysis of 800 paired scans showed that PCAT attenuation measurements differed between whole-vessel and lumen references. External EAT evaluation showed strong volume correlation and good spatial overlap. Explicit anatomical rules make the resulting measurements interpretable and inspectable. In practice, the framework provides a standardized pipeline for processing cardiac CT cohorts and quantifying coronary calcium, PCAT, and EAT for population-level analysis and comparison across studies. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/HarryTRAN207/CardiacCT-Research

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32114) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32114)

## 188. PhysRF-WorldJEPA: Transforming Heterogeneous Large Language Models into Manifold-Decoupled Millimeter-Wave Radar World Models

- **作者**：Yicheng Huang, Feng Ke, Linhan Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV, eess.SP
- **备注**：11 pages, 6 figures, 4 tables

Adapting Large Language Models (LLMs) to millimeter-wave (mmWave) radar enables privacy-preserving, all-weather physical scene understanding across darkness, smoke, and walls. However, standard instruction tuning treats continuous radar streams as static text-like tokens, causing near-random global radar-to-text retrieval and severe physical drift under sensor blackouts. Conversely, coupling pairwise contrastive losses (e.g., SigLIP) directly into the shared backbone improves retrieval but injects $O(B^2)$ repulsive gradients that fracture continuous coordinate manifolds ($R^2 < 0$). We present PhysRF-WorldJEPA, a manifold-decoupled Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) that separates continuous electromagnetic state prediction from cross-modal language alignment. An unattenuated 1.0x clock anchor and parallel dual-scale RF encoder feed a lean ~3% LoRA LLM trained with two-step state-space EMA-JEPA (Denoise -> Rollout) and sample-axis Batch-VICReg to predict +3.0 s physical states; a stop-gradient alignment head then maps frozen prompt states onto the text hypersphere with false-negative masking. On a 4,541-window tri-domain suite (77/60.25/60 GHz) against 10 baselines, freezing the physical backbone and training only the decoupled head lifts R->T R@10 from 5.95% to 15.01% and improves MedRank from 107.0 to 60.0 while preserving identical physical readout: 3.37 deg ($R^2=+0.961$) blackout +3.0 s torso tilt, 1.89 m ($R^2=+0.679$; 1.19 m/+0.863 at the RF front-end) cluttered UAV altitude, 2.04 m ($R^2=+0.461$) behind-wall NLOS range, 0.9976 virtual-RIS invariance, and 100% fall recall. Across five 0.5B-1.8B LLM families (15 zero-overhead models), World-JEPA improves retrieval and 75%-drop completion in 10/10 runs and blackout tilt in 4/5 (Span-Euc), 2/5 (Scatter), and 5/5 (per-family best) over SFT.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32113) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32113)

## 189. RISE: Reasoning with Implicit State Embeddings for GUI Agents

- **作者**：Enqi Liu, Zhi Gao, Liu Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Graphical user interface (GUI) agents translate user instructions and screenshots into executable actions. To learn executable actions, existing approaches often rely on explicit reasoning during training, which encourage over-reliance on specific reasoning patterns rather than generalizable action-relevant information. In this paper, we propose an implicit framework, RISE, which integrates action-relevant reasoning into implicit state representations to guide GUI action prediction. To this end, action-relevant reasoning is captured through three complementary supervision objectives: full-CoT state alignment preserves information across the reasoning process, action-distribution alignment encodes action preferences, and reasoning-to-action boundary alignment captures the representation immediately before action generation. In this case, our RISE effectively learns informative implicit representations, thereby improving the accuracy of next-action token prediction. RISE achieves 70.6\% accuracy on OSWorld-G, establishing state-of-the-art performance among compared 8B models with a 4.3-point gain over its backbone. Results across two distinct backbones and six diverse benchmarks further demonstrate its effectiveness in GUI grounding and action prediction. Our code is available on our project page.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32102) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32102)

## 190. PreferenceScene: Personalized Indoor Scene Synthesis from Feedback

- **作者**：Seongrae Noh, RyunHa Lee, HyeongYeop Kang
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.GR
- **备注**：Project page: https://seongraenoh.github.io/preferencescene/

Text-to-scene methods turn a short instruction into a furnished 3D room, but the room that a particular person wants rarely comes across fully in words: many preferences surface only when the person sees a concrete room and can point to what they like and dislike in it. We therefore study personalized indoor scene synthesis under hidden user preferences: over several turns, a system shows a 3D scene, and the user, who sees only rendered images of the scene, replies with brief free-form feedback and a satisfaction score. The system must therefore bring the whole room toward the user's preferences from feedback that comments on only part of it at a time. It must also be able to change every part of the room, because a preference concerns not only single objects but the look and atmosphere that surfaces, lighting, textiles, furniture and their arrangement create together. We present PreferenceScene, in which a large language model keeps an explicit estimate of the user's preferences for every design attribute of the room and, after each turn, infers the taste behind the feedback and revises the whole estimate, including the parts that the feedback does not mention. A scene instantiation module builds every estimated value as its own part of the room, so that each of these parts can follow the user's preferences. We evaluate on the test set of a benchmark with 320 instances in ten room types, with a simulated user and a render-based evaluator. PreferenceScene matches the hidden preferences better than five published text-to-scene baselines adapted to the same interaction, and its match rises with every turn. It even surpasses them when they are given the preferences directly, and a vision-language model rates its scenes as the most realistic and functional.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32099) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32099)

## 191. GUI-PRISM: Efficient Action Decoding for GUI Agents

- **作者**：Enqi Liu, Zhi Gao, Liu Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Graphical user interface (GUI) agents translate screenshots and user instructions into executable actions. Most existing approaches rely on vision-language models to generate actions as text, often leading to substantial inference latency due to autoregressive decoding. To address this inefficiency, we propose GUI-PRISM, a hybrid action decoder that directly predicts action types and structured arguments while retaining language generation for open-ended text. Unlike existing spatial decoding methods that primarily focus on target localization, GUI-PRISM extends direct prediction to action types and their associated arguments, enabling complete GUI action prediction. Specifically, GUI-PRISM employs field-specific queries to extract action-relevant semantic and spatial information from a shared multimodal feature bank. It further models dependencies among action components through conditional queries. Gated residual cross-attention refines these predictions using fine-grained multimodal information. GUI-PRISM achieves 95.1\% accuracy on ScreenSpot-v2, outperforming the strongest head-based spatial decoding method by 2.0 percentage points while delivering a 3.8$\times$ inference speedup over the baseline. On AndroidControl-v2 and MM-Mind2Web-v2, it improves complete-action accuracy by 4.8 and 0.7 percentage points while achieving 2.7$\times$ and 3.9$\times$ inference speedups over the baseline, respectively. Our code is available on our project page.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32097) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32097)

## 192. Rethinking Post-Training Quantization for Large Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Minchan Kang, Kyeonghye Park, Seoyoung Cho et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Post-training quantization (PTQ) compresses large vision-language models (LVLMs) by calibrating quantization parameters on a small dataset without retraining. However, despite increasingly sophisticated calibration strategies, prior methods suffer substantial performance degradation under low-bit weight--activation quantization. To understand this degradation, we locate where quantization error is concentrated and examine the corresponding weight and activation distributions. We observe that a few outliers dominate the ranges shared by per-channel (PC) weights and per-token (PT) activations, leaving insufficient resolution for most values and mapping many of them to zero. These findings motivate a shift in focus from calibration refinement to the underlying quantization representation. We therefore present Rotate-and-Refine Quantization (RRQ) as a simple, effective baseline for LVLM PTQ, combining local Hadamard transforms with group quantization and efficient group-aware execution. Across 8 multimodal tasks and diverse quantization settings, RRQ substantially outperforms baselines while reducing inference time and memory usage. At W4A4, it retains over 96\% of full-precision performance on 8B models and over 99\% on a 32B model, compared with less than 8\% for these baselines. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/kmc3661/RRQ

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32096) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32096)

## 193. Clinical3D-Bench: Evaluating and Improving Spatial Intelligence of Multimodal LLMs in Hospital Environments

- **作者**：Yang Bai, Yingzhi Xia, Ling Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Spatial reasoning in hospitals requires tracking look-alike equipment, interpreting patient-relative directions, and judging passage widths, yet existing benchmarks provide limited coverage of these abilities. We introduce Clinical3D-Bench, a simulated benchmark with 3,368 questions of eight types on egocentric walkthroughs of 373 hospital-room layouts. Layouts follow hospital design guidelines, answers are computed from the 3D scenes, and paired scene states test whether models change their answers when relevant evidence changes. Videos are photorealistic, and ten physicians screened the layouts. Among 35 multimodal large language models (MLLMs), the best open and proprietary models reach 45.7% and 53.6% macro accuracy, against 40.8% for an answer prior and 79.0% for physicians on a subset, and 27 of 32 open models score below 40% on two-way patient-relative directions. Fine-tuning on Clinical3D-45K, our 44,750-question training set, lifts two MLLMs 16-20 points above the best proprietary model. 3D maps given to the fine-tuned MLLM as input go largely unused: replacing them with another video's, even when they are ground truth, barely changes its accuracy. The proposed GeoJeV instead lets the MLLM compute with 3D: it reconstructs a scene memory from each video, selects 3D operations and their arguments from the MLLM's question state, and adds their evidence to its option scores. It achieves the highest accuracy of all evaluated models, narrowing the gap to physicians.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32094) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32094)

## 194. Not the Scalar, but Its Evolution: Robust Source Attribution for Generated Images

- **作者**：Chao Wang, Zijin Yang, Yaofei Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

The widespread deployment of latent generative models (LGMs) makes generated image source attribution essential for copyright auditing and data governance, yet practical attribution should remain reliable under common post-processing and platform-induced processing. Existing reconstruction-based attribution methods summarize an image's reconstruction response to a single global scalar, such as the reconstruction error or the ratio of two consecutive errors. These summaries discard channel-frequency structure, allowing image content and post-processing variation to obscure source evidence and making attribution after spreading difficult. We instead uncover a more stable source-discriminative cue under post-processing: the frequency-resolved evolution of residual energy across repeated applications of the same reconstruction operator. Motivated by this insight, we characterize a phenomenon we term Spectral Contraction and use it to build SpCon, a source-attribution framework that accesses only the target model's VAE. SpCon obtains adjacent residuals through two deterministic reconstructions, builds a spectral-contraction representation from their frequency-resolved relative energy change, and aggregates Student-t response densities for each source-condition pair over a finite set of fitted reference components. The same representation supports three settings according to non-belonging reference availability: none (Target-Only, TO; belonging references only), external out-of-domain sources (External-Reference, ER), or known in-domain competitors (Known-Reference, KR). Across seven LGMs, TO, ER, and KR attain average AUROC/AP of 0.8786/0.6344, 0.9404/0.7886, and 0.9652/0.8626 over the 35 conditions, all outperforming the strongest baseline (0.8154/0.5277), and achieve 0.8581/0.5507, 0.9369/0.7549, and 0.9734/0.8773 on the three social platforms.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32074) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32074)

## 195. LoopDVR: Looped Transformer for Efficient Dense Visual Recognition

- **作者**：Sirui Liang, Xuanlong Yu, Longfei Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://siruuui.github.io/LoopDVR/ ; Github Repo: https://github.com/Intellindust-AI-Lab/LoopDVR

We propose LoopDVR, a framework for converting pretrained Transformer-based dense visual recognition models into parameter-efficient looped networks. Rather than reducing the network width or depth directly, LoopDVR reuses Transformer blocks across layers in both the ViT backbone and Transformer decoder, reducing the number of parameters while retaining the original computational structure. However, naively sharing parameters can disrupt the distinct transformations learned at different depths. To address this challenge, we identify suitable sharing groups based on inter-layer representation dissimilarity and keep the final backbone block independent for multi-scale feature construction. We then initialize each shared backbone block with the pretrained weights of its group's terminal layer to better preserve the learned representations. An independent Loop Adapter at each layer further helps retain depth-specific transformations within shared blocks. Subsequently, we distill visual representations into the looped backbone and integrate it with a looped decoder for task-specific training. Experiments demonstrate that LoopDVR achieves the best accuracy--parameter trade-offs across dense recognition tasks. Compared with the reference models, it reduces parameters by approximately 28% while maintaining competitive accuracy. Notably, LoopDet-S achieves 50.9 box AP with only 7.0M parameters, highlighting its effectiveness in compact models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32067) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32067)

## 196. SurgGenesis: A Generative Surgical World Model for Future-Aware Surgical Understanding

- **作者**：Qirui Zhong, Quande Liu, Yang Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Surgical scene understanding is constrained by data scarcity and long-tail class imbalance, as annotating surgical videos demands scarce clinical expertise while rare procedural events remain underrepresented. We tackle this through data generation and propose SurgGenesis, a generative surgical world model that learns future surgical state transitions in a latent space from visual observations and textual procedural descriptions, serving as a scalable surgical data engine. To adapt a large-scale video diffusion model to the data-scarce surgical domain without catastrophic forgetting, we introduce a three-stage progressive LoRA strategy that incrementally injects endoscopic visual priors, procedural semantics, and spatial motion cues. Rather than judging generation by visual fidelity alone, we validate SurgGenesis along two axes that separate a world model from a generic generator: (i) future-awareness, by comparing predicted futures against the real future through a GT-latent prediction gap and temporal-horizon analysis, and (ii) action-faithful usefulness, by checking that generated clips contain the commanded surgical triplet and that using them to rebalance long-tail events and drive downstream understanding (segmentation, triplet recognition, 3D reconstruction) yields action-specific gains. Conditioned futures therefore complement conventional long-tail sampling. Code: https://zh-qr.github.io/SurgGenesis

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32054) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32054)

## 197. SegPFN: Training-Free In-Context Segmentation with Tabular Prior-Data Fitted Networks

- **作者**：Jiajian Ma, Yihao Qiao, Ke Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, including 8 pages of supplementary material

In-context segmentation (ICS) uses one or a few annotated reference images to specify what to segment in a target image. Existing training-free ICS approaches typically rely on manually designed inference procedures, which may limit how flexibly reference information is combined. Learned in-context inference offers an adaptive alternative, but acquiring this capability through segmentation-specific training can compromise generalization to unseen domains. We therefore ask: can a pretrained in-context learner infer what to segment from annotated references without segmentation-specific training? We introduce SegPFN, a training-free framework that reformulates ICS as tabular classification over frozen visual features. A frozen tabular prior-data fitted network (PFN) uses labeled reference patches as context to classify patches in the target image. We comprehensively benchmark SegPFN against major existing ICS methods across 11 datasets spanning semantic, part, and personalized segmentation. SegPFN improves overall mean intersection-over-union (mIoU) by 4.7 points over the strongest overall baseline and achieves the highest average mIoU in four of five task-shot settings. Further analyses show that hard-negative reference patches improve fine-grained target discrimination, while joint conditioning enables SegPFN to use multiple references effectively. These results show that SegPFN offers a simple and effective paradigm for training-free in-context segmentation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32045) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32045)

## 198. Instant LiDAR: Feed-Forward Gaussian Reconstruction for LiDAR Simulation

- **作者**：Xin Kang, Jiahui Huang, Jiawei Ren et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

LiDAR reconstruction supports closed-loop autonomous driving simulation, but hours of per-scene optimization make large asset libraries costly to build and maintain. We present Instant LiDAR, a feed-forward pipeline that reconstructs multiple LiDAR scans as reusable Gaussian assets in minutes without per-scene optimization. LiDAR measurements provide strong geometric priors, but directly initializing Gaussians from them yields poor reconstruction due to nonuniform sampling and a limited field of view. Sparse returns leave surface gaps, while combining scans expands coverage but introduces redundant primitives. To address these challenges, Instant LiDAR combines measurement-anchored Gaussian prediction, sampling-aware Gaussian scales, and multi-frame consolidation. The predictor refines attributes anchored to recorded returns, while the scales adapt spatial support to LiDAR sampling. Multi-frame consolidation merges overlapping predictions into a compact asset spanning the driving log. Compared with NuRec, Instant LiDAR reconstructs scenes approximately 46 times faster on Physical AI (PAI) and 70 times faster on Waymo. It also achieves comparable downstream 3D detection accuracy on Waymo.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32042) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32042)

## 199. DeCo-Attention: Decompose to Compensate for Low-Precision Attention

- **作者**：Yuhang He, Harrison Zhang, Fanrong Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：30 pages, 14 figures, 30 tables

Self-attention over tens of thousands of tokens dominates the generation time of video diffusion transformers, because its cost grows quadratically with sequence length. The FP4 matrix products of modern GPUs can speed up attention, but the 4-bit rounding of the queries Q and the keys K introduces large errors. We show that the exponential in softmax amplifies the error of their product QK, which therefore dominates the attention error. We propose DeCo-Attention, a co-design of algorithm and system for low-precision attention. DeCo-Attention is training-free and compensates the rounding error of Q and K along a few principal directions found by a per-head eigendecomposition. The lightweight compensation terms are appended to Q and K as extra channels, so the FP4 matrix product corrects its own rounding error while it computes the attention scores. Once the products run in FP4, the softmax bounds the kernel, and we optimize the softmax so that the gain of FP4 computation translates into realized speedup. With QK in FP4, we implement DeCo-Attention with the rest of attention in FP8 on GB300 and VR200 and in FP4 on RTX PRO 6000. On Wan2.2, these kernels run 2.13x, 2.49x, and 2.82x faster than BF16 attention, and the whole generation runs 1.55x, 1.68x, and 1.80x faster. On four video generation models, the FP8 configuration achieves LPIPS comparable to prior 8-bit QK methods, and the fully FP4 configuration lower LPIPS than prior fully 4-bit kernels.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32035) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32035)

## 200. BodyWeave: Native Body-Conditioned 3D Garment Generation

- **作者**：Junyi Zhang, Yuanchuan Wang, Qichao Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Generating 3D garments for digital characters requires preserving a reference design while adapting the garment to the target body's shape and pose. Sewing-pattern methods can struggle to reproduce unfamiliar designs and fine details, while image-to-3D generators offer rich shape priors but typically lack explicit 3D body context. We present \textbf{BodyWeave}, a framework that unifies garment generation and fitting through native 3D body conditioning. Building on 3D generative priors, BodyWeave uses the body as a spatial reference throughout geometry generation: a \textbf{Global Body Adapter} guides garment placement and extent in the shared coordinate frame, while \textbf{Local Body Attention} guides surface refinement using nearby body geometry. A \textbf{Surface Penetration Loss} supervises decoded surfaces to reduce body intersections, and the resulting geometry conditions material synthesis to produce garments with physically based rendering attributes. To support learning across designs and wearers, we construct \textbf{GarmentWeave3D} with 293K aligned garment--body observations, including 114K same-design cross-body pairs. Evaluations on out-of-domain \textbf{Artist-Created Garments} demonstrate high geometric fidelity and low body penetration. Cross-body evaluations demonstrate accurate adaptation to new wearers with consistent garment--body spacing, while appearance comparisons show faithful fabric reproduction. Additional results and resources are available at https://gavin-jy.github.io/bodyweave-page/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32034) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32034)

## 201. Do Occupancy World Models Really Follow Actions?

- **作者**：Jongseong Bae, Ha Young Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Occupancy world models predict future 3D scenes conditioned on ego actions, yet their predictions may not faithfully follow the specified trajectory under counterfactual actions. Because driving datasets provide sparse coverage of alternative trajectories for each scene, existing models learn the spatial effect of ego motion implicitly from observed scene--trajectory combinations, which may entangle action effects with scene--trajectory correlations. We propose ReCast, an ego-trajectory-guided transformer that grounds future prediction in action-induced geometric correspondence across ego-centric frames. ReCast decouples historical encoding from future decoding and aligns deformable interactions using relative ego transformations, while retaining flexibility for non-rigid scene evolution. We also introduce a counterfactual action-faithfulness benchmark covering in-distribution, tail, and out-of-distribution trajectories. On Occ3D-nuScenes, ReCast achieves competitive standard forecasting performance while improving counterfactual forecasting, with particularly clear gains under out-of-distribution trajectories.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32029) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32029)

## 202. View Management with Gaussian Placement and Opacity Regularization for Online 3DGS

- **作者**：Dong Hwi Kim, Park Sangwon, Chaehyeon Song et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：17 pages, 5 figures, including supplementary material

The realistic rendering of 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has drawn interest from real-time applications that require maps to be built online. This led to coupling 3DGS with SLAM, providing camera poses and initial Gaussians for the map. However, the map is still optimized with designs inherited from conventional SLAM, which train only on keyframes selected for localization and regularize geometry with uncertain frontend depth. These designs overlook the model complexity of a 3DGS map: with millions of parameters but few views of each region, the map overfits to sparse inputs. Resolving this is challenging, as every view and Gaussian added to the map competes for the same real-time budget. We close this gap from both sides. To add views while keyframes preserve the structure of the map, we progressively admit non-keyframe images as mapping updates complete and allocate the limited updates across different groups of frames according to the supervision each group provides. To control the model complexity of the map, edge-guided initialization places new Gaussians where image detail requires them. To complement the uncertain frontend depth, Self-Referenced Opacity Regularization (SOR) uses accumulated rendering evidence, at no extra rendering cost, to suppress the opacity of Gaussians that persistently contribute in front of a map-derived surface reference. Together, these designs improve both the appearance and the geometry of online maps within the real-time budget.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32025) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32025)

## 203. IncrementDET: Continual AI-Generated Music Detection via Enrollment

- **作者**：Runbao Du, Wei Li, Qile He
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.SD, cs.CV, cs.LG, eess.AS
- **备注**：17 pages, 3 figures

AI-generated music has become common on music streaming platforms, and listeners often cannot distinguish it from music made by humans. AI-music detection is therefore needed, but detectors trained on a fixed set of generators often fail on unseen generators. As new generators emerge frequently, updating a universal detector, a single model for all generators, typically requires substantial training data and computation, while risking performance degradation on previously learned generators. We therefore introduce IncrementDET, a detector that learns each new generator by a process called enrollment. For each generator, IncrementDET trains one linear classifier, which we call a needle, to separate samples of that generator from a fixed set of human reference tracks. Enrolling a new generator adds one needle while the previously constructed needles remain unchanged. A Sidak correction keeps the overall false-positive rate at its target as needles are added. To compare detectors under these conditions, we define the task of continual AI-generated music detection and introduce a benchmark in which generators arrive one at a time. The benchmark measures the accuracy of a detector before and after each update and the cost of each update. For this benchmark, we also construct EvoMusicFake-17K, a dataset of instrumental music from six recent generators and four human music platforms. Compared with SpecTTTra, a state-of-the-art universal detector, IncrementDET learns a new generator more accurately from only a few samples, generalizes better to unseen generators and better preserves detection performance on previously learned generators, at a substantially lower update cost. The structure of IncrementDET can also be applied to the detection of generated images and video, where new generators are released just as frequently.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32023) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32023)

## 204. Beyond Before-and-After Images: Evaluating Temporal Grounding in a Lightweight VLM for Disaster Assessment

- **作者**：Shubh Garg, Pratham Arora, Debabrata Ghosh
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：13 pages (4 main + references + supplementary), 4 figures, 14 tables. Accepted at the CV4EO Workshop, WACV 2027

Bi-temporal satellite imagery is central to disaster assessment: deciding what was damaged requires comparing a pre-event scene with its post-event counterpart. A vision-language model (VLM) can nonetheless appear to answer change questions while relying mainly on the post-event image or on the prompt's temporal framing. We present a controlled diagnostic of temporal grounding and apply it to one lightweight VLM, InternVL3-2B, on DisasterM3: 1,659 optical and SAR image pairs from 41 events and nine disaster types, in event-disjoint partitions, with 9,031 reasoning questions. We compare post-only input, the true pre/post pair, the post image repeated twice, reversed image order, and an unrelated pre-event image. The true pair scores $+0.5$ accuracy points above the repeated-post control (95\% paired bootstrap CI $[-0.2, +1.2]$), and reversing the order changes accuracy by $-0.2$ points ($[-0.8, +0.5]$). Under this protocol we therefore find no measurable benefit from the content of the true pre-event image and little sensitivity to temporal order. The model is not blind to the first image, however: the true pair scores $2.5$ points above a pair whose pre-event image comes from another event ($[+1.6, +3.4]$). State-transition and multi-hop questions remain hard, and optical and SAR inputs show different profiles. We outline temporal scene graphs as a testable next step, not a validated solution, and argue that temporal benchmarks for Earth observation should report whether answers depend on the intended evidence, not only how often they are right.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32022) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32022)

## 205. HuluOPD: Unifying Heterogeneous Medical Specialists into One Lightweight Model with Unlabeled Prompts

- **作者**：Yuncheng Zhang, Songtao Jiang, Yitian Tang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL
- **备注**：18 pages, 6 figures, 12 tables

Specialist medical vision-language models (VLMs) are strong within their specialties but hard to unify: their training data are often private, and independently developed specialists cannot be assumed to share an architecture. Deploying these large models separately is costly. We present HuluOPD, an on-policy distillation recipe that unifies heterogeneous specialists into one lightweight VLM using only unlabeled prompts, without requiring reference answers or the specialists' training data. The lightweight model answers each prompt, and the specialist of the prompt's modality supervises its response; a continuation-aligned cross-tokenizer loss makes this possible even when they differ in architecture and tokenizer. We apply HuluOPD to five specialists, for ultrasound, pathology, optical coherence tomography, fundus photography, and dental imaging. To distill and evaluate them, we build HuluOPDCorpus, 914k unlabeled prompts, and HuluOPDBench, 47k held-out test items, from over 90 public datasets, and score all models under one protocol on 132k test items, including third-party benchmarks. A single model with 10% of the five specialists' combined parameters retains 94% of their question-answering performance, and one with 2% retains 89%. To our knowledge, this is the first demonstration of unifying heterogeneous medical specialist VLMs without requiring their training data or a shared architecture.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32021) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32021)

## 206. Learning Agent States for Latent Action Modeling

- **作者**：Shaoan Xie, Fan Feng, Peiyuan Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：27 pages, 12 figures, 20 tables. Project page: https://agent-state-lam.github.io

Latent action models (LAMs) learn action-like latents from unlabeled video by coupling frame-pair inference with latent-conditioned prediction, enabling policy and world-model pretraining. However, what the latent must explain depends on the representation space used for action inference and transition prediction. When this space entangles the agent's evolving physical state with visual context, such as background and persistent agent appearance, inferred latents can encode information unrelated to the agent's actions. To address this challenge, we propose Agent-State LAM, which jointly learns a compact agent-state representation and latent actions modeling its transitions. The compact state aggregates dense visual features into a shared space for both action inference and forward prediction. To guide what this state retains, we introduce spatially matched temporal discrimination (SMTD), which trains the state to distinguish its source frame from nearby frames through spatially flexible matching with frozen local visual features. Rather than comparing global frame representations, SMTD scores each frame by emphasizing its strongest local feature matches across spatial locations. This scoring allows background and persistent agent appearance to remain matched across frames despite displacement, making these shared cues less informative for identifying the source frame and encouraging the representation to capture the agent's evolving physical state. Extensive experiments on distracting control tasks and six real robot video datasets demonstrate improved action recovery, downstream control, and world-model prediction. Code and checkpoints are available at https://github.com/Mid-Push/Agent-State-LAM.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32017) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32017)

## 207. RoboHarness-RL: Agentic Reinforcement Learning for VLM-Based Robot Manipulation

- **作者**：Liyang Li, Mingyu Liu, Xiaoman Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Agentic harnesses enable frontier vision-language models (VLMs) to manipulate robots through perception, planning, and control tools, yet achieving comparable performance with compact open-weight VLMs remains challenging. We present RoboHarness-RL, an agentic reinforcement learning framework for improving VLM-based robot manipulation through tool calls. Starting from supervised fine-tuning on successful teacher demonstrations, the VLM further improves through multi-turn reinforcement learning with task-outcome rewards, without relying on vision-language-action models or world action models. To support efficient multi-turn training, we develop LiteHarness, which uses hierarchical memory to keep interaction contexts compact while preserving task performance. With Qwen3.5-9B, RL raises success from 31.2% to 44.0% on RoboTwin 2.0 and from 57.8% to 73.3% on LIBERO-Pro; these gains extend to randomized RoboTwin scenes unseen during training (26.4% to 38.0%). Behavioral analysis further shows that RL reduces repetitive-call rates by up to $4\times$, shortens successful executions, and improves recovery from negative feedback. Project page: https://aim-uofa.github.io/RoboHarness-RL/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32016) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32016)

## 208. DynaCue: Comparing Motion and Timing Between Groups of Videos

- **作者**：Hao Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A score that distinguishes videos with different temporal-consistency ratings can miss differences associated with motion preference. VideoScore's temporal consistency score distinguished higher- from lower-rated Pika videos in 95% of comparisons with 15 videos per group, yet could not detect the difference between preferred videos and their same-prompt alternatives in any of the 200 comparisons of 41 VideoGen-RewardBench pairs. We introduce DynaCue, a two-sample permutation test that combines complementary measurements of motion, frame continuity, and appearance to compare groups of videos without choosing a single cue in advance. DynaCue measures local changes in optical flow both before and after accounting for each clip's usual activity, captures interruptions through frame differences and freeze counts, and incorporates scores for motion and appearance. In controlled experiments, DynaCue detected group differences across five interventions affecting playback continuity, displacement, or rhythm. In real-world evaluations of generated videos where viewers rated temporal consistency or chose which of two videos they preferred for its motion, DynaCue detected group differences in at least 80% of resampled comparisons on each of the three datasets at the smaller sample sizes studied. Every other method evaluated on all three had a detection rate of 60% or less on at least one dataset.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32014) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32014)

## 209. Distractors are the Real Enemy: Visual Distraction Adversarial Perturbations on SAM2-Based Video Tracking and Segmentation

- **作者**：Adonay Demewez Gebremedhin, Mohamad Alansari, Wessam Shehieb et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Promptable video trackers such as SAM2 achieve accurate and robust performance through strong temporal consistency, making them important foundations for downstream video understanding and tracking applications. As their use continues to expand, understanding their robustness to adversarial perturbations becomes increasingly important. Existing attacks on SAM2 largely focus on local mask degradation and short-term temporal feature disruption, leaving its longer-term dynamics and competing object interferences (Distractors) insufficiently explored. We observe that SAM2 itself exposes useful distractor cues when tracking becomes uncertain. Based on these observations, we propose DistractAttack, a white-box attack that uses background distractors to disrupt target tracking persistently. Its core procedure, Counterfactual Distractor Search (CDS), propagates clean and attacked trajectories in parallel to discover plausible competing regions and identify those that become increasingly effective under perturbation. The discovered evidence is accumulated over time to weaken target support and promote recurrent alternatives or disappearance predictions. Extensive experiments under video-level and one-shot perturbations across Visual Object Tracking and Video Object Segmentation demonstrate strong attack effectiveness, raising DRE to $4.4\times$ and NRE to $7.3\times$ their nearest baselines under video perturbation, while transferring effectively across model scales and tracker variants in black-box settings. Project page: https://adonaydem.github.io/sam2-da-page/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32011) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32011)

## 210. Change the Base, Change the Game: Rethinking Pan-sharpening

- **作者**：Jeonghyeok Do, Geunhyuk Youk, Munchurl Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：The first two authors contributed equally to this work (equal contribution). Please visit our project page at this https://kaist-viclab.github.io/PanBase_site/

Many pan-sharpening (PS) networks reconstruct high-resolution multispectral (HRMS) images by adding learned residuals to 4$\times$-interpolated low-resolution multispectral (LRMS) inputs, incorporating panchromatic (PAN) detail only through these residual corrections. This raises a simple question: Why start with a blurry base when sharp spatial detail is already available? We propose PanBase, a sharper multispectral starting point designed for more effective residual learning. Guided by physical sensor characteristics, PanBase combines PAN spatial detail with LRMS spectral information while enforcing consistency with the LRMS observations under sensor-specific blur. We construct this base without neural network training, estimating only nine coefficients per spectral band through closed-form calibration. Our PanBase can be easily integrated into existing backbones with a global residual skip through two simple changes: (i) replacing the skip base with PanBase and (ii) concatenating it as an additional input. Using each backbone's original training recipe and matched training budgets, PanBase improves most reduced- and full-resolution metrics on WorldView-3, QuickBird, and GaoFen-2, while achieving 4.4$\times$ faster training convergence on average. In zero-shot evaluation on WorldView-2, standalone PanBase achieves competitive reconstruction without target-sensor fitting or neural refinement.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32002) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32002)

## 211. Reconstructing Natural Images from Brain Activity during Natural Vision

- **作者**：Tejasvi Kothapalli, Ryan Ressmeyer, Declan Rowley et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：40 pages, 18 figures, 4 tables. Includes supplementary material

Reconstructing images from brain activity has two broad goals. The first is to build brain-computer interfaces. The second is to understand how the brain sees. The eyes deliver incomplete, noisy input, so the brain must fill in the gaps with its expectations of what natural scenes look like. This filling-in relies on the brain's prior over natural images. Image reconstruction lets us test which natural image prior best matches the brain's natural image prior. However, most previous work on image reconstruction relies on an unnatural condition: subjects must hold their eyes still. In reality, primates constantly move their eyes to see the world. We introduce a framework for reconstructing images from neural activity while a primate freely views a static natural scene. We record from five monkeys with high-density electrode arrays in the lateral geniculate nucleus (LGN) and primary visual cortex (V1). The recorded receptive fields cover only a small part of the image. Thus, we combine neural activity across eye movements, trials, and sessions to reconstruct the full image.
