We first present a simple linear method with a 1/f prior. We then scale this approach up with modern deep learning. We train a decoder by tokenizing spiking activity and measured gaze; these tokens are fed into a transformer and diffusion model to reconstruct the full scene. Three factors determine reconstruction quality: the amount of neural evidence combined over viewing time, the accuracy of eye tracking, and the choice of image prior. Our method sets a new state-of-the-art performance in reconstructing natural images from brain activity, across brain recording modalities: fMRI, EEG, MEG and electrophysiology.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31989) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31989)

## 212. Describe, Don't Decide: Correcting Answer Bias in Multimodal LLMs

- **作者**：Shuwei Wu, Longin Jan Latecki
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Comparing two entities in an image, multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) often answer wrongly. We find that the answer bias enters not when the model sees, but when it decides with the image in view: the model's own descriptions of the two entities often already hold the right answer. We therefore propose D$^3$ (Describe, Don't Decide): the model describes each entity alone, decides from these descriptions without the image, and merges this decision with its direct answer. Without any training, D$^3$ improves all 79 model-benchmark pairs across ten benchmarks and eight 7-9B MLLMs. On held-out GQA comparison questions, the mean accuracy of these models rises from 72.4% to 83.2% (up to +21.5% per model), higher than that of Qwen2.5-VL-32B answering directly (82.8%). Code will be available at https://github.com/paul-swu/describe-dont-decide.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31988) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31988)

## 213. InCue: Making Better Use of What Multimodal LLMs Already See at Test Time

- **作者**：Shuwei Wu, Siyue Yu, Longin Jan Latecki
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Even when multimodal large language models (MLLMs) answer incorrectly, their internal cues often point to the relevant region. A widely used remedy re-presents the cue-selected region as an enlarged crop to supply finer detail. However, we find that much of its gain requires no new detail: it largely survives when the crop comes from the already-seen low-resolution input, and partly even when the region's cached tokens are merely copied. Part of re-presentation's gain thus comes from drawing attention to evidence the model already holds. We propose InCue, a test-time framework that makes better use of this evidence without additional visual input. InCue treats visual attention and predictive-entropy gradients as Internal Cues, couples them into a spatial target, and optimizes only an offset on the existing visual tokens to steer attention toward it. Under matched visual-token budgets, InCue achieves state-of-the-art performance, consistently outperforming recent test-time methods and improving four MLLMs by up to 8.9% on V$^*$ and 23.4% on its hard samples. Its gains carry over to eight general benchmarks. Code will be available at https://github.com/paul-swu/InCue.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31987) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31987)

## 214. WaveSR: Physics-Inspired Wave Evolution Attention for Efficient Image Super-Resolution

- **作者**：Xiaoyan Lei, Biao Luo, Wenlong Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：This paper has been accepted by IEEE Transactions on Multimedia

Recently, physics-inspired neural networks have gained widespread attention due to their interpretability. The closed-form, frequency-time decoupled solution of the wave equation offers linear complexity and allows different frequency components to evolve independently, making it a potential alternative solution for efficient image super-resolution. Therefore, a physics-inspired wave evolution attention framework is proposed, namely WaveSR. The WaveSR framework involves two key modules, i.e., wave evolution attention (WEA) and Frequency-Adaptive Feed-Forward Network (FA-FFN). The WEA module achieves feature interactions through the analytical solution of the 2D wave equation in the Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) domain, enabling independent evolution of different frequency components with learnable wave speed \(c\) and damping coefficient \(α\). The FA-FFN module realizes adaptive frequency feature selection through learnable spectral modulation, effectively enhancing feature representation capability. Subsequently, we employ a two-stage training strategy with a hybrid objective that combines a spatial-domain pixel loss and a frequency-domain loss to improve reconstruction quality. Our method provides a preliminary exploration of physics-inspired neural networks for efficient image super-resolution.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31984) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31984)

## 215. Distill to Loop: Turning Pretrained Diffusion Transformers into Looped Models with Adjustable Depth

- **作者**：Chenru Wang, Shengwei An
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Looped transformers reuse one block of shared weights along the depth of the network. Recent looped diffusion transformers are trained from scratch, which repeats the full pretraining cost although strong pretrained non-looped diffusion transformers exist. We propose Distill to Loop (Dooop), which distills a pretrained non-looped diffusion transformer into a looped student whose blocks are initialized from the teacher's. We cast distillation as a fixed-point iteration toward the teacher, in which the output after every loop is trained toward the teacher, and each loop is one step of an ordinary differential equation in depth. As a result, one model runs any number of loops at test time without retraining, and the higher-order ODE solvers of diffusion model sampling can be applied along the depth dimension for better results. Meanwhile, because a looped model produces an output after every loop, we also construct loop guidance between the outputs of different loop counts, which improves the generated samples at little extra compute. On SD3-medium, a looped student with about 37% of the teacher's parameters matches the teacher on average over GenEval, ImageReward, PickScore and HPSv2.1 with two to four loops, and with two loops it uses about 58% of the teacher's compute. And on a four-step SD3-medium, loop guidance raises the average of the four metrics from 88.2 to 99.2.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31977) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31977)

## 216. CodeHOI: Code with Agents for Physically Plausible 4D Hand-Object Interaction

- **作者**：Seonghwan Park, Inho Chang, Min-Gyu Park
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Preprint. Project page: https://shpark97.github.io/CodeHOI/

Modeling 4D hand-object interaction (HOI) is fundamental to understanding manipulation. This requires physics, since hands move objects only by touch and each material responds differently. Yet existing methods ignore this physics or simulate only a narrow range of matter. Programs can instead describe any matter with its physics, but coding agents omit the hand that drives the interaction. We close this gap with CodeHOI, to our knowledge the first agentic framework that writes physically plausible 4D HOI as programs. To write one, an Object Agent first builds each object with the physics of its matter. An HOI Agent then specifies the hands by their contacts and simulates each kind of matter with an established physics solver. Finally, an Evaluator Agent checks each result against physical laws. We also introduce CodeHOI-Bench with 100 reconstruction and generation tasks across four kinds of matter. With off-the-shelf frontier models, CodeHOI writes interactions that are plausible for every kind of matter and remain so when transferred to an independent physics engine. It thereby enables coding agents to write physically plausible HOI across diverse matter.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31976) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31976)

## 217. Visual Thinking: Training-Free Active Perception for Counting and Localizing Hard-to-Find Objects

- **作者**：Emil Kielar, Pratik Chaudhari
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision foundation models can precisely segment objects based solely on a text prompt. However, they struggle to recover objects that are small, occluded or whose appearance is poorly captured by the original textual description. Vision language models can examine the image and its context, they can propose richer descriptions of the objects to be detected. But these models struggle with precise segmentation and localization. We introduce Visual Thinking, a training-free framework that combines a vision-language model with Segment Anything Model 3 (SAM 3) through an iterative active perception loop. At each iteration, the VLM proposes short noun phrases using the image, candidate crops and history from past iterations. A controller ranks these phrases using embedding similarity between the original prompt and the proposal, an entropy-based verification priority and the novelty of the prompt. Next, SAM 3 uses prompts to detect objects in the image. We associate detections across iterations and update each object's confidence score using a weighted mean of scores obtained from SAM 3 with a larger weight for detections using narrower prompts. We supply confident detections as positive visual exemplars for SAM 3, and incorporate image tiling to recover small objects in cluttered images. We evaluate counting and localization on CARPK, FSCD-147, PixMo-Count, and OmniCount (Fruits). Compared with SAM 3, Visual Thinking reduces the counting mean absolute error (MAE) by about 18 on FSC-147 and about 8 on CARPK. It improves box average precision (AP) by more than 12 on FSCD-147 and CARPK. Visual Thinking, with fixed or no tiling, performs on par with Claude Opus 5.5 at extra-high reasoning effort, while running on single desktop GPU in about 5.91 seconds per image, compared to about 46.53 seconds per image for Claude through its API.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31973) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31973)

## 218. SPAR: Self-Referenced World-Model Rewards for Physical Alignment in Video Generation

- **作者**：Junyi Zhang, Qichao Wang, Wenzhe Qian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Generating videos with physically plausible dynamics remains challenging despite advances in visual quality. Latent world models offer predictive signals for evaluating physical plausibility, but higher agreement between predicted and observed features may reflect easier-to-predict scene content rather than more plausible dynamics. We propose \textbf{S}elf-Referenced \textbf{P}hysical \textbf{A}lignment \textbf{R}ewards (\textbf{SPAR}), a framework that uses world-model predictions for inference-time physical alignment. SPAR compares each video's prediction error with that of static clips constructed by repeating its own frames, providing a content-matched reference for evaluating generated dynamics. To account for the uneven spatial and temporal distribution of physical interactions, we introduce a unified \textbf{spatiotemporal reward refinement} strategy. \textbf{Spatial refinement} emphasizes localized prediction discrepancies, while \textbf{temporal refinement} moderates brief error peaks and supplements prediction errors with agreement between predicted and observed feature changes. Evaluations across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that SPAR improves the physical plausibility of both \textbf{text-to-video generation} and \textbf{image-to-video continuation}, bringing generated dynamics into closer alignment with the physical regularities governing real-world events. Additional results and resources are available at https://gavin-jy.github.io/spar-page/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31958) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31958)

## 219. TACTUS: Unifying Surgical Action Understanding in Time and Space

- **作者**：Lurui Wang, Hao Ding, Chenhao Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：32 pages, 11 figures, including supplementary material. Lurui Wang and Hao Ding contributed equally. Mathias Unberath is the corresponding author. Project page: https://ruifreddie.github.io/TACTUS/

Advancing surgical automation requires a comprehensive spatio-temporal understanding of how instruments act on tissue throughout an action. Existing surgical benchmarks, however, largely define supervision through task-specific targets, leaving the relationships between action semantics, instrument motion, and tissue interaction incompletely specified. To address this limitation, we introduce TACTUS (Tool, Action, Contact and Trajectory Understanding in Surgery), a new dataset and benchmark that establishes a unified annotation framework for complete surgical actions. The framework jointly specifies tool and action identities, nested action and interaction windows, instrument-specific landmark trajectories, and tissue-region tracks. To construct these annotations at scale, we develop a two-tier pipeline combining human annotation, point tracking, and model-generated spatial prompts. Using this pipeline, we re-annotate 141 videos from five public sources, producing 22,995 action clips spanning 2.9 million frames. The dataset comprises 15,668 Gold clips with human spatial prompts followed by review and re-checking, and 7,327 Silver clips combining human semantic and temporal annotations with model-generated spatial prompts. We further introduce TactusNet, the companion reference network, to benchmark semantic, temporal, and spatial prediction from shared video features. Across TactusNet and 13 published baselines, tool-action accuracy reaches 95.8% with per-head encoder fine-tuning, while the best temporal recall and contact Dice reach 46.6% at temporal IoU 0.7 and 0.351, respectively. Moreover, adding Silver improves the default TactusNet's pair accuracy from 93.8% to 95.5%. Together, these contributions establish a foundation for learning and evaluating the instrument-tissue interactions underlying future surgical automation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31956) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31956)

## 220. VLG-Drive: Unifying Vision-Language and Geometric Priors for Autonomous Driving

- **作者**：Junge Zhang, Jie Zhu, Yanqi Jia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：19 pages, 5 figures, 16 tables

A driving planner needs both scene semantics, which vision-language models (VLMs) provide, and spatial structure, which geometric foundation models recover from visual history. We present VLG-Drive, which keeps both models frozen and trains only a fusion interface and a planner from trajectory labels. At each camera-grid cell, the interface fuses the paired VLM and geometry features into one joint token through a geometry-anchored residual, and a command-conditioned planner reads these tokens to predict the ego trajectory. VLG-Drive achieves 31.78 EPDMS on NavHard under perturbed initial states, the highest among the compared methods, and 88.27 PDMS on NavTest from cameras alone. Pointwise fusion outperforms independent VLM and geometry streams on both benchmarks. In closed-loop driving on 420 HUGSIM scenarios, VLG-Drive outperforms all compared planners. These results indicate that a lightweight planner trained on fused features of frozen vision-language and geometric foundation models is an effective alternative to adapting either model for planning.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31952) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31952)

## 221. Beyond Rigid Hands: Deforming Skin into Contact-Consistent Hand Meshes

- **作者**：Seonghwan Park, Min-Gyu Park
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Preprint. Project page: https://shpark97.github.io/BeyondRigidHands/

Hands grasp objects with soft skin. A fingertip flattens against a mug, and a palm curves around a bottle. Yet hand-object datasets and estimators represent the hand as a rigid articulated mesh, which hovers above or penetrates the object where skin would press against it. Existing refiners keep the mesh rigid and adjust only the pose, trading pose accuracy for physical plausibility. We ask whether a rigid hand can instead touch the object as skin does while staying close to its original pose. We lift annotated or estimated rigid hands to contact-consistent meshes through an optimization that (i) slightly adjusts the joints and wrist to bring the fingers onto the object, (ii) deforms the skin so that penetrating regions settle onto the surface, and (iii) keeps this deformation consistent over time. Since deformation reaches only skin already near the object, we further train a lightweight head on the lifted annotations to predict where an estimated hand should touch and steer the deformation there. Unlike existing refiners, the lift more than doubles the skin lying on the object surface for three published estimators while barely changing the pose. Guidance from the learned head further improves grasp stability by closing contact gaps that the estimate leaves open. Existing hand-object datasets and estimators can thus adopt contact-consistent hands without changing the hand model or retraining the estimators.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31950) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31950)

## 222. DraftKV: Protecting Persistent Worlds through Hierarchical Streaming Retrieval

- **作者**：Xiaoling Zhou, Haopeng Li, Yufa Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Includes supplementary material. Code and project page are available online

Interactive video world models enable action- and camera-controlled exploration, yet struggle to maintain consistent spatial geometry and appearance when revisiting previously explored locations. Historical KV caches help auto-regressive video diffusion models maintain long-term consistency, but their retrieval and attention computation introduce substantial latency as history grows. We propose DraftKV, a training-free framework that retrieves historical KV caches based on geometric grounding and query-conditioned relevance, while combining CPU offloading with tile-wise streaming attention to reduce GPU memory usage and inference latency. Theoretically, we show that draft relevance reproduces the mean branch of full-resolution block relevance exactly, so its deviation is confined to the $β$-weighted max branch, and we bound retrieval utility loss in terms of the measured Top-K selection overlap. We further show that draft relevance cannot distinguish blocks with similar pooled descriptors regardless of camera pose, motivating geometric grounding before refinement. Then, we introduce streaming CG-Attention, which overlaps CPU-to-GPU KV transfers with attention computation through tile-wise streaming, hiding transfer latency while maintaining bounded GPU memory usage. Experiments across diverse model families show that DraftKV achieves superior generation quality and consistency while achieving $3.27\times$ end-to-end speedup and 42.2% peak GPU memory reduction over other CPU-offloaded KV cache retrieval methods. Notably, DraftKV enables high-quality interactive world generation with historical KV retrieval on a single H100 GPU using SolarWM-33B.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31944) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31944)

## 223. Decompose, Verify, Evolve: Physically Grounded Shape Decomposition

- **作者**：Wenchao Ma, Rui Yu, Fan Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Shape decomposition represents 3D objects as a compact set of geometric primitives for interpretable modeling and simulation. However, existing decomposition methods are trained primarily for geometric fidelity, without explicit constraints on physical plausibility, and can therefore produce disconnected parts or physically unstable reconstructions. We present PhysDec, an agentic framework that draws on a pretrained vision-language model's semantic understanding and commonsense reasoning to predict material-aware primitive decompositions from a few images. Within this framework, PhysDec-Loop is an agentic pipeline that reasons about part structure and materials, using a reference 3D point cloud for geometric feedback and physical verifiers to guide iterative refinement. Through self-evolution, we further develop another agentic pipeline, PhysDec-Evolved, which learns from prior corrections to predict primitive decompositions from images without point-cloud feedback or a correction loop at inference. Experiments show that PhysDec-Loop improves geometric fidelity, physical plausibility and visual quality over geometry-focused baselines, while PhysDec-Evolved approaches its performance at substantially lower inference cost.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31924) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31924)

## 224. TANDEM: Temporal Anchor Densification With Reliability-Aware Memory for Lightweight Sparse Depth Completion in Endoscopic Robotic Navigation

- **作者**：Weikuan Qiu, Jiale Hou, Chongzhao Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Submitted to IEEE Transactions on Medical Imaging, 2026. 11 pages, 3 figures, 4 tables

For endoscopic robotic surgery, accurate dense depth perception serves as a foundational capability, which enables navigation, instrument localization, and tissue interaction. However, endoscopic video lacks reliable metric depth, while metric measurements obtained via tracking, time-of-flight sensing, or instrument contact only cover a small fraction of pixels in each frame, rendering sparse depth completion an urgent and unsolved challenge. In this work, we propose Temporal Anchor Densification with Reliability-Aware Memory (TANDEM), a causal sparse depth completion framework for endoscopic video. It adopts a lightweight design and leverages the video stream: past measurements re-projected into the current view recover the metric evidence unavailable within a single sparse frame. To keep this evidence interpretable, accurate, and efficient, an explicit memory scores each re-projected measurement with analytic re-observation, age, and motion-parallax factors without overriding current measurements. A compact network then reconstructs depth via reliability-weighted gradient integration and a frequency-gated reference prior. We evaluate TANDEM on the C3VD colonoscopy and StereoMIS laparoscopy datasets. With only 5.58 M parameters, TANDEM reduces the root mean square error by 11.7% and 32.0%, respectively, compared with the strongest baseline, and achieves state-of-the-art accuracy. It also remains accurate under pose errors of the magnitude reported for endoscopic pose estimation. Code will be released at https://github.com/Wk-cpu-art/TANDEM upon acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31922) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31922)

## 225. DUALingo: A Dual-Hand Instruction-Guided Dataset for Affordance Grounding on 3D Objects

- **作者**：Ming-Lun Lee, Yi-En Li, Feng-Dian Su et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：23 pages, 15 figures. Project page: https://nycu-pair-labs.github.io/DUALingo/

Affordance grounding localizes where an agent should interact with an object to perform a task. Many everyday manipulation tasks, however, require bimanual affordance, in which two hands assume complementary and cooperative roles to accomplish a shared goal. Existing 3D affordance datasets primarily ground single interaction regions, and manually annotating dual-hand, cooperative contacts does not scale. To address this gap, we introduce DUALingo, a large-scale dataset for task-conditioned, role-aware bimanual 3D affordance grounding on diverse everyday objects. DUALingo represents each task as two cooperative, hand-agnostic roles, each grounded as a heatmap on the object surface. It contains 271K free-form tasks on 71K objects, generated by a fully automated pipeline of open-weight foundation models without human labeling. Expert ratings and a validated LLM judge confirm the quality of the resulting annotations. Trained on DUALingo, existing 3D affordance models learn to ground both cooperative roles from natural-language instructions, with the best-performing model achieving a more than threefold improvement in aIoU over the zero-shot baseline, from 10.3 to 37.8. Robotic manipulation demonstrations illustrate the actionability of the predicted bimanual affordances in performing cooperative tasks. These results position DUALingo as a scalable foundation for learning everyday bimanual affordances. Project page: https://nycu-pair-labs.github.io/DUALingo/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31919) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31919)

## 226. HiGS: A Hierarchical Architecture for Fast Differentiable 3D Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Dawid Pająk, Martin Bisson, Rodolfo Lima et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR

3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) makes one screen tile the cell of partitioning, the cell of rasterization, and the unit of parallel work. Partitioning favors large tiles and rasterization small ones, and with one unit per tile the densest tiles set the frame time. We present Hierarchically Tiled Gaussian Splatting (HiGS), a unified architecture for rendering and reconstruction that gives each role its own granularity: Gaussians are partitioned over coarse macro-tiles, rasterized over the fine render tiles within them, and processed in bounded depth-sorted batches. A dense region issues more batches instead of one longer unit, so frame time follows the work in a view rather than its densest tile. The same batches carry reconstruction: compositing supplies the transmittance and color needed to differentiate each one independently, leaving optimizer memory traffic as the dominant training cost. We fuse the optimizer into the backward pass, halve the parameters and moments it stores and updates directly, and defer appearance updates outside the view. HiGS renders scenes faster than every published method we evaluate, by the widest margin on views with uneven load, and reconstructs them faster and in less memory, at equal image quality and with exact front-to-back alpha compositing.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31918) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31918)

## 227. Your VLM Knows What to Inspect: Semantic Probe-Guided Adaptive Focusing for GUI Grounding

- **作者**：Ruixiao Shi, Fu Feng, Yucheng Xie et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

GUI grounding requires resolving fine visual details within the context that gives them meaning. Additional views can improve localization, but their value depends on what evidence they expose and how much context they preserve. We explore a capability of vision-language models (VLMs): even when their coordinate predictions are incorrect, they can describe visual evidence worth inspecting. We introduce \textbf{SPARK}, \textit{\textbf{S}emantic \textbf{P}robe-guided \textbf{A}daptive \textbf{R}egion see\textbf{K}ing}, a training-free framework that turns this semantic guidance into targeted visual observations. Exploration probes describe relevant context and distinguishing target details, while verification probes identify evidence needed to assess emerging hypotheses. Their spatial attention guides view placement, contextual responses determine crop extent, and observation history encourages complementary inspection. The resulting predictions are combined through uncertainty-weighted voting. On ScreenSpot-Pro, SPARK improves grounding accuracy over single-view inference by 13.9, 25.2, and 16.1 percentage points with Qwen3-VL, Qwen2.5-VL, and UI-TARS, outperforming all compared test-time methods for each backbone. Consistent gains across four additional benchmarks demonstrate its effectiveness across diverse interfaces and grounding tasks. These results establish semantic probing as an effective mechanism for turning a frozen VLM's understanding of what to inspect into more accurate GUI grounding.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31909) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31909)

## 228. Beyond Expert Replay: Cross-Instruction Anchoring for Continual Vision-Language-Action Learning

- **作者**：Ziyang Chen, Dehua Chen, Shaoguang Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：19 pages, 12 figures, 10 tables, including supplementary material

Vision-language-action (VLA) models need to continually acquire new manipulation skills while retaining the ability to follow previously learned instructions. Experience replay (ER) can mitigate catastrophic forgetting, but retaining only a small number of historical expert frames per task leaves many old-instruction responses without direct supervision. Under limited replay, we observe a structured failure mode in which an earlier instruction can trigger a subsequently learned, competing goal. To address this gap, we propose Cross-Instruction Anchoring (XIA), which supplements expert replay with instruction-conditioned response preservation. At each task boundary, XIA freezes the previous policy as a teacher and matches its responses to old instructions paired with current observations. This extends supervision beyond stored historical examples without requiring additional expert replay data. Experiments across multiple VLA architectures and three LIBERO suites demonstrate improved success rates and reduced forgetting compared with ER under identical historical expert-data budgets. Additional RoboCasa and real-world experiments further support the effectiveness of XIA.

## 229. Loupe: Reinforcing Knowledge-Intensive Video Generation Agents

- **作者**：Kai Zeng, Ruichuan An, Zhen Fang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Knowledge-intensive video generation remains challenging because prompts often omit the facts, appearances, and temporal relationships required for accurate generation. While search-based workflows can retrieve missing evidence, manually designed strategies limit adaptive planning, and conventional training suffers from costly video-level feedback and coarse trajectory-level credit assignment. In this paper, we present Loupe, a video generation agent that retrieves and verifies textual and visual evidence to construct evidence-grounded temporal video prompts with reference images. We further introduce Rubric-Based Credit (RBC), which leverages prompt-specific factual, visual, and temporal requirements to evaluate intermediate search and planning decisions before video rendering. Building on RBC, RBC-SFT reweights assistant-token learning by step quality, while RBC-GRPO incorporates step-level credits alongside a final planning reward, without rendering videos during RL. To address the lack of dedicated search-oriented evaluation, we construct Search-VBench, which contains 150 held-out prompts across seven categories. Experiments show that Loupe improves all four evaluation dimensions across three video generators, raising the overall score of MiniMax-H3 from 32.80 to 70.67 and outperforming an untrained search workflow. Ablation experiments show that full RBC training outperforms standard SFT followed by trajectory-level GRPO by 5.27 points. Human evaluations and results on Sci-VBench and KIVI-Bench further demonstrate improved generation quality, scientific grounding, factual precision, and helpfulness. Our project page is available at https://zengkaiya.github.io/Loupe/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31895) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31895)

## 230. Open-OmniCoach: Evidence-Enhanced Trajectory Learning for Omni Video Agents

- **作者**：Weisong Liu, Xiangtai Li, Haochen Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：24 pages. Project Page: https://open-omnicoach.github.io , Code: https://github.com/Open-OmniCoach/omnicoach , Models & Data: https://huggingface.co/Open-OmniCoach

Training capable omni video agents requires more than diverse question-answer pairs: agents must learn to reliably find, connect, and correct task-relevant evidence in omni-setting. However, we argue that existing data pipelines provide limited control over task-relevant evidence and weak supervision for these capabilities. In this work, we present Open OmniCoach, a new framework for evidence-seeking omni video agents. It contains a high-quality dataset, OmniCoach-240K, and a new agentic reinforcement learning (RL) method, Step-Level Evidence-Guided Policy Optimization (SEPO). For the former, OmniCoach-240K comprises 248.2K interaction trajectories, including 230.7K multi-span trajectories and 17.4K reflection trajectories. We design a three-stage data pipeline by leveraging both fine-grained omni-evidence and the agent's own errors. Meanwhile, compared with other RL baselines, SEPO provides turn-level credit based on improvements in evidence coverage and temporal localization. Applying both trajectory data and the SEPO method, we obtain Open OmniAgent models. The resulting models outperform the baselines across multiple video and audio-visual understanding tasks by significant margins, demonstrating the effectiveness of the Open OmniCoach framework. In particular, Open OmniAgent-30B improves average accuracy over Qwen3-Omni by 6.3 percentage points across nine benchmarks, with gains of 12.2 and 13.3 points on LVOmniBench and LVBench, respectively.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31891) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31891)

## 231. Rethinking Video Agents: When and Why Video Harnesses Work

- **作者**：Luoyuan Zhang, Xuanyu Zheng, Yancheng Long et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM

Video understanding depends not only on how models reason about visual evidence, but also on which evidence they observe. Fixed observation policies determine this evidence in advance; agentic harnesses allow models to seek further observations as their understanding develops. This shift creates an opportunity to realize capabilities that fixed inputs leave untapped, yet the ability to request observations does not ensure their effective use. We investigate when and why video harnesses improve understanding, examining how task demands and models' capacity for interaction jointly shape their benefits. Using Video-Relook as an experimental framework, we let models adapt their temporal and spatial observations from a common default configuration, and analyze performance and interaction trajectories across models and tasks. Adaptive observation yields substantial gains on fine-grained action understanding, with benefits also extending to Video-MME-v2. Even under the same observation interface, gains differ substantially across models; interaction traces reveal corresponding differences in evidence seeking and the reliability of answer revision. The benefits also vary across tasks for the same model, ranging from substantial improvements to limited gains and regressions. Together, these findings support an account in which harness effectiveness depends on the alignment between opportunities for informative observation and the model's ability to turn interaction into reliable revisions. They motivate video harnesses that coordinate perception and reasoning, matching observational autonomy to task demands and model capabilities.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31883) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31883)

## 232. RELAY: Learning Compatible Representations One Encoder at a Time

- **作者**：Yu Wang, Chuan Qin, Qiang Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.IR
- **备注**：26 pages, 6 figures

Old and new document embeddings coexist during incremental gallery re-encoding. RELAY trains a new query encoder with a learned projection against the original gallery, then freezes both to train the new document encoder. The same query vector scores both document versions throughout a single re-encoding pass. After backfill, optional query refinement drops the projection and reuses the stored document vectors. Across three text-to-image retrieval upgrades on COCO and Flickr30K, RELAY improves Recall@1 on untouched galleries by 8.66-10.23 percentage points over the original systems. For direct re-encoding, RELAY ranks first in both Qwen-to-Qwen upgrades and second in CLIP-to-Qwen by area under the Recall@1 curve, under both native scoring and matched calibration. With embeddings held fixed, calibrating scores separately for each document version can reduce drops in Recall@1 during backfill and change which method achieves the highest backfill AUC. After query refinement, final Recall@1 is within 0.97 percentage points of conventional joint training in all three upgrades.

## 233. Don't Miss the Forest for the Trees: Zero-Shot Skeleton Action Recognition with Hyperbolic Hierarchical Semantic Prototypes

- **作者**：Zixiao Jin, Siyu Zhu, Feng Ding et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 7 figures, 2 tables

Zero-shot skeleton-based action recognition is evaluated almost exclusively on flat leaf labels, which leaves open whether a representation also grounds the broader concepts that contain an action in skeleton motion. We find that strong leaf alignment does not carry over to them: when ancestor concepts are scored directly under independent decoding, eight flat methods with 57.86-73.29% mean leaf Top-1 reach only 6.15-31.21% mean full-path accuracy across four NTU RGB+D splits. We propose the Hyperbolic Hierarchical Semantic Prototype ($H^2SP$) framework, which represents every node of an action taxonomy in the Lorentz model of hyperbolic space. First, instead of learning a free vector per node, $H^2SP$ synthesizes hierarchy-aware prototypes by recursive parent-child geodesic interpolation under a single tree-wide gate. Second, tangent-space semantic and distributional alignment on seen classes anchor the leaf prototypes in skeleton motion. Third, a soft entailment-cone regularizer encourages directional parent-child inclusion, propagating this motion grounding to ancestor concepts. We evaluate hierarchical concept prediction under independent and cascade decoding, and transfer to an alternative taxonomy without retraining. Across the four splits, $H^2SP$ attains the best mean independent and cascade full-path accuracy among the evaluated methods, at a comparable leaf Top-1 of 72.40%. Transferred to the alternative taxonomy, it outperforms both flat baselines and the same-tree Euclidean control across every split and decoding mode, indicating that its concept-level correspondence extends beyond the training taxonomy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31877) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31877)

## 234. Subliminal Learning Crosses Modalities

- **作者**：Saraswathy Amjith, Kimia Hamidieh, Antonio Torralba et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CL, cs.CV

Subliminal learning refers to a surprising phenomenon in language models wherein a prompt given to one model (e.g., preference for cats) can be subliminally distilled to another model via training on data that is ostensibly meaningless (e.g., number sequences). This paper shows that text-based subliminal learning transfers cross-modally, in particular, a topic/preference (e.g., animals) transferred through number sequences across vision-language models also changes how images are generated and represented. First, we show that a diffusion model using the student as its text encoder generates the student's animal more often than with the original model as its text encoder, but not its color or style. We then investigate the mechanism behind this phenomenon and which concepts transfer across modalities. A constant component of the student's change accounts for much of the shift of image representations toward the trait. Adding this component uniformly can change the generated animal, which depends on the whole prompt, but not concepts such as color or style, which are encoded in a single token. Finally, we introduce weight lens as an approach to identify a subliminal trait from the weights of a single trained model, and show that it identifies the trait of nearly every student, including traits that do not surface in generated images.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31870) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31870)

## 235. CALVIN-MAX: When Does a Mid-Execution Change Hurt a Robot Policy?

- **作者**：Sajjad Pakdamansavoji
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：34 pages (8-page main paper and 23-page supplementary material), 19 figures, 28 tables. Project page: https://forall-labs.github.io/CALVIN-MAX/

Robots must often keep working after the world changes, as when a block moves or the camera shifts while the robot still acts on what it saw before. Recent work showed that one change during a task lowers success for every policy tested, but did not separate the effect of its timing from the cost of the changed world, or measure what it costs afterwards. We introduce CALVIN-MAX, a benchmark of 7,828 cases on CALVIN's five-instruction chains, each with one change to the objects, the camera, the robot, or the instruction. Each case compares three runs that are identical until the affected instruction begins: one without the change, one with it in place from that moment, and one in which it arrives while the robot is moving. The second run against the first gives the cost of the changed world, and the third against the second the effect of meeting it mid-motion. On 1,526 held-out cases per policy, with hypotheses fixed in advance, each of ten released policies loses 4.4 to 9.9 percentage points on the affected instruction over the eight core changes. Whether timing matters depends on the change: one that alters what the robot sees costs 5.4 points less mid-motion than from the start, whereas a change to the scene itself or to the robot shows no detectable effect. Rewinding the robot to the start of the instruction removes the benefit for every policy; the stricter test we fixed in advance, that the rewound run then matches the second run, passes for three. The cost also outlasts the affected instruction: the change stays in place, and a policy that still completes that instruction is 5.0 to 16.8 points less likely to complete the next one.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31868) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31868)

## 236. Start Where the Data Are: Statistical Priors for One-Step Generation on Manifolds

- **作者**：Yuyao Wang, Haoliang Sun, Xiankai Lu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：33 pages (8 main + references + 22 supplementary), 22 figures, 24 tables. Code: https://github.com/olivia3395/statprior-onestep

Fast generation on manifolds is important for scientific applications such as protein design, yet existing one-step generators often produce spurious samples in low-density gaps between data modes. Our analysis highlights the mismatch between uniform noise and concentrated data, motivating a statistical prior that captures the data's coarse structure. We first estimate this prior by fitting mixtures of tangent-space Gaussians through geometry-aware moment matching. However, independent noise-data pairing can still connect different modes and weaken the benefit of the fitted prior. We therefore introduce posterior coupling, which samples a latent component conditioned on each data point and draws its paired noise from that component. To extend this approach to long torsion-angle chains, where full-covariance mixtures become costly, we use a hidden Markov prior to model sequential dependence with shared parameters. The resulting framework plugs into existing one-step training pipelines while retaining the generator architecture, training objective, and single network evaluation at inference. Experiments on spheres, tori, and SO(3) demonstrate substantial improvements across multiple generators, reducing distributional error by up to $125\times$ and the protein Ramachandran outlier rate from 12.1% to 0.55%.

## 237. JECT: Addressing the Context Shortcut in Joint-Embedding Predictive Learning for Chest CT

- **作者**：Hanbin Ko, Hong-Jun Choi, Jihun Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：24 pages, 7 figures, 23 tables, including supplementary material. Code and weights: https://github.com/lukeingawesome/JECT

Joint-embedding predictive architectures learn by predicting features of masked regions from visible context. In chest computed tomography, the findings that decide a diagnosis, such as a small nodule, are often not predictable from the surrounding anatomy. A hidden finding is then best predicted toward normal anatomy, and the prediction loss stops rewarding the encoder for representing it. We call this the context shortcut. Conditioning the predictor on the radiology report repairs the prediction, but in our setup the encoder gains little over image-only pretraining: the report supplies what the encoder never had to learn. JECT therefore gives the report two roles: a condition for the predictor and, through masked section-text prediction, a target for the encoder, which must predict the report's anatomical sections from the unmasked scan. A hierarchical three-dimensional encoder that predicts at every stage keeps this clinical content at every scale. In ablations, the text target improves classification and localization, while conditioning adds little to the encoder. Trained from scratch on 173k volumes, the recipe reaches the level of current computed tomography foundation models in classification, retrieval and nodule malignancy, and of task-specific models in lung-cancer risk prediction after fine-tuning. Conditioning still matters: its predictor can be reused, with no extra training, to show where a report sentence acts, changing most at the finding the sentence describes. Code and pretrained weights are available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31849) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31849)

## 238. BendGS: Observation-Conditioned Gaussian Self-Modeling for Continuum Robots

- **作者**：Jeong Woon Lee, Doungjun Kim, Namho Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：27 pages, 18 figures

Visual self-models learn from images how a robot moves under motor commands. Continuum robots need them even more, as they bend continuously without measured joint angles or fixed link geometry. Existing continuum self-models predict point clouds, image-space curves, or future images, but not the next 3D shape, appearance, and tip pose together from an observed body. 3D Gaussians offer a renderable representation of shape and appearance, yet articulated Gaussian self-models rely on joint angles to drive deformation. In this work, we propose BendGS, which uses a virtual backbone estimated from observation to drive command-conditioned Gaussian deformation, enabling next-state prediction for continuum robots without measured joint angles. In Gaussian modeling, a feed-forward network reconstructs the robot as 3D Gaussians from multi-view images, and the backbone, a chain of coordinate frames, is fitted to back-projected depth points to estimate the bending state. In transition, a network predicts its change under a relative motor command to deform the bound Gaussians. The deformed Gaussians render the next images and the 3D shape, and the distal frame gives the tip pose. Training losses use RGB-D images and masks, without bending or tip labels. On simulated and real robots, BendGS achieves the lowest Chamfer distance, best image quality, and lowest tip errors among the compared methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31844) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31844)

## 239. Gaze World Model

- **作者**：Jiawen Zhang, Houze Yang, Xu Cao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：25 pages, 8 figures, 15 tables

Where a person looks indicates their focus of attention, and how their gaze shifts between targets reveals how that attention responds to a changing scene. Existing gaze target estimation methods largely operate frame by frame and do not model these shifts. Gaze shifts are often driven by dynamic changes in the scene, which is the kind of information world models are designed to encode. This relationship is subtle, however, since an event far across the frame may produce only a slight movement of the person's head or eyes. We therefore introduce the Gaze World Model (GWM), which co-trains latent scene prediction with person-conditioned gaze supervision on a frozen V-JEPA 2.1 encoder; in our ablations, the latent prediction objective improves both shift detection and gaze localization. Given a video clip and the person's head box, GWM estimates gaze locations and detects gaze shifts in the observed clip, and forecasts future gaze and shifts from predicted scene features. To train and evaluate these tasks, we augment VideoAttentionTarget and ChildPlay with event-level gaze-shift annotations. GWM achieves state-of-the-art shift detection on these benchmarks, about 10 points higher in four-class AP than the strongest baseline, while remaining comparable to the strongest image-based models in gaze target estimation, and outperforms the compared baselines on future gaze localization and shift forecasting.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31836) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31836)

## 240. G0.7: Scaling Up a Fast World Action Model with Long Context

- **作者**：Tianyuan Yuan, Baijun Ye, Shicheng Cao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

World action models (WAMs) adapt pretrained video generators for robot control by jointly predicting future camera frames and actions, transferring physical priors learned from video into manipulation policies. However, generating future frames before every action makes test-time inference too slow for closed-loop control, while most existing policies remain restricted to short observation histories. Building on Fast-WAM, which showed that video prediction shapes policy representations during training so that future frame synthesis can be skipped at deployment, we present G0.7, a generalist world action model scaled along two axes: extending the context window from a single observation to up to five minutes of history, and scaling pretraining to a large-scale corpus of robot teleoperation and human egocentric (ego) video. Across public simulation suites and real-robot deployments, direct action decoding (Fast mode) matches imagine-then-act (IDM) inference from the same checkpoint while running in 60 ms per 16-step action chunk on a single RTX 5090 (7.6x faster than IDM) and supporting pause-free real-time chunking. Performance scales consistently with pretraining data, with human ego video driving the largest gains in out-of-distribution generalization: G0.7 achieves a 70.8 mean score across five simulation benchmark suites, 48% and 54% zero-shot pick-and-place success on Galaxea R1 Pro and R1 Lite, 96.7% in-distribution and 93.3% out-of-distribution success on video-conditioned in-context learning tasks, and 86.9% mean task progress across seven real-world post-training tasks including multi-minute box folding, drink preparation, and product bagging.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31822) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31822)

## 241. Compressible Representations: Functional Spines in Deep Neural Networks

- **作者**：Niranjan Rajesh, Meenakshi Khosla
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026

Deep networks embed inputs in high-dimensional representations, but how much of this capacity actually drives behavior? We introduce a framework for identifying, at each layer, the subspace of the representation that is functionally sensitive to the task. Using a matrix analogous to the Fisher information but defined over the hidden representation, we rank directions by their influence on downstream computation and project onto the most task-sensitive subspace. Across pretrained vision networks, representations are remarkably compressible: a small fraction of the task-sensitive subspace preserves most performance, often requiring far fewer dimensions than geometric alternatives. We find that this compressed representation is also more robust to adversarial attacks generated on the full network. We additionally show that this decomposition recovers functionally distinct subspaces within the same representation: on cue-conflict stimuli, shape-sensitive and texture-sensitive directions are largely non-overlapping, and ablating one selectively impairs the corresponding task while leaving the other intact. Task-sensitive subspaces also provide a principled foundation for continual learning: constraining gradient updates to lie orthogonal to the task-sensitive subspaces of prior tasks outperforms standard variance-based gradient projection methods. Finally, restricting representational comparisons to task-sensitive subspaces reveals that inter-model alignment is higher in task-sensitive directions than in task-insensitive directions at later layers, and that conditioning on different tasks over the same stimuli uncovers asymmetries that full-space similarity measures cannot detect. Together, these results establish that deep networks compute through a low-dimensional functional spine embedded within a much larger representational space.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31817) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31817)

## 242. Beyond the Benchmark: Localizing Released Autonomous Driving Models to Real Vehicles

- **作者**：Morui Zhu, Deyuan Qu, Qi Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 5 figures

Released autonomous-driving models achieve strong benchmark performance, yet deploying them on a new real vehicle remains challenging. We study this deployment gap and find that released models can remain coupled to assumptions from their source sensing, data, and execution processes even after conventional adaptation. We introduce Released-to-Real (R2R), a deployment-localization methodology that identifies such source-system coupling and corrects only the affected components while preserving the released model. For data and behavior adaptation, we find that the mismatch between trajectory-level optimization and receding-horizon execution enters through the ego state rather than the observation, and introduce Human-Anchored Reinforcement Learning (HA-RL), which corrects it on recorded target driving without the costly rollouts of generative closed-loop reinforcement learning (RL). For physical sensor adaptation, R2R localizes and corrects source-dependent spatial and temporal representations with frozen perception weights, requiring neither target annotations nor retraining. HA-RL improves the closed-loop success rate of a released diffusion-based planner from 82.2% to 95.2%, while sensor adaptation across multiple released detectors and three real sensor rigs improves the vehicle mean average precision (mAP) of a released multi-camera detector from 0.041 to 0.420 on a five-camera target rig. These results provide a practical path for preparing released models before deployment on new real vehicles. Our project page is publicly available at https://released-to-real.github.io.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31814) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31814)

## 243. Generalizable Feed-Forward 3D Hand-Object Reconstruction with Coarse-to-Fine Contact Estimation

- **作者**：Seonghwan Park, Yong-Hoon Kwon, Min-Gyu Park
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Preprint. Project page: https://shpark97.github.io/Horcrux

Reconstructing the 3D poses of hands and objects from a single image, together with the contact between them, is fundamental to understanding manipulation. To be practical, the reconstruction must generalize to unseen objects, and the contact must be localized precisely. Yet joint feed-forward estimators often rely on predefined object sets, while contact is typically predicted from image features alone, without the explicit geometry needed to localize it. To address both limitations, we present HORCRUX, a unified feed-forward transformer with coarse-to-fine contact estimation. For unseen objects, a frozen foundation model encodes the object from a given or image-reconstructed mesh rather than from object-specific knowledge. A shared transformer then reasons jointly over the object and one or two hands to keep them aligned. For precise contact, coarse per-joint predictions from latent features are resolved over finer hand regions using the reconstructed geometry. Across seven benchmarks, HORCRUX achieves state-of-the-art object and hand pose accuracy among joint estimators and outperforms a dedicated contact estimator in fine-grained contact. These results suggest that contact is best estimated jointly with reconstruction, whose geometry directly localizes it.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31812) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31812)

## 244. SceneMemBench: A Benchmark for Scene Memory in Interactive Video World Models

- **作者**：Yuheng Si, Linye Lyu, Jiawei Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：32 pages, 14 figures, 23 tables. Project page: https://yuheng-si.github.io/SceneMemBench/

Interactive video world models generate navigable worlds frame by frame, but a persistent world requires previously observed scenes to remain recoverable as users move away and return. Existing memory evaluations, however, often focus on seen-view revisits and full rollouts, leaving cross-view scene recovery underexplored while entangling memory with trajectory-following errors and accumulated generation degradation. We introduce SceneMemBench, a benchmark for scene memory in interactive video world models. It evaluates two complementary dimensions: seen-view recall versus novel-view recovery, and controlled diagnosis versus end-to-end interaction. We introduce Continued Rollout from a shared ground-truth observation history, alongside Full Rollout over complete model-generated interactions. SceneMemBench contains 600 controlled trajectories across 33 Unreal Engine scenes and 60 Full Rollout cases, covering eight representative open- and closed-source models. We evaluate scene recovery along subject consistency, appearance fidelity, and geometric consistency. Results show that novel-view recovery is generally more challenging than seen-view recall, while model profiles under controlled histories can differ markedly from those observed in full interaction. Together, these findings reveal complementary failure modes obscured by seen-view revisits or end-to-end rollouts alone.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31803) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31803)

## 245. VISOR: A Vision-Language Selector for Multimodal Time-Series Forecasting

- **作者**：Seunghan Lee, Jun Seo, Junhyeok Kang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Recent time-series foundation models (TSFMs) forecast a distribution over futures, and multimodal time-series (TS) forecasting further exploits other modalities to improve forecasting accuracy. However, existing works have two limitations: 1) TSFMs reduce the predictive distribution to its median or mean, flattening diverse futures into a calm line, and 2) multimodal TS forecasting models incorporate text into the forecaster through TS-language alignment, which is substantially weaker than vision-language alignment. To address these limitations, we propose the VIsion-language SelectOR (VISOR), a multimodal TS forecasting framework that selects one plausible future instead of averaging across candidates, leveraging the alignment capabilities of vision-language models (VLMs) to select the most suitable candidate based on textual information. VISOR consists of two components: 1) a text-free stochastic predictor that samples diverse candidate futures, and 2) a VLM selector that reads the candidates as a single chart image and selects one based on the text. To avoid overly calm forecasting paths, we train the selector with a shape-aware target that evaluates each candidate based on both point-wise error and path shape. Across three benchmarks covering ten datasets, VISOR achieves the best average ranking in both point and volatility errors compared with TSFMs and multimodal TS forecasting models. Code: https://github.com/seunghan96/VISOR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31780) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31780)

## 246. A Well-Posed Fourth-Order Navier-Stokes Model for Image Inpainting: Theory, Order Selection, and Stripe Decomposition

- **作者**：Yiwen Dou, Yiqiang Ding, Zhongyun Bao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.IV
- **备注**：33 pages, 11 figures, 10 tables

The Navier-Stokes (NS) inpainting model of Bertalmío, Bertozzi and Sapiro is the only transport-type paradigm among PDE-based methods, yet it has never received a systematic higher-order extension. We show that the obvious fourth-order correction is ill-posed, and we construct the corrected, well-posed model. In the vorticity-streamfunction formulation we add a gated fourth-order term whose sign is decisive: the backward form is a Perona-Malik-type anti-diffusion, Hadamard ill-posed and consistently harmful in experiments, whereas the forward form is uniformly parabolic, energy-stable and consistently beneficial. An order-dissipation principle, stating that odd orders are dispersive and even orders dissipative, singles out the fourth order as the lowest admissible one. Large missing blocks pose a separate problem, because the correction gate stays silent there; we answer it with stripe decomposition, a Telea base fill followed by interleaved slit-wise NS passes, which converts block damage into the structural-slit regime where the model excels. The device is base-agnostic, and its gains add to those of the forward correction. On three datasets, namely BSDS500, DIV2K and a classical Chinese painting benchmark, and on three damage types, namely cracks, blocks and text occlusion, the training-free model beats every classical baseline on every sample of structure-dominated damage, gains +1.02 dB PSNR over Telea on the full 42-image painting test set with a 42/42 per-sample win rate, matches the strongest recent PDE solvers within 0.4 dB while attaining the best perceptual quality of the entire PDE family, and runs at 4-16 s per image on a consumer GPU.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31777) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31777)

## 247. ReCAP: Sequential Concept Acquisition with Guarantees under Imperfect Concept Predictors

- **作者**：Md Nahiduzzaman, Steven Korevaar, Alireza Bab-Hadiashar et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Sequential concept acquisition enables interpretable diagnosis from a compact set of human-understandable findings. Such explanations are useful only if clinicians can trust them, yet most existing methods treat predicted concepts as correct, so a correct diagnosis can rest on incorrectly predicted evidence. We propose Reliable Concept Acquisition with Pruning (ReCAP), which reasons over what the concept predictor reports, including errors and abstentions, and replaces conditional information gain with a label-discriminating objective that separates cases requiring different diagnoses. We prove this objective adaptively monotone and adaptively submodular, yielding a (1-1/e) guarantee for greedy fixed-budget acquisition that degrades boundedly under learned estimates, and combine it with abstention-aware acquisition and diagnosis-preserving pruning to keep explanations short and reliable. Across five medical imaging datasets, ReCAP reduces the share of correct diagnoses supported by at least one incorrectly predicted concept from 27.1% to 9.6% relative to the strongest baseline, while using fewer than half as many concepts and achieving higher diagnostic accuracy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31776) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31776)

## 248. BODYBENCH: Evaluating Adversarial Image Defenses Against AI Nudification Inpainting

- **作者**：Li Qiwei, Salma Abdel Magid, Olga Russakovsky et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CR
- **备注**：Li Qiwei and Salma Abdel Magid contributed equally to this work and share first authorship

Adversarial perturbation defenses protect images from unauthorized edits by adding imperceptible noise. Yet none of these methods have been evaluated against nudification, despite AI-generated non-consensual intimate imagery (AIG-NCII) being among the most documented harms of generative AI. Moreover, nudification edits are readily carried out through inpainting, the primary setting these protection methods are designed to defend against. We argue this is a problem-formulation failure since prior threat models do not correspond to nudification edits (also called attacks). The misalignment of existing approaches is structural, spanning mask shape, training and testing prompt distributions, and success criterion which causes existing evaluations to systematically overestimate protection effectiveness. We reformulate image protection against unauthorized editing around this documented harm. We introduce BODYBENCH, a benchmark of 648 fully synthetic clothed adult subjects, each paired with 11 mask variants spanning body, face, and context regions. We propose three harm-aligned metrics: sexualization, naturalness, and recognizability that jointly determine whether an inpainted output constitutes a successful nudification attack. Auditing four recent protection methods (PhotoGuard, DiffusionGuard, AdvPaint, DiffVax) on BODYBENCH, we find that protection succeeds in only one configuration of one method, and only under exact mask alignment between defender and attacker---a condition no real defender can guarantee and one that we show is easily always bypassed. We also show that standard evaluation metrics such as peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) are essentially uncorrelated with nudification protection. BODYBENCH makes this misalignment measurable and provides a foundation for defenses grounded in documented harm.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31774) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31774)

## 249. ET: Efficient Transfer from Softmax to Hybrid Attention

- **作者**：Guangfu Guo, Xiaoqian Lu, Linsey Pang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Converting pretrained softmax Transformers to efficient attention often causes substantial performance degradation, while recovery typically requires costly fine-tuning. We propose ET (Efficient Transfer), a framework for high-fidelity attention conversion with minimal optimization cost. ET combines exact local and shared-key softmax attention with a mean-anchored, scan-free global linear correction and adaptive gating, achieving linear complexity in sequence length. To preserve pretrained knowledge, ET freezes pretrained weights and calibrates only newly introduced parameters through alternating least squares on a small set of unlabeled samples, without gradient updates. On ImageNet-1K, ET achieves 79.64% and 81.55% top-1 accuracy on DeiT-S and DeiT-B, respectively, within 0.25 percentage points of their softmax teachers. At $768\times768$, ET outperforms T$^5$ by 3.59 percentage points at comparable inference FLOPs, without gradient-based fine-tuning. On DiT-XL/2, ET achieves an FID-50K of 2.59 with only 250 LoRA updates. On Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium, ET matches the teacher's GenEval score at 1K resolution with just 0.73 A100 GPU-hours of gradient-free conversion and a 1.39$\times$ inference speedup.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31770) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31770)

## 250. RoMo Hands: A Large Scale Richly Organized Text to Hand Motion Dataset

- **作者**：Yizhak Ben-Shabat, Jiahao Zhang, Joseph Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.GR, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://romodataset.github.io/romo-hands/ Dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/RoMoDataset/RoMo-Hands

While text-driven body motion generation has matured, pure-hand synthesis remains bottlenecked by data. Existing datasets often treat hands as rigid end-effectors, lack fine-grained captions, or are too limited in scale to support robust generalization. We introduce RoMo Hands, an in-the-wild, text-paired dataset of ~542K sequences (884 hours, 95.5M frames at 30 fps) which is an order of magnitude larger than prior work. To ensure high-quality supervision, a stringent dexterity filter discards low-articulation sequences, focusing the distribution on complex bimanual movement. Each clip is paired with five captions at increasing granularities from global semantic tags to atomic finger states, organized under a hierarchical taxonomy. To bridge the gap between body and hand architectures, we propose a novel 393-D bimanual representation that adapts redundant body-motion paradigms to the high-DoF kinematics of hands. Systematic benchmarking of diffusion, masked-token, and autoregressive generators reveals distinct trade-offs under these hand-specific adaptations, including a sharp divergence between semantic alignment and kinematic plausibility. Our dataset, representation, and benchmark establish a rigorous foundation for the next generation of dexterous motion synthesis. Project page: https://romodataset.github.io/romo-hands/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31766) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31766)

## 251. Dreamcatcher: Remembering What Surprises a World Model in Video Streams

- **作者**：Jinxi He, Ce Zhang, Wenhe Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

As live video streams grow longer, repeatedly encoding and processing their full histories becomes increasingly costly. Answering questions as they arrive therefore calls for visual memory built in one pass before future questions are known. Existing methods mainly keep tokens that differ from earlier ones or match surrogate queries fixed in advance. Without modeling world dynamics, these criteria struggle to tell subtle surprising details from expected change. We introduce Dreamcatcher, a training-free framework and, to our knowledge, the first to use predictive signals from an independently pretrained, frozen video world model for online memory formation in streaming video question answering. Conditioned on recent history, the world model predicts incoming features using learned world priors. Its normalized prediction errors yield temporal and spatial surprisal signals that guide online segmentation and the retention of a frozen Video-LLM's native visual tokens, balancing surprising details with broad visual coverage under budgets that decay with segment age. At query time, an evidence gate admits a segment from a similarity-based shortlist only when the Video-LLM judges that it adds evidence for the question beyond the current view. We evaluate Dreamcatcher on three streaming and three offline long-video benchmarks. On OVO-Bench, Dreamcatcher achieves the highest overall accuracy among the compared training-free methods for each of four frozen Video-LLMs, reaching 78.9% with Qwen3.8-27B. With the three Qwen Video-LLMs, it also reduces average input tokens for answer generation by more than 80% relative to their base models at 1 fps.

## 252. Xtream: An Explicit Metric State for Large-Scale Streaming Reconstruction

- **作者**：Ernesto Lozano, Alberto Jaenal, Javier Civera
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, 3 figures

3D foundation models (3DFMs) enable dense reconstruction in a feed-forward pass, yet their effective span is limited by cross-view attention. Local consistency has been expanded through geometric alignment of video chunks and recursive updates of an implicit memory, and large-scale scenes with pose graphs. However, both strategies are prone to drift, largely due to the lack of metric-scale observability from monocular geometry. We present Xtream,a hybrid streaming pipeline that maintains an explicit metric state through a factor graph over keyframes, while using a 3DFM as a geometric front end. Xtream incorporates metric information in two ways: through metric scale predictions from 3DFMs; or through inertial measurements if available. Across the large-scale benchmarks of VBR, Oxford Spires and KITTI, Xtream achieves state-of-the-art performance in 3DFM-based online reconstruction, reducing average absolute trajectory errors compared with published baselines and concurrent work. https://ernestolozano.github.io/xtream

## 253. RVLM-X: Towards a Universal VLM Harness

- **作者**：Aadi Palnitkar, Dmytro Kurdydyk, Xiaomin Lin
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：11 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables

Harnesses for vision-language models (VLMs) have grown with the models' capabilities, notably for images far larger than those seen in training, where downscaling loses small details. Most of them wrap the VLM in a fixed procedure, such as a zoom tree, that decides in advance where it looks and how the evidence is combined. These procedures make up for the weaknesses of older models by keeping them on a task-specific path, but with frontier models that reliably plan and use tools, they can lower accuracy. We propose RVLM-X, a task-generalizable recursive VLM harness that runs unchanged across photographs, charts and maps. Following the recursive inference of Recursive Language Models, the VLM programmatically controls its own visual context in a code REPL that holds the full-resolution image and calls itself recursively on regions that it chooses. With a cost-efficient frontier model as the base of every method, RVLM-X is more accurate than a single call on all 7 public benchmarks and has the highest pooled accuracy. Because existing benchmarks mostly test whether a model finds one small detail, we also introduce MapGenBench, procedurally generated maps whose questions combine fine-detail perception with multi-step reasoning. There, RVLM-X answers 71.4% of held-out questions against 55.5% for the strongest baseline. Overall, our findings suggest that as VLMs plan and use tools more reliably, letting the model write its own visual search works better than fixing one in advance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31755) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31755)

## 254. LeanWAM: Learning Latent World Action Models from Frozen Video Models

- **作者**：Shaolong Li, Hongze Fu, Joyce Chai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：Project Page: https://shaolonl-16.github.io/leanwam/

World action models (WAMs) typically adapt large pretrained video generators to jointly predict future observations and robot actions, incurring substantial training and deployment costs. However, it remains unclear which future representations a policy actually needs and how compactly they can be represented. Our probing study shows that complementary future representations can be compressed into a few tokens while retaining most of their action-decoding performance. Motivated by this finding, we introduce LeanWAM, a latent WAM that transfers video priors from a frozen video generator to a lightweight policy via two-stage training. In the first stage, a Future Extractor compresses observed transitions into 32 predictive tokens. These tokens condition the frozen generator and are jointly shaped by action, RGB, semantic, and geometric supervision, while a compact readout of the generator's hidden states supplies complementary world tokens. The second stage distills both representations into the policy, which learns to predict them from current observations while generating actions. With 1.4B parameters and no large-scale embodied pretraining, LeanWAM outperforms strong vision-language-action (VLA) and WAM baselines on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin~2.0. It predicts an action chunk in 25.1\,ms on an A100 GPU, 12.3--41.5$\times$ faster than representative WAMs. These results demonstrate that compact predictive supervision can transfer video priors to robust and efficient control.

## 255. BEARING: Who Is Talking to Whom? Grounding Acoustic Direction in Vision for Online Multi-Party Conversation

- **作者**：Nader Lobandi, Lombe Chileshe, Mohammad Mehdi Hosseini et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.SD
- **备注**：26 pages, 15 figures, 16 tables. Includes supplementary material. Demo video as an ancillary file

Understanding a group conversation means deciding, from video and audio, who is speaking and to whom. Leading systems treat these tasks offline on single-channel clips and align models with a self-evaluation reward. We propose BEARING, a geometry-first alternative built on one measured signal: the direction from which a microphone array beside the camera hears a sound. A label-free calibration learned from the stream registers this direction into the camera image, and an online registry of faces, voices and directions needs no enrollment. The registered direction serves as (i) a visual prompt for a vision-language model, a marker on the speaker or an arrow toward an off-screen source; (ii) one input of a reliability-weighted fusion with visual active-speaker detection, for speakers in the same direction from the array; and (iii) a verifiable reward for alignment: the named speaker must sit where the sound comes from, the confidence must match that check, and always answering "group" earns nothing. Every decision is online, with the lookahead as an explicit parameter. On 9,498 addressee-labelled utterances from three AMI sites and egocentric EasyCom, the direction prompt raises speaker accuracy from 54% to 74%; addressee accuracy moves by about two points, because telling one addressee from the group depends on the words more than on where people are. For co-located speakers on AVDIAR, visual active-speaker detection holds 95% where direction alone falls to 59%. The verifiable reward beats self-evaluation under GRPO, reward-ranked fine-tuning and DPO; shrinking the lookahead from 5 s to 0.5 s costs 4.5 DER points on AMI, and learning the registration online costs a dip in the first two minutes before the system overtakes vision alone. Code and evaluation protocols will be released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31735) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31735)

## 256. EventSSM: Causal Spatial-State Modelling for Asynchronous Events Event-Depth Estimation

- **作者**：Sadiq O. Macaulay, Dipon Ghosh, Hadfield Simon
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

Event cameras report asynchronous brightness changes, yet metric-depth supervision is typically available only at sparsely sampled reference timestamps. Aggregating events into frame-like representations simplifies learning but can obscure the fine-grained temporal structure of the original event stream. We introduce EventSSM, a causal spatial-state framework for event-associated metric-depth estimation using a recurrent memory updated by chronological event packets. To bridge asynchronous predictions and frame-time supervision, predicted event depths are geometrically transported using calibrated camera poses and differentiably rendered at reference timestamps. Benchmark evaluations demonstrate improvements over established event-depth methods across multiple depth-error measures. Controlled ablations further examine cross-modal initialization, temporal recurrence, individual state, readout, and supervision components, and packet subdivision. Together, these findings establish EventSSM as an effective framework for causal event-associated metric-depth estimation, combining persistent spatial context with physically timed state retention.
