## 257. NOBODY Was There: Bridging the Human-VLM Gap in Unwarranted Person Attribution Bias

- **作者**：Parsa Madinei, Ziqi Wen, Miguel Eckstein
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Describing an image requires not only recognizing what is visible but deciding what should be inferred. We investigate a systematic gap between humans and VLMs in their tendency to attribute visible actions or events to people who are not present in an image. We term this behavior unwarranted person attribution bias and find that, across 25 SOTA VLMs, captions of person-absent scenes mention a person four times as often as human-written captions of the same images. Through a preference study, we further show that both humans and VLMs prefer captions that avoid such attributions. Motivated by this, we introduce NOBODY, a preference dataset designed to bridge the gap between humans and VLMs in unwarranted person attribution. Through direct preference optimization (DPO), we align open VLMs on NOBODY and show that their agreement with human captions on person attribution reaches or exceeds inter-human agreement, whereas nine existing public preference datasets leave this behavior largely unchanged. Furthermore, we show that human judges preferred the aligned VLM captions significantly more than those of the baseline model. Alongside NOBODY, we release NOBODY-Bench, which evaluates VLMs for unwarranted person attribution bias and their alignment against human captions and preference. Project page: https://pmadinei.github.io/NOBODY/

## 258. E-Waste-Bench: A Large-Scale Benchmark for Visual Triage of Data-Center Hardware

- **作者**：Nader Lobandi, Mohammad Mehdi Hosseini, Lombe Chileshe et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：30 figures, 22 tables. Includes supplementary material

Retired data-center hardware can often be refurbished or resold, but triage demands more than object categories. A model must tell visually similar parts apart, judge condition under damage and occlusion, and read the printed part number. We audit nine public e-waste collections. They cover mostly consumer devices (only 10% of images are labeled as a data-center part), label neither condition nor printed text, and contain 42% exact or augmented duplicates. We introduce E-Waste-Bench, 328K photographs drawn from 762K deduplicated images and labeled for class (24 classes), granularity, condition and printed text. Images come from those collections and the web, where a recipe-based crawler works beside an agent built on an open-weight vision-language model. The agent vets new sources and reads sites the crawler cannot parse, and both are steered toward under-represented classes. Training labels are model-made, splits follow near-duplicate groups, and human annotators label a test set of nearly 10,000 images. We benchmark classification, condition, detection, text perception and cross-dataset transfer. On this test set, a ConvNeXt-T trained on E-Waste-Bench reaches 64.3% top-1, against 31.5% with the public collections alone. Near-duplicate leakage inflates top-1 by 3.0 points under model-made test labels but by less than 0.1 under human labels, so model-labeled benchmarks need group splits. Accuracy falls to 42.1% on marketplace and auction photographs, the closest proxy for hardware leaving service. Data and code will be released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31703) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31703)

## 259. GRUN: Generalized Retinex Unfolding Network for Image Restoration

- **作者**：Rihan Zhang, Fengyang Xiao, Yuhan Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Image restoration remains a fundamental challenge in low-level vision, requiring methods that handle diverse degradations while preserving scene structures and details. We revisit classical Retinex theory and propose a Generalized Retinex formulation that extends the illumination-reflectance decomposition into a Retinex-inspired content-environment factorization prior for image restoration. The key idea is to restore images using two learned factors: a content-dominant response (generalized reflectance) capturing scene properties and an environment-side response (generalized illumination) encoding task-dependent variations associated with the observed degradations. Based on this formulation, we develop GRUN (Generalized Retinex Unfolding Network), a MAP-guided deep unfolding framework that alternates reflectance restoration, illumination estimation, and progressive degradation inference across K stages with degradation-conditioned proximal operators. We further introduce a Degradation-aware Coupling Regularizer (DCR) that combines mutual information minimization, gradient decorrelation, and scale anchoring to promote complementary content-environment factor representations. Experiments on seven image restoration tasks, including denoising, dehazing, deraining, low-light enhancement, deblurring, underwater enhancement, and backlit enhancement, demonstrate competitive results on multiple benchmarks using the same architecture with task-specific weights. Moreover, the proposed Generalized Retinex design can be integrated into existing restoration methods, demonstrating its cross-architecture applicability. Code will be released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31690) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31690)

## 260. GeoMVGen: Joint Appearance-Geometry Modeling for Multi-View Video Generation in Robotic Manipulation

- **作者**：Qiaoqiao Jia, Guile Wu, Dongfeng Bai
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Technical Report

Robotic manipulation requires predicting how robot-object interactions evolve over time, encompassing both visual appearance and scene geometry. In multi-camera settings, such predictions need to capture the evolving 3D scene consistently across viewpoints, while supporting controllable generation under different robot actions and camera configurations. Existing video generation models provide strong priors for visual appearance and motion but typically lack explicit geometric modeling. Meanwhile, recent advances in geometry-aware and multi-view generation have explored joint appearance-geometry modeling and cross-view consistency, yet generating temporally coherent appearance and geometry across multiple viewpoints under robotic manipulation conditions remains challenging. In this work, we propose GeoMVGen, a framework for joint multi-view RGB and pointmap video generation in robotic manipulation. Built on a video diffusion transformer, our framework employs modality-specific adaptation modules to capture the distinct characteristics of appearance and geometry, while using cross-modal residual connections to facilitate information exchange between the two modalities. To establish geometric correspondences across viewpoints, we further introduce a cross-view projection mechanism with complementary geometric supervisions, combining pointmap consistency with geometry foundation model-guided feature alignment. These designs enable controllable generation of temporally evolving multi-view observations with coherent appearance and geometrically consistent scene structures. Extensive experiments on 4D video generation dataset comprising simulated and real-world robotic manipulation scenarios demonstrate that GeoMVGen outperforms state-of-the-art methods in video quality, geometric accuracy, and cross-view consistency.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31689) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31689)

## 261. Medical Image Segmentation Is Now Just Another Coding Task

- **作者**：Shuheng Cao, Ruiqi Chen, Qizhen Lan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

For decades, every new medical image segmentation task has needed an expert, a dedicated architecture, and a framework such as nnU-Net. This is no longer the case: medical image segmentation is now just another coding task. Given only a task description, labeled data, and a CPU (with no segmentation framework, no pretrained weights, and no human in the loop), four off-the-shelf coding agents built segmenters for 13 tasks across four imaging modalities within hours, an ability that has emerged rather than been built. On the nine 2D tasks, their best programs reach 99% of the Dice of a GPU-trained nnU-Net. Like any code, a segmenter improves by being revised and checked, so we ask how far the same agents can improve their own segmentations and what unlocks further gains. On unseen cases there is no reference, so the agents inspect renderings of their own predictions: this alone lifts the worst tenth of cases by 0.09 Dice and cuts boundary error (HD95) by almost a quarter. Far more is within reach. Told the true Dice of each attempt, the same agents improve in all 14 matched pairs, rescue 85% of failed masks instead of 30%, and surpass the GPU-trained nnU-Net on average, by producing better masks rather than by choosing among them. What unlocks these gains is a quality signal, which neither visual inspection, nor more rounds, nor a quality estimator trained by the agents themselves yet supplies: we call this the quality blind spot. The ability to build and to repair is already present; the next advance will come not from another segmentation model, but from reference-free quality estimation that coding agents can call.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31673) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31673)

## 262. A Piecewise-Linear Activation Function for Implicit Neural Representations

- **作者**：Shahd Hekal, Joram Kim, Jeova Farias Sales Rocha Neto
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：5 pages, 3 figures. Submitted to IEEE ICASSP 2027. Code: https://github.com/jeovafarias/jasin

Coordinate networks with ReLU activations perform poorly on implicit neural representation tasks, and the activations that address this, such as sinusoids and Gabor wavelets, all leave the piecewise-linear function class, so it remains unclear which property of ReLU is at fault. We introduce JaSIN, a family of bounded piecewise-linear activations that interpolates between ReLU and a piecewise-linear sinusoid without any the use of transcendental operation. Our results show that a bounded range improves image reconstruction PSNR on average by 0.06 dB, a single bump 3.29 dB, and centering that bump a further 3.64 dB. The oscillation count improves 6.30 dB more, raising ReLU from 24.0 to 37.3 dB on Kodak images, placing third over eleven activations, within 1.3 dB of the state-of-the-art, though beyond four oscillations the reconstruction collapses. Because an INR is fit on the grid on which it is evaluated, a unit whose pre-activations never reach the support of its activation is certifiably inactive over the entire domain; deleting such units is lossless without fine-tuning, removing up to 84% of parameters at zero loss in performance (dB) and decoding 3.2 times faster. Reference code and demos are available at https://github.com/jeovafarias/jasin.

## 263. LineEX: Data Extraction from Scientific Line Charts

- **作者**：VP Shivasankaran, Muhammad Yusuf Hassan, Mayank Singh
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In this paper, we introduce LINEEX that extracts data from scientific line charts. We adapt existing vision transformers and pose detection methods and showcase significant performance gains over existing SOTA baselines. We also propose a new loss function and present its effectiveness against existing loss functions. In addition, we synthetically created the largest line chart dataset comprising 430K images. The code is available at https://github.com/Shiva-sankaran/LineEX.

## 264. Anchoring VAE Fine-Tuning to Its Latent Diffusion Prior

- **作者**：Rafael Bischof, Dominik Narnhofer, Julius Erbach et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Pretrained latent diffusion models are effective priors for transfer learning, from geometry estimation to physics simulation. Existing approaches fine-tune the diffusion backbone but freeze the encoder of the VAE to preserve the latent distribution. However, a frozen encoder may miss salient details in non-RGB inputs and is limited to three channels. We introduce Diffusion Prior Anchoring (DPA), a regularizer applied during VAE fine-tuning that uses a score-distillation update to steer latents toward the high-density manifold on which the pretrained diffusion model denoises accurately. We validate DPA across three image and video models (FLUX 3, LTX-Video, Wan2.2) and four tasks: monocular depth estimation, MRI reconstruction, wind-flow simulation, and surface normal estimation, where DPA lowers the prediction error by 7.5% relative to the frozen VAE, 4.9% to decoder-only fine-tuning, and 4.0% to unanchored VAE fine-tuning. Its gain grows the further the target data lies from the RGB training distribution, from 3.3% on monocular depth estimation to 7.2% on complex-valued MRI. DPA also applies to modified VAEs, including pruned and multi-channel variants. With a four-channel VAE that encodes depth and surface normals, it lowers depth estimation error by 9.3%.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31651) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31651)

## 265. MSW: a Multi-Scale Sliced Wasserstein Distance Between Image Distributions

- **作者**：Youssef Haouchat, Stanislas Ducotterd, Bassam El Rawas et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.SP

We propose the multi-scale sliced Wasserstein (MSW) distance between image distributions, which operates in pixel space and requires no trained network. MSW convolves images with a fixed bank of discrete cosine filters at several scales, pools the responses over all positions, and compares the resulting one-dimensional distributions using the Wasserstein distance. We pair MSW with an exact two-sample test that gives calibrated p-values. Most metrics compare two image sets in the latent space of a pretrained network. We show that these metrics are insensitive to simple perturbations of the images, because the latent space is nearly invariant to them. In contrast, MSW detects smaller perturbations than latent-space metrics and needs far fewer images to do so. Although it uses no learned features, it still detects semantic changes of the image distribution. Finally, MSW matches or outperforms existing Wasserstein-based metrics in our experiments.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31647) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31647)

## 266. What You See Is What You Certify: Concept-Based Certificates for Unseen Compositions

- **作者**：Phuc Linh Ngo, Thang Nguyen Duc, Truong Thanh Hung Nguyen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：25 pages, 7 figures, 22 tables

Concept-based models justify a decision by concepts they claim to see, decoded through an explicit ontology. Yet concept predictions are known to leak label information, ignore locality, and be poorly grounded on new attribute combinations. A decision decoded from such concepts can satisfy the ontology and still be wrong, so we ask when it can serve as a certificate. On unseen combinations of familiar parts, a joint concept bottleneck model and a concept embedding model, trained as is standard, certify wrong classes 93-94% of the time despite a correct ontology (60% for a joint model trained only on visible parts and gated by a localizer). The joint model's part errors copy co-occurring values more often than chance (cross-concept shortcutting). Treating the ontology as an error-detecting code, we show that a certificate needs visible evidence excluding every rival, unseen parts reported as such, and rare misreads not driven by a shared shortcut. A minimal reference design, OCVE, reads each part from a local crop in its own forward pass. On unseen FunnyBirds classes, it certifies 52% of images at 0.4% risk, against 14% for mask-pooled features and 33% for full-image part readers (5.6% when trained and gated alike), at similar zero-shot accuracy. The best baselines, even calibrated or restricted to unseen classes, and a decoder counting only agreeing parts remain about 3-8 times riskier. On CUB, isolated region readers are more accurate than an attribute-prototype model (63.1% vs. 55.3%) and certify unseen species at about 6 points less risk than it and about 10 points less than features pooled in the same keypoint boxes. On real traffic signs, isolated readers lower certified risk from 99.8% to 67%, which is still far too high; most remaining errors are misread pictograms. On seen classes the ontology brings no gain over a fairly trained black box, and isolation costs accuracy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31627) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31627)

## 267. Adapt, Label, Distill: Foundation-Model Supervision for Efficient Object Detection

- **作者**：Zhengye Yang, Anthony Wang, Yuyang Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at the 7th Workshop on Real-World Surveillance: Applications and Challenges (RWS), WACV 2027. 12 pages, 6 figures, including supplementary material

Object detectors often transfer poorly to specialized domains, where box annotations are scarce and deployment requires compact, real-time models. Large vision foundation models can provide offline supervision, but their zero-shot predictions may be poorly aligned with the target domain. We present an adapt-label-distill pipeline that uses limited target annotations to specialize a foundation teacher and transfer its supervision to compact detectors. We adapt SAM3 on the labeled subset, generate pseudo-labels for the remaining unlabeled images, and train YOLOv11 students with confidence-guided supervision. Teacher confidence weights pseudo-label losses, while zero-gating retains uncertain hypotheses during assignment without applying their associated training losses. Experiments across six target domains and three student capacities show substantial gains in several domains, while revealing limited or negative transfer in others. Teacher comparisons under a common hard-label training recipe support the choice of SAM3, and comparisons with an EfficientTeacher adaptation provide additional evidence against an online SSOD baseline. With YOLOv11m, our pipeline improves over STAC-style self-training by up to 15.3 AP and reaches 51.6 AP on Garbage, compared with 54.1 under full supervision. On NVIDIA Jetson AGX Orin, the deployed student achieves 188 FPS versus 2.9 FPS for SAM3, benefiting from foundation-model supervision without incurring the teacher's deployment cost. Code is available at https://github.com/zk2172-columbia/ProjectAdaptLabelDistill_ObjectDetection.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31604) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31604)

## 268. RePT: 3D Geometry Emerges from 2D Relational Pretraining

- **作者**：Xize Duan, Binke Hou, Xingqiao Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：16 pages, 6 figures, 9 tables

Learning spatial representations often relies on explicitly predicting geometric quantities, but how physical geometry is organized within learned representations remains less understood. We ask whether 3D structure can emerge from learning relations across 2D observations, without 3D prediction targets. We introduce RePT, which learns compact relations between distinct physical points through cross-view 2D point prediction. The learned relation space develops a geometric organization in which magnitudes, angles, and neighborhoods reflect physical 3D displacement. A single linear readout fitted on 16 scenes recovers displacement length and angle on 39 held-out scenes without refitting (Pearson r = 0.72 and 0.78). The ARKitScenes-trained checkpoint also retains strong geometric organization on ScanNet++, showcasing generalizability. In controlled comparisons, distinct-point relational prediction consistently yields stronger geometric organization than matched same-point correspondence learning. This organization depends on additive relations and adaptive scene context, and higher point-transfer accuracy does not by itself produce stronger organization. More broadly, our results suggest that 3D geometry need not be an explicit prediction target and can instead emerge as a structural property of representations trained to relate 2D observations, pointing to relational prediction as a complementary route to learning the representations that spatial intelligence builds on.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31601) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31601)

## 269. GoldiBench: Right Tools, Wrong Amount -- Benchmarking Agents on Effect Judgement in Professional Editing

- **作者**：Wang Yang, Xinpeng Li, Yanyan Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.SE
- **备注**：20 pages. Project page: https://uservan.github.io/GoldiBench

Real editing requests describe a look, not the steps to reach it, so an agent that serves them must judge how much of each tool the look takes. Existing benchmarks do not measure this: their tasks either state the tools and values, or leave the amount open and are scored by VLM judges that pass more than half of half- and triple-strength results. We introduce GoldiBench, a benchmark of 500 editing tasks in five professional applications (darktable, RawTherapee, GIMP, Inkscape, Blender), solvable by writing a program or by operating the GUI. Each task pairs a START image with a TARGET rendered from a hidden program, so the amount is fixed by a known target; the agent's edit is re-rendered and compared with TARGET, and the same task is posed with the tools and values, the tools only, or the goal only, separating operation from effect judgement. Across 25 open, GUI and closed models, agents operate well (99-100% with programs from 4B up when told the values) but fall off a cliff when they must judge the amount: given only the tools, the best model passes 19% of the tasks with programs and 33% through the GUI, and scale does not close the gap. Manuals barely help; the measured TARGET-START difference more than doubles the pass rate, but the same difference shown as an image does not for open models. Allowed four renders, agents almost always stop after the first; forced to compare, the strong models double their pass rate. Agents know the tools, but neither read nor check the amount.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31590) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31590)

## 270. Spec$^2$R: Recovering Emergent Properties in Supervised Vision Transformers

- **作者**：Adam Wróbel, Jacek Tabor, Bartosz Zieliński et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision Transformers (ViTs) have achieved strong performance across computer vision tasks, but the quality of their learned representations differs drastically depending on the training paradigm. While self-supervised ViTs often demonstrate emergent properties, such as object-aligned patch representations and attention maps, supervised ViTs typically lack these properties, even when trained with strong data augmentations. Understanding the origin of this difference is important for explaining and controlling the object-level structure. To investigate it, we perform a spectral analysis of supervised and self-supervised models, and identify a key distinction: MLP layers in supervised ViTs introduce high-frequency variations into patch representations, disrupting their spatial organization. Motivated by this finding, we propose Spec$^2$R, a lightweight spectral regularization method that fine-tunes the MLP layers of pretrained supervised ViTs. Spec$^2$R recovers object-aligned patch representations and attention maps, while improving adversarial robustness and preserving competitive classification accuracy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31589) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31589)

## 271. Lit Up but Not Needed: Scoped Lesions Show Where Multimodal Mixture-of-Experts Depend on Their Experts

- **作者**：Zhixuan Ge, Anqi Li, Kexin Geng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 7 figures, 8 tables. Code: https://github.com/gezhixuan/moedep

Cognitive neuroscience learned that an activation map is not a map of necessity. Regions that light up during a task are not always needed for it, and only lesions reveal which ones are. Mixture-of-experts (MoE) vision-language models come with an activation map for free, since the router records which experts respond to image and text tokens, and recent work reads modality-specialized experts off this map. We bring the lesion. In Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B, Kimi-VL-A3B and Gemma-4-26B-A4B, 3 to 12% of the experts are vision-exclusive: image tokens route to them at up to five times the uniform rate and text tokens almost never. In Qwen3-VL they receive a quarter of all image-token routing, and lesioning all of them costs less than one percentage point on average over eight perception benchmarks and free-form OCR, no more than lesioning a random set of the same size. In Kimi-VL and Gemma-4 the lesion also costs no more than a random set. Scoped lesions, which remove an expert set only at image positions or only at text positions, then show where the models do depend on their experts. In Qwen3-VL, silencing every expert at every image position costs 3 points, against 45 at text positions, where the dependence sits in dual-use experts that text-only tasks need as well. Across models the image-side dependence is 2 to 3 points in Qwen3-VL and Kimi-VL and 26 in Gemma-4. Reading text in images is the one ability that needs image-position experts in every model. The exclusive experts receive informative patches, produce outputs of ordinary size, and stay inert under sensory deprivation: text-only training recruits them only when load balancing forces it. In Kimi-VL the balancing bias itself manufactures most of them. Load balancing draws the modality map in the part of the computation the models depend on least. Expert selection, pruning and skipping should follow the lesion, not the map.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31576) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31576)

## 272. Denoising Diffusion with Progressive Posterior Sampling

- **作者**：Zeyu Luo, Shuai Huang
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV

Diffusion models provide effective generative priors for solving noisy linear inverse problems. However, existing posterior sampling methods may require singular value decomposition (SVD) of the measurement operator or prior knowledge of the measurement noise variance, while guidance based on local gradients provides only a limited view of posterior geometry. We propose progressive posterior sampling (PPS) with manifold projection, which automatically estimates the measurement noise variance and avoids SVD. During reverse diffusion, PPS constructs a Gaussian approximation to the posterior distribution in closed form and guides a sample drawn from the local prior toward the approximate posterior mean. We then project the guided sample onto a spherical level set that approximates the local noisy image manifold, promoting consistency with the local geometry of the diffusion prior. Experiments on FFHQ and ImageNet across four restoration tasks and three measurement noise levels demonstrate competitive reconstruction accuracy and perceptual quality by PPS.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31558) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31558)

## 273. Tempering and few-shot calibration of pathology foundation models improve digital biomarker reproducibility across slide scanners

- **作者**：Frank T Wen, Kyler Rosen, Siddhi Ramesh et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：q-bio.QM, cs.AI, cs.CV

Pathology foundation models underpin a growing number of histology-based cancer biomarkers, but sensitivity to slide scanner-specific image signatures degrades cross-center reliability and reproducibility. We developed a foundation model fine-tuning method that improved consistency in head-and-neck cancer human papillomavirus (HPV) prediction, colorectal cancer microsatellite instability (MSI) identification, prostate cancer grading and breast cancer phenotyping. This tempering method decreased cross-scanner prediction differences by 67.5% for HPV (95% CI 59.0-73.9%), 69.3% for MSI (95% CI 51.9-76.5%) and 63.4%-81.2% for breast signatures. Cross-scanner prostate grade agreement rose from 42.5% to 92.7%. Under full scanner-HPV confounding during training, AUROC fell from 0.80 to 0.68 with original model features but only from 0.86 to 0.84 with tempering. Unlike post hoc feature calibration, tempering used only public data yet matched or exceeded on-site calibration. These findings support tempering to reduce scanner dependence of pathology foundation models.

## 274. Hi3R-VLA: Grounding Semantics and Actions in 3D

- **作者**：Shizhe Chen, Paul Pacaud, Cordelia Schmid
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

While recent vision-language-action (VLA) models increasingly incorporate 3D geometry, how to effectively exploit it for precise and generalizable action prediction remains a central challenge. We introduce Hi3R-VLA, a 3D policy that grounds heterogeneous modalities - vision, language, and action - in explicit 3D geometry for robotic manipulation. Our model integrates spatially-aligned visual features and language conditioning into point cloud representations, enriching scene geometry with fine-grained, task-relevant semantics. It then anchors low-level control commands in the same 3D coordinate frame and uses 3D-RoPE enhanced action-to-point attention over coarse-to-fine point features to model geometric relationships between action targets and observed points. Our analysis demonstrates the importance of semantic fusion within 3D and spatial alignment between observations and actions. With fewer than 250M trainable parameters, Hi3R-VLA achieves state-of-the-art performance on RoboCasa, RLBench, RoboTwin2, DexJoCo and DexSuite across diverse manipulation tasks and embodiments, from single-arm and dual-arm grippers to dexterous hands. Real-robot evaluations further demonstrate its effectiveness in the physical world. The model, code and dataset are publicly released at https://cshizhe.github.io/projects/hi3r_vla.html.

## 275. Fast Anatomy-Aware Diffeomorphic Augmentation for Brain MRI

- **作者**：Tonmoy Hossain, Alexandra G. Roberts, Mark Olchanyi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Data augmentation is essential for training deep learning models that are generalizable, particularly when training data are limited. In brain MRI, augmentation methods that synthesize anatomically diverse images without relying on large cohorts are particularly attractive, especially when training supervised models for scans with a specific resolution or MRI contrast. While conventional random nonlinear fields offer a simple means of achieving such diversity, they fail to capture the underlying spatial dependencies among morphological changes across brain regions. To address this limitation, we introduce BrainAug, an anatomy-aware augmentation framework that generates diverse, anatomically coordinated deformations without relying on population imaging data. BrainAug constructs an anatomical graph encoding spatial proximity and bilateral correspondence between brain regions, then incorporates these relationships into a probabilistic model designed to sample correlated regional volume changes. These sampled variations are translated into diffeomorphic transformations, enabling substantial structural changes while preserving anatomical topology. Combined with contrast-agnostic image synthesis, BrainAug generates training images with independently varying anatomy and appearance. Evaluations across eight brain MRI datasets demonstrate stronger segmentation generalization across diverse populations, while registration experiments show improved alignment under substantial morphological differences.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31524) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31524)

## 276. Your Codec, Your Generator: Idempotent Generative Compression without Retraining

- **作者**：Raz Haim, Assaf Shocher
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We introduce an image compression framework with three properties: (i) adaptation of pretrained codecs without retraining or fine-tuning, (ii) cycle-consistent recompression, even of already-encoded images, and (iii) decoding with independently chosen generative priors. We wrap the frozen encoder with non-learned operations so that every continuous latent has a preimage, yielding a surjective pseudo-invertible neural network. A nonlinear right inverse built from the pretrained decoder satisfies $E'(D'(E'(x)))=E'(x)$. To extend consistency to quantized bitstreams, the receiver searches for a continuous latent that reproduces both the transmitted main symbols and entropy side information. A successful search and an exact correction preserve both streams under deterministic coding; the final reconstruction is checked through the actual coding path. The encoder's pseudo-inverse enables a nonlinear counterpart of back-projection. In the linear case, back-projection has the closed-form solution $x'=x+A^\dagger(y-Ax)$, giving the closest reconstruction to $x$ satisfying $Ax'=y$. Our nonlinear formulation enforces the transmitted code on proposals from an independently chosen generative model. Adjusting the starting noise controls the prior's influence without changing that code. Experiments show small first-pass quality changes, higher retained quality than trained consistency codecs over 50 recompressions, also under 8-bit storage, and decoder-side control of the perception-distortion trade-off. Decoupling the codec from its prior lets the receiver adopt future generators without retraining or re-encoding.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31520) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31520)

## 277. InitFree-SfM: When Initialization-Free Bundle Adjustment meets Learned Structure-from-Motion

- **作者**：Simon Weber, Mateo de Mayo, Je Hyeong Hong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Initialization-free bundle adjustment (InitFree BA) aims to jointly recover camera poses and scene structure without requiring an initial global estimate. It is a very challenging problem, especially compared to traditional BA where a good estimation is assumed. While recent methods optimize surrogate Object Space Error (OSE) objectives from random initialization, a low objective value does not necessarily yield an accurate reconstruction, revealing a gap between optimization and reconstruction. We show that existing InitFree BA pipelines struggle to produce accurate 3D reconstructions on modern SfM benchmarks, and demonstrate that incorporating learned metric depth substantially improves their practical feasibility. We introduce InitFree-SfM, a complete initialization-free SfM pipeline combining learned correspondences, metric depth, depth-consistent track construction, and joint metric refinement. Within this framework, we propose a simple depth-augmented OSE (dOSE), which complements the directional constraints of OSE by constraining landmark positions along their viewing rays. Our approach substantially outperforms existing InitFree BA methods and achieves competitive accuracy with established SfM pipelines, including on challenging low-overlap image collections. Importantly, our analysis challenges the prevailing emphasis on surrogate objectives in previous InitFree BA research, revealing that geometric constraints play a central role in accurate reconstruction, while OSE-based optimization provides further improvements.

## 278. Synthetic Augmentation for Data-Scarce Object Detection: Allocation, Dose, and Filtering Across Six Datasets

- **作者**：João Félix, Atabak Dehban, Rodrigo Ventura
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 2 figures. Submitted to European Robotics Forum 2027

Robotic perception for capturing uncooperative orbital objects and inspecting variable industrial products must often operate with limited labelled imagery. We examine a modular synthetic-augmentation pipeline, evaluating generation planning and post-generation filtering as separate interventions, with computational cost as a design constraint. Across six object-detection datasets, FLUX.1-Fill with class-specific LoRA adaptation produced dataset-dependent dose effects; the validation-selected augmentation improved mAP@50:95 on four datasets and reduced it on two. Filtering did not yield consistent gains, and FID, KID, precision, recall, and LPIPS did not consistently track detector changes. The results do not establish that image quality alone identifies useful samples. They motivate standardised evaluation and inexpensive candidate-utility measures that could identify useful samples before detector retraining, reducing repeated training cost.

## 279. HyMTS: A Multiscale Tensor Factorization-Based Network for Hyperspectral Image Restoration

- **作者**：Lei Xu, Fahad Sohrab, Moncef Gabbouj et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Hyperspectral image (HSI) restoration has been explored using deep learning techniques recently. Because hyperspectral images have strong spectral and spatial redundancy, the underlying clean signal is usually represented in a low-dimensional spectral subspace and locally low-rank spatial structures. Existing methods primarily explore dense attention or feature mixing across spectral bands. In this work, we propose HyMTS, a hyperspectral restoration network with a backbone built on the Multi-scale Tensorial Summation (MTS) factorization operator. HyMTS provides a natural architectural mechanism for exploiting a low-rank prior, embedded directly in the network operator rather than imposed via an auxiliary loss or a constraint on the reconstructed output. Specifically, HyMTS stacks a unified block that couples factorized layers with input-adaptive layers, capturing both local and global dependencies across the spectral and spatial dimensions. Extensive experiments without auxiliary data on standard benchmarks for Gaussian and real-noise denoising and single-image super-resolution show that HyMTS efficiently achieves superior or comparable performance to various state-of-the-art approaches.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31506) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31506)

## 280. GDD: A Remote Sensing Image Deepfake Detection and Manipulation Localization Dataset

- **作者**：Jacob Arndt, Philipe Dias, Debvrat Varshney et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Verifying image authenticity is a critical need for a variety of applications and decision-making in the remote sensing (RS) domain, yet it is becoming more challenging as advancements in generative AI enable producing increasingly realistic synthetic overhead data. However, existing RS deepfake benchmarks prioritize sheer volume at the expense of visual realism and diversity, while also lacking rigorous out-of-distribution (OOD) testing protocols to evaluate real-world robustness. To address this, we introduce the Geospatial Deepfakes Dataset (GDD), a benchmark for RS deepfake detection and manipulation localization. Rather than relying on uncurated, redundant synthetic generation, GDD comprises images across diverse modern generative models and real image sources, alongside manipulations from diffusion inpainting, splicing, and copy-move operations. Crucially, GDD establishes an OOD evaluation protocol for deepfake detection comprising unseen real image sources and generative models. We benchmark five deepfake detectors and two localization models across intra- and cross-dataset regimes and reveal that detectors trained on high-volume, uncurated benchmarks overfit to dataset-specific artifacts, whereas training on GDD yields superior cross-dataset generalization despite its compact scale. This benchmarking also exposes critical failure modes in current state-of-the-art methods. Combined, GDD and its evaluation protocol establish a high-quality standard for developing and stress-testing detection and localization models in this safety-critical RS domain. The dataset is publicly available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31487) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31487)

## 281. Which Way Is Up? Rotated Photos Silently Hurt Multi-View 3D Foundation Models

- **作者**：Yukun Shi, Minglun Gong
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Multi-view 3D foundation models estimate camera poses and 3D points from a set of photos in one forward pass. Many cameras store a portrait shot sideways, with an orientation tag telling viewers how to turn it, and this tag can be ignored, lost or wrong. We measure how rotated photos affect five such models on three datasets. A few rotated photos lower camera pose accuracy for every model, and the sideways photos already in a standard benchmark do the same. Even the most robust model still loses accuracy. The failure is also silent: the models' confidence does not reliably flag it, and most official image loaders ignore the tag. The fixes we test each correct only part of the problem. These findings call for orientation checks in reconstruction pipelines and benchmarks.

## 282. PromptSMC: Enabling Particle Exploration in Flow Matching via LLM-Based Prompt Rejuvenation

- **作者**：Wenyi Lian, Zheng Zhao, Thomas B. Schön
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages

This paper is concerned with prompt-image alignment at inference time through exploration of generative trajectories and dynamical prompt rejuvenation. We leverage sequential Monte Carlo (SMC), to offer a gradient-free and principled framework for this purpose through reward-guided trajectory selection. More specifically, we introduce LLM-based prompt rejuvenation, allowing for efficient particle exploration in SMC samplers along the diffusion time in flow matching models. The proposed method, PromptSMC, retains the selected latent states and uses a pretrained LLM to dynamically revise the prompts of duplicated particles under semantic-preservation constraints. These revised prompts induce alternative conditional velocity fields, allowing particles to explore different continuations from a shared intermediate state. The method further uses a reward model to evaluate each particle's alignment with the original prompt, and the resulting scores guide particles through resampling. PromptSMC thus integrates prompt-based exploration with reward-guided sampling, achieving faithful prompt-image alignment without additional noise injection, model training, or reward gradients. Our experiments and ablation studies across multiple benchmarks and backbones demonstrate the effectiveness of the method for text-to-image flow matching. Code is available at https://github.com/shermanlian/PromptSMC.

## 283. AdProducer: Towards Agentic Advertising Video Generation

- **作者**：Kunyang Li, Chaoyang Wang, Mubarak Shah et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Project page: https://lky-ang.github.io/AdProducer-page/ Code: https://github.com/lky-ang/AdProducer

Recent advances in video generation have made automated advertising production increasingly feasible. However, an effective advertisement must faithfully represent the product and communicate its selling points through compelling visual storytelling, which requires more than visual synthesis: it calls for deeper product understanding and strategic decisions about how to present product value. Crucially, product research and creative design are closely intertwined, as the information gathered about a product determines which features can be faithfully demonstrated and how they should be presented. In this paper, we introduce AdProducer, the first end-to-end agentic framework for advertising video creation, transforming concise creative briefs into finished advertisements. Our multimodal agent autonomously researches products through web and image search and translates its findings into selling-point-driven storyboards that guide a frozen video generator. To achieve this, we develop a dedicated data construction pipeline that yields two training datasets, AdProducer-SFT and AdProducer-RL, and train the agent through supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning with joint optimization of storyboard- and video-level rewards. We further introduce AdCreation-Bench, comprising 305 advertising briefs across eight domains, with two complementary metrics: PG-Score for product correctness and creative execution, and Ad-Score for overall advertising effectiveness assessed by an independent judge. Experiments demonstrate that AdProducer improves the PG-Score of MiniMax H3 from 17.3 to 51.3 and generalizes to Veo 3.1 without additional training, improving its score from 28.0 to 35.9, with consistent gains in Ad-Score, VideoScore2, and human evaluations. Code, models, and data are released.

## 284. A dataset of clinically similar image pairs from citation-linked case reports for medical image retrieval

- **作者**：Guy Shmueli, Keno K. Bressem, Tom Hope
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.IR

Retrieving images of patients clinically similar to a given patient can support diagnosis, teaching and research. Training and evaluating retrieval systems requires knowing which images of different patients show the same finding, and existing datasets are small or narrow in scope. We construct PMC-PaIR, a dataset of 86,144 pairs of clinically similar images from 56,013 open-access PubMed Central case reports, which often document rare or challenging presentations. We develop an approach that follows citations, which often link case reports of similar patients. To determine what two linked cases share clinically, it prompts large language models to generate and answer hierarchical questions, from modality to diagnosis and findings, and to match images by caption using the answers. Experts find the pairs highly accurate, and this question-guided approach finds twice as many correct pairs as direct matching. Fine-tuning BiomedCLIP on PMC-PaIR improves retrieval in all modality, anatomy and diagnosis categories tested, for image-only queries and image queries with a short text naming the finding. We release the dataset and the automated pipeline, which can extend PMC-PaIR as new case reports are published.

## 285. The Fingerprinting Paradox: Why Sensitivity Undermines Black-Box Model Integrity Verification

- **作者**：Shahinul Hoque, Farhin Farhad Riya, Jinyuan Sun et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CR, cs.CV

Black-box integrity verification aims to detect unauthorized modifications to outsourced models using only prediction APIs. Existing frameworks seek to improve verification efficiency through sensitive fingerprints: queries whose predictions are more likely to change under tampering than those of genuine inputs. We show that this sensitivity can also help dishonest providers recognize verification queries and selectively conceal tampering. We prove that excess sensitivity relative to genuine inputs lower-bounds statistical distinguishability. We evaluate five integrity verification frameworks across three model architectures, three datasets, and three adversarial knowledge settings. When the deployed framework is known, our fingerprint detectors achieve AUCs at least 0.989, and selective filtering makes all five frameworks require significantly larger estimated query budgets than genuine inputs, reversing their intended efficiency advantage. A query-efficiency advantage remains marginally in a small subset of evaluated settings with poor detection transfer. We further design verification methods under stronger concealment constraints but find that reduced detectability comes with losses in sensitivity or fingerprint-generation success. Our results expose a sensitivity-detectability tension and show that sensitivity alone does not ensure efficient black-box verification against adaptive malicious providers.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31455) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31455)

## 286. Ground, Guide, Act: Improving VLA Spatial Generalization with Multimodal Foundation Models

- **作者**：Guangting Zheng, Shaoxuan Wang, Yu Yuan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models effectively learn action policies from robot demonstrations, but struggle to generalize to changes in object positions and task goals. General multimodal foundation models (MFMs) offer complementary scene understanding and spatial reasoning, but remain slow and unreliable for direct fine-grained control. To understand how MFMs can improve VLA spatial generalization, we conduct a systematic capability analysis across multiple VLA architectures using atomic-stage interventions, revealing that many failures stem from poor target grounding and global spatial routing, while local manipulation skills remain largely usable. Based on these findings, we propose MFM-VLA, a selective collaboration framework in which an MFM provides target grounding and spatial guidance, controllers execute free-space motion, and the VLA retains control over fine-grained manipulation. We fine-tune the VLA with action-chunk-level dense geometric captions and perturbed-state recovery data to improve local guidance following and recovery from unfamiliar handoff states. A development-calibrated capability router selects between direct VLA execution and MFM-assisted execution to avoid unnecessary intervention. Across LIBERO-PRO, ALOHA-Sim-PRO, RoboCasa365, and real-world chemistry tasks, MFM-VLA improves success rates by 14.9, 74.0, 3.6, and 30.9 percentage points, respectively, over the corresponding base VLA. The project page is available at https://tom-zgt.github.io/MFM-VLA

## 287. A multimodal foundation model for tissue proteomics and morphology in cancer

- **作者**：Benedikt von Querfurth, Lukas Klein, Cédric Vincent-Cuaz et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：q-bio.QM, cs.CV

Cancer diagnosis, prognosis, and treatment decisions increasingly rely on integrating tissue morphology with specific molecular readouts of the tumor and its microenvironment. H&E histopathology provides scalable morphology, whereas multiplex spatial proteomics quantifies dozens of proteins in situ with spatial context, but remains selectively deployed, often with different marker panels across cohorts. This creates a translational bottleneck: most patients lack high-depth molecular profiling, and existing models rarely integrate morphology and spatial molecular data in a single framework. We present VirTues-M2, a multimodal foundation model that jointly learns from H&E and spatial proteomics using a novel vision transformer-based architecture. By encoding marker identity during pretraining, VirTues-M2 is robust to missing modalities, imperfect modality pairing or registration, and variable marker panels, enabling unified inference for tissue phenotyping, panoptic cell segmentation, virtual staining, annotation-free biomarker discovery, and reinforcement learning-based adaptive marker panel selection to reduce assay burden. As a concrete demonstration of translational utility, in a newly collected cohort of 1,053 treatment-naive non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC) patients with paired spatial proteomics and H&E, VirTues-M2 discovers high-risk and low-risk tissue signatures that stratify overall survival in both the discovery cohort and an independent validation cohort, outperforming current state-of-the-art stratification schemes; all enabled by VirTues-M2's ability to jointly analyze both modalities within a single model.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31451) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31451)

## 288. Which Ellipses Are Outlines of a Given Ellipsoid? A Ratio Test with Unknown Focal Length

- **作者**：Michael Werman
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, math.MG
- **备注**：36 pages, 6 figures, 4 tables; supplementary material included as an ancillary file. Submitted to SIAM Journal on Imaging Sciences. Code: https://github.com/michael-werman/realizability-code

When is an image ellipse the outline of a given ellipsoid with semi-axes $a <= b <= c? For a camera with square pixels, zero skew and known principal point, we show that it is an outline at focal length $f$ exactly when a cone ratio kappa(f), computed from two eigenvalues, is at most a/c; the middle semi-axis b plays no role. With f unknown, the admissible focal lengths form an interval; whether it is empty is decided by the smallest cone ratio over $f$, which, when attained, is obtained from an explicit quartic. The interval is bounded above only when the principal point is closer to the long axis of the ellipse than to its short axis. Thus one ellipse can rule out ellipsoids that are too round, never ones that are too elongated, and none if the principal point is unknown. Simulations and 45 Cassini images of Saturn's moons support the test.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31437) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31437)

## 289. Tornado4D: Physics-Guided 4D Gaussian Reconstruction of a Laboratory Tornado from Sparse One-Sided Videos

- **作者**：Yucheng Zhu, Maria J. Molina, Christopher Metzler
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：9 pages, 8 figures, 1 table

Characterizing how tornadoes evolve over space and time is critically important for understanding and preparing for these highly destructive weather events. Towards this end, we develop algorithms capable of forming time-resolved 3D reconstructions of laboratory smoke tornadoes from sparse one-sided videos. Even in a controlled environment, reconstruction is severely underconstrained: appearance is view dependent, motion is non-rigid, and the unobserved volume admits many geometries. We present Tornado4D, a physics-guided 4D Gaussian framework capable of reconstructing laboratory tornadoes from sparse, one-sided videos. Tornado4D uses knowledge of the enclosure geometry to introduce rotational airflow field priors that constrain how the 3D structure evolves over time. These rotational priors allow us to reconstruct otherwise unobserved structure. We evaluate reconstruction accuracy by computing novel-view synthesis accuracy on a held out camera view: Tornado4D outperforms frame-by-frame 3DGS and video-based 4DGS by a considerable margin.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31435) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31435)

## 290. Enhancing Sparse-View Gaussian Splatting with Feed-Forward Priors via Spectral Fusion and Distillation

- **作者**：Weikai Lin, Jun Dai, Sheng Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages. Project page: https://linwk20.github.io/spectral-fusion-gs/

Gaussian splatting from sparse training views renders high-quality images near training views, but reconstruction quality degrades at distant regions, which are undersampled by the training set. Feed-forward novel view synthesis (NVS) models use strong learned priors to resample the scene from substantially different viewpoints, but they take more than ten times as long to render and produce high-frequency artifacts on out-of-domain scenes. We propose a plug-and-play method that enhances an existing sparse-view Gaussian model with a pretrained feed-forward NVS model. We observe that the two are complementary in the frequency domain: the prior is more accurate at low frequencies, and the Gaussians at high frequencies. The reliability of the prior relative to the Gaussians is inversely proportional to the image frequency, so the optimal fusion weight is a first-order low-pass filter with one parameter that can be fitted. Our key idea is then to intelligently fuse the reliable part of each of the two methods. To remove the slow feed-forward model at render time, we use these fused images as teachers and distill them back into Gaussians, balanced with supervision from input views. Our method improves the quality of various sparse-view Gaussian methods without sacrificing rendering speed, and our analysis works on different prior models. Project page: https://linwk20.github.io/spectral-fusion-gs/

## 291. CoRe: Jointly Learning Contextual Representations for Understanding and Generation

- **作者**：Hangke Sui, Chang Li, Yuyan Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.LG

We introduce CoRe, in which understanding and generation jointly shape one contextual representation for images and one for text, trained from scratch. Each representation is read for understanding, conditions the other modality, and is the space in which that modality is generated by a continuous flow from Gaussian noise. With 383M parameters trained on CC12M and no pretrained language model, text encoder, or VAE at inference, CoRe matches a same-data contrastive-only baseline in global understanding and reaches COCO-30K FID 10.10, comparable to much larger generators built on pretrained components. In controlled comparisons at equal data and architecture, CoRe generates better in both directions (text to image and image to text) than a representation shaped by understanding alone and then frozen, and follows prompts better than generating in a space that understanding does not shape. In the other direction, with an attentive segmentation probe, on monocular depth, and when read by a frozen language model, CoRe surpasses the contrastive-only baseline: generation adds information that stronger readouts reach. Because generation runs inside the understanding representation, frozen real-image probes read and steer intermediate states and the model ranks its own samples without extra training. Because both modalities use continuous flows, both also distill to a few steps. Our controls indicate why one representation suffices: understanding organizes the representation semantically, while generation makes it retain more information.

## 292. Look Where Camouflage Breaks: Concealment-Guided Observation for Camouflaged Object Detection

- **作者**：Dingming Zhang, Fengyang Xiao, Jingjia Feng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, including supplementary material

A camouflaged object can blend into its surroundings at one scale and become distinguishable at another. Yet a region's apparent ambiguity gives limited guidance on which observation would resolve it. The challenge is to connect the form and scale of concealment to the evidence that a new view can reveal. We introduce CamoBreak, an active framework for camouflaged object detection guided by a Concealment Spectrum. From the image and current predictions, the spectrum measures regional resemblance and boundary ambiguity across comparison scales, distinguishing persistent concealment from separation emerging at finer or broader scales. These measurements guide crop selection from the already-captured image and recognition against global foreground and background references. An object-conditioned spatial preference then selects where local evidence should update the prediction and feature memory, retaining target corrections while suppressing background distractions. Accepted corrections reshape the next measurement, while predicted segmentation benefit determines whether to observe again. Experiments across encoder families and related segmentation tasks show consistent improvements. Targeted analyses support improved small-target recovery, multiple-object coverage, and selective evidence integration. Code will be released.

## 293. Nemotron-Labs-VisualCreation: Pixel as Code for Agentic Visual Creation

- **作者**：Guo Chen, Fuxiao Liu, Haoqin Tu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Guo Chen, Fuxiao Liu, Haoqin Tu, and Yiyang Zhou contributed equally and are listed alphabetically by surname. Project lead: Guilin Liu

Visual creation demands expressive imagery together with precise control over text, layout, and structure. We introduce Nemotron-Labs-VisualCreation, an agentic framework that connects these capabilities through Pixel as Code. Raster images are represented by learned discrete visual tokens, while structured artifacts use explicit programs. A shared generation-and-rendering interface connects these representations to visual feedback across images, vector graphics, webpages, and documents. A Nemotron-native harness coordinates planning and raster candidate selection, with targeted repair for editable structured and hybrid artifacts. To support this interface, we develop Nemotron-Labs-Image-Tokenizer, the autoregressive raster generator Nemotron-Labs-Image-30B-A3B, and the structured-code model family Nemotron-Labs-VisionCode. On approximately 178K images spanning six visual domains, our tokenizer improves reconstruction PSNR from 26.08 to 29.57 dB over Emu3.5 IBQ. It also provides 7.13x faster encoding and 10.93x faster decoding at batch size one on A100. In standalone evaluation, one Nemotron-Labs-VisionCode variant surpasses Kimi K3.1 Pro on the aggregate across Chart2Code, MMSVG, Design2Code, and Image2Struct. At the system level, the Nemotron-native configuration raises GenEval2 Soft-TIFA GM from 31.71 to 52.56 using the same raster generator. With our best setting, Nemotron-Labs-VisualCreation achieves performance competitive with GPT Image 2 and Nano Banana Pro across most benchmarks with available comparisons.

## 294. Beyond Static Charts: Benchmarking Interactive Chart-to-Code Generation for MLLMs

- **作者**：Yuhan Bian, Haoyu Liu, Siyu Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：ACM WSDM 2027 (Main Track), Our code and data are available at https://github.com/bianyh/ChartInt

In recent years, multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved significant progress in chart-to-code generation. However, existing benchmarks mainly focus on static charts, overlooking interactive charts that enable richer and more expressive data exploration. Unlike static chart-to-code generation, which emphasizes visual consistency, interactive chart-to-code evaluation targets behavioral consistency, requiring chart states to evolve correctly under sequences of user interactions, thereby rendering traditional one-step evaluation paradigms ineffective. To address this gap, we introduce ChartInt, the first benchmark specifically designed for interactive chart-to-code generation. ChartInt consists of 2,779 carefully annotated examples covering common interaction patterns such as hover, highlight, and tooltips. Additionally, ChartInt incorporates editing tasks such as data updates, style adjustments, and cross-chart style transfer to expand real-world application scenarios. To enable reliable evaluation of interaction behavior, we propose a preset state snapshot protocol that predefines target interaction states and deterministically triggers interaction events, transforming dynamic behaviors into reproducible evaluation targets. Based on this protocol, we design a multi-dimensional evaluation framework measuring semantic and stylistic consistency. Experiments show that while current MLLMs perform well on static charts, their performance drops significantly on interactive tasks, highlighting the need for interaction-aware benchmarks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31345) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31345)

## 295. Exact Volume-Preserving Diffeomorphic Shear Splitting: Continuous-Discrete Geometric Bounds, Reversible Memory Backpropagation, and Expressivity Frontiers in Volumetric Mapping

- **作者**：Alvaro Garcia-Casas
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：17 pages, 4 figures, 4 tables. Reversible PyTorch autograd engine with exact volume-preserving diffeomorphism guarantees

Incompressible diffeomorphic registration requires continuous spatial mappings that preserve local tissue volume (det(DΦ) = 1) without topological folding (det(DΦ) > 0). Standard velocity-field methods (SVF, LDDMM) approximate incompressibility via numerical ODE integration or soft penalties, exhibiting numerical volume drift (~10^-4 to 10^-1), iterative grid inversions, and linear backpropagation memory O(L * N_vox). We formulate an exact structure-preserving framework for 3D registration based on cascaded coordinate shears. By decomposing the divergence-free Lie algebra sdiff(Ω) into six coordinate-shear generators whose convective non-linearities vanish identically, each elementary stage integrates analytically to a closed-form triangular shear with det(DS_k) = 1 and exact machine-precision inverse requiring zero iterations and zero spatial interpolations. Multi-stage compositions generate the connected volume-preserving group SDiff_0(Ω). We establish: (i) an unconditional global diffeomorphism proof closing the Hadamard-Caccioppoli injectivity gap; (ii) a Dirichlet boundary confinement lemma; (iii) an anisotropic Riemannian similarity invariance; (iv) a Sobolev H^2 spectral budget preventing chaotic mixing; and (v) an exact reversible backpropagation engine eliminating O(L) trajectory storage (0.00 MB intermediate activations). We prove second-order O(h^2) sub-voxel convergence across 16^3 to 64^3 lattices and second-order trajectory accuracy for palindromic Strang splitting (468.1x error reduction vs. Lie-Trotter; drift |det(DΦ)-1| <= 2.22e-16). Execution is 2.1x-4.0x faster than SVF with 78.4% GPU memory reduction. On cardiac cine-MRI (ACDC, N=100) and brain MRI (OASIS-1, N=414), our model achieves competitive Dice (0.884 and 0.841) while strictly maintaining 0.0000% folding and zero volume drift.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31343) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31343)

## 296. RenGen: Generating Images with as Many Tokens as the Scene Needs

- **作者**：Savya Khosla, Jay Mahajan, Akin Tuna Sakalli et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We present RenGen, a latent diffusion model that generates simple scenes from a few tokens and busy scenes from many, saving time and compute on simple scenes without losing realism. Most latent generators represent every image as a fixed grid of patch tokens, so a simple image costs as much to generate as a complex one. Prior methods that vary the token count through content-adaptive tokenization lose image quality as the count shrinks. RenGen instead trades scene complexity, rather than image quality, for fewer tokens. It does so by generating in the latent space of a pretrained region encoder, where each token represents one region of the image, and a decoder renders these region tokens back into pixels. Unlike patch tokens, region tokens cover areas of varying shape and position, so each token must capture both what its region contains and where it lies on the image canvas. We encode this spatial extent by adding positional embeddings to the encoder features before pooling, and we generate it by anchoring each token to a single randomly sampled point, from which the generator infers the region's shape, size, and content. At sampling time, the number of anchor points sets the trade-off between scene complexity and compute. On ImageNet $256{\times}256$, RenGen reaches a gFID of 1.34 with 88 tokens per image on average, the best among methods that vary their token count. It approaches the quality of RAEv2, a state-of-the-art patch-based model, at $2.5{\times}$ lower latency, advancing the Pareto frontier of generation quality versus compute. RenGen's gFID stays flat across scene complexity while latency varies by $2.4{\times}$ between simplest and busiest scenes, and its autoencoder reconstructs nearly as well as RAEv2 (rFID 0.32 vs. 0.30) with $2.9{\times}$ fewer tokens.

## 297. MOZAIC: An Audio-Video Deepfake Benchmark for Telling What Is Fake

- **作者**：Shaina Raza, Mohamed Hafez, Ahmed Y. Radwan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.SD

Audio-video (AV) deepfakes can fake the face, the voice, or both. Most benchmarks only ask whether a clip is real or fake, so they cannot show whether a detector relies on the picture, the voice, or encoding traces. We present MOZAIC, a paired benchmark with a 4-way label in which every source clip is released in all four combinations of real or fake video and real or fake audio so each modality contribution can be measured directly. Fakes cover inner-face swapping, whole-frame video diffusion and voice conversion. MOZAIC has 137,745 clips from 15,014 source pairs and four tasks: T1 AV authenticity, T2 manipulation type, T3 identity verification and T4 explanation. We evaluate 12 AV multimodal LLMs (MLLMs), train four detectors and fine-tune MLLMs with LoRA. Overall, the best MLLM reaches only 35.6% on the four-way T1 and catches far more video-only fakes than voice-only fakes (43.6% vs 7.5%). Trained detectors reach 98.4% on T1, and LoRA fine-tuning raises explanation scores (OmniVinci 4.17 to 7.40, Qwen2.5-Omni 4.42 to 6.98), but the best T1 detector falls to 78.3% when the face-swap generator is held out of training, so generalization and explanation remain open. We release the dataset, the compact MOZAIC-SMALL split, the code and the prompts. https://vectorinstitute.github.io/mozaic/

## 298. PEACE-TEST-PROTOCOL: Polarization-Keyed Examination Papers and Mandatory Issued Eyewear that Displace AI Glasses and Extend Universal Design

- **作者**：Dohyeon Kwon
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CR
- **备注**：18 pages, 4 figures. Framework, optical analysis, simulation study (Mueller-calculus page model, capture/OCR leakage experiment, metamerism test with 151 observers and 28 cameras), and evaluation protocol; no physical or human-subject results. Companion to arXiv:2611.26033 (SEOYUN). Simulation code in anc/

Camera-equipped AI glasses turn an examination page into a query for a remote vision-language model. Device detection targets possession, and our companion work SEOYUN targets the model. PEACE-TEST-PROTOCOL instead changes who owns the eyewear. Every candidate wears authority-issued glasses for the whole session, and the question paper is printed so that it can be read only through them. As in cinema stereoscopy, a patterned laminate encodes the text in the handedness of circular polarization rather than in brightness. The page looks uniformly grey to the naked eye and to unfiltered cameras, and the issued circular analyzer reveals the text. Compulsory issued eyewear turns the smart-glasses form factor against itself. The face position is occupied by a device the authority supplies and collects, and a camera anywhere else needs its own analyzer. Spectacle wearers receive prescription inserts in the issued frame, low-vision candidates may use an offline assistive mixed-reality tier, and blind candidates keep braille, audio, and screen-reader formats. We derive the luminance and contrast budget and show why circular keying tolerates head tilt. A simulation study supports the analysis. A spectral Mueller model confirms tilt invariance and invisibility to linear-polarization cameras, but a 1 degree axis misregistration leaks a few percent of contrast. A capture-and-OCR experiment on English and Korean text shows that video averaging can recover leakage too weak to read in a single frame, so leakage must be bounded against the adversary's total photon budget. Data from 151 observers and 28 cameras show that spectral (metameric) keys are visible to all of them. The key carries no secrecy, and filtered covert cameras remain possible. All results are simulations; no prototypes, physical measurements, or human-subject results are reported.

## 299. Hidden Clues in WM-811K: Uncovering the Spatial Signals Behind Wafer Map Prediction

- **作者**：Kabir Tolani
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：19 pages, 8 figures

WM-811K (also known as LSWMD) is a wafer map dataset first made publicly available in a 2015 paper by Wu et al. Convolutional neural networks have been used to achieve high classification accuracy on this dataset, yet it's not easy to decipher which features of the dataset are driving that performance. This study attempts to answer this specifically for WM-811K: how much of the predictive performance results from the wafer layout, the count of failed dies, their distance from the center and their exact 2-D location. After analyzing duplicate wafer maps, removing same wafer maps with conflicting labels and ensuring that the same manufacturing lot or exact same map doesn't appear in training, validation and test data, we were left with 25,513 defect-labeled wafer maps from eight classes. We then constructed four variations of each wafer map. The Original was the actual wafer map present in the dataset. The Layout-only kept only the layout of the non-background dies. The Global-shuffle kept the count of failed dies while shuffling them around. The Radial-shuffle randomized the locations of failed dies only in their radial bin, which was chosen out of 8 radial bins based on distance from the center. The data split, preprocessing and CNN architecture remained the same for all four of them. Macro-F1 progressed like this: 0.3829 (Layout-only), 0.5803 (Global-shuffle), 0.7439 (Radial-shuffle) and 0.8563 (Original). These results show that WM-811K contains useful information at several levels: wafer layout, number of failures, distribution of failed dies in a given radius, and exact 2-D locations of the failed dies. Therefore, high classification performance on this benchmark does not come only from learning the exact defect configuration.
